| `02e_land.js` | [land] The mountains' shader side: the land map `uLandMap` (2 m texels: r bare rock round the crags, g scree, b gullies, a heather) and `GLSL_LAND` (`landMapAt`, `landRockTone`, `landStrata`, `landAlong`: a coordinate along a face that never swings with its normal (world x or z, switched on a ragged fault line); `landFacets`: the faces broken as bedded rock breaks, not as a wall is built (version 10's first blocks read as coursed masonry from the orchard): beds about 2.9 m on average, thick and thin, their lines wavy, each with its own tone, the harder ones with a rounded lip that catches the light and holds the snow and a shaded undercut; bed lines open only along stretches; joints 6 to 18 m apart, wandering and only some open, within about 70 m; the beds within about 230 m; smooth where a bed would be only a few pixels; cracks at least a pixel wide or none (no dashed lines); `landStreaks`, `landSnowLine`: Thule's peaks white all year, the south's only in winter; `landSnowHold`: how much of it holds on a face that steep) |
| `03c_land.js` | [land] The mountains. `mtnShape` (from `buildWorld`, once the walk mask exists): the mountain ground with nothing walkable within two texels and no hollow near it is reshaped (teeth and notches along the crests, gullies down the fall line, ledges along the bedding planes as a monotone remap of height), then any hollow it made is filled and any change to a spill height elsewhere taken back, so every height, slope and normal a body or a later placement reads is exactly as it was. `mtnPlaceCrags` ([heights] from `mtnHeights`, once the whole mountain is shaped: 03d_heights.js): crags on the final ground, clear of every walkable texel's square by 1 m, of the water and the trees (kinds: 0 cliff band, a lens along the contour, 1 buttress leaning into the slope, 2 pinnacle on a crest or a sharp ridge, 3 tor, 4 boulder in the scree below a crag; shrunk to fit narrow faces), the bare rock and scree round them, `MTN.top` (the highest rock over each texel), the land map. `buildCrags`: one ring-and-layer mesh shaped per instance in `CRAG_VS` (uneven strata, stepped ledges, lobes, joints, lean, a top that follows the slope, a broken edge), a near model (12 x 7) and a far one (9 x 4) dithered across each other and out by `MTN_D` per quality; the nearest cast shadows. `updateMountains` (from `tick`) refills the lists when the camera has moved 3 m. `mtnCamRoom` (asked by `cameraRoom`, 09_player.js) pulls the camera in before it would enter a crag; `mtnCoverAll` (from `buildCoverMask`) keeps grass out of them. Test hooks `__hm.mtn` |
| `03d_heights.js` | [heights] The mountains shaped from everywhere, the walkable slopes and the skyline too (version 11). `mtnHeights` is a boot step after the farming (13_main.js, "Raising the mountains"): everything (trees, rocks, camps, plants, herbs, workstations, boats) has been placed on version 10's ground by then, so every list and every saved index is as it was. `htsField` reads each part of the range (the ring, the ridges between the realms, the outer wall: `htsParts`) in its own frame, along its crest and across it (the across direction squeezed so patterns run down the fall line), and adds peaks and saddles along the crests (`htsPart`), ribs down the faces, knobs, notches and false summits whichever way a ridge runs (`htsKnobs`, isotropic ridged noise), roughness and a lift to the peaks (the south's stay under its summer snow line: `htsCap`); the ribs, knobs and roughness grow with the slope (to 2.6 times), since a change of height moves a cliff only by the height over the slope. `htsErode`: a few rounds of stream-power erosion (each texel drains to its steepest neighbour; the water gathered cuts by its square root and the fall, never below the next texel down and never more than 10 m; the hillsides creep and the steepest faces slump), so gullies branch down the fall line round the ends of the ring at the passes and the junctions as anywhere. `htsKeep`: the ground stays exactly as it was on and round the roads, sites, camps, rivers, water and the ponds' hollows, standing stones, every collider and the farm plots (ground rising well above a road, a site or a camp is not kept: the walls of the passes are shaped); trees, rocks, plants and herbs are not kept but keep their footing (the ground round each moves as one with it, `objs`) and settle onto it. `htsApply`: every texel anyone could reach (`htsReach`, flooded from the roads as the land's checks do) stays walkable (where a slope there would pass the limit the share is eased off over a few metres, never at one texel; a few texels by a filled hollow may end a little steeper and stay walkable), any hollow the shaping made is filled (`mtnFlood`: no dry pond bed), and round ground left as it was that would become a deeper hollow, or drain another way by a pond, the shaping is taken back; the map's corners beyond the outer wall are left as they were. Then: the walk mask (texels no one could reach that are too steep now are blocked, `HTS.walkLost`; nothing new opens), the rock, grass and flower masks from the new slopes, everything on the mountains settled (trees in their chunk instances, rocks in theirs, colliders' `baseY`, plants, herbs), the land map's gullies, `htsTrees` (hardy pines and birches on the gentler lower slopes, sparse, thinning and stunted with height, none above 46 m in the south or 30 m in Thule; added after every tree there was, each with its collider, near-tree grid entry and nature state, drawn in a chunk mesh that already draws pines or birches there, so no draw is added: 126 of them, 75 pines and 51 birches), the crags on the final ground (`mtnFreeAgain`, `mtnPlaceCrags`: about 1,300) and outcrops on the walkable lower slopes (`htsOutcrops`, 40 of them: tors and boulders, mostly on the spurs, in belts, clear of everything kept and placed and of the roads; each a crag with `c.outcrop` and a collider of its own, tag `rock`, marked `outcrop` and left out of mountains.py's checksum of the colliders, stamped into `MTN.top` for the camera; the walk mask is left as it was; `mtnCheck` leaves them out of its checks of crags on unwalkable ground), `buildCrags`, the terrain's vertices, bounds and textures (`htsRefresh`) and the cover map. About 2.8 s at boot here on a quiet machine, 6-7 s while other runs kept it busy (the field 0.2 s, the erosion 0.5, the slope limit and the fills 1.0, the crags 0.5, which version 10 spent in `buildWorld`: `stats().T`, `TT`). Test hooks `__hm.heights` |
| `04_props.js` | Building materials (`MAT` ids and the building shader), keeps, temples, doors, banners, relics, rocks, hero tree |
| `04b_town.js` | Towns: framed houses, roofs, chimneys, yards |
| `04c_trees.js` | Near-camera trees: bark, branches, leaf cards |
| `04d_barrow.js` | The Barrow: the surface mound and the dungeon grid and geometry; indoor mode (`INDOOR`, `setIndoor`, `travel`) |
| `04e_houses.js` | Working houses: two-layer walls with real openings, hinged doors and shutters, glazing, the house shader (patched from the building shader at anchors; it throws if one is missing), interactions, camera inside, fire (`igniteHouse`, `houseAt`, `fireLandsAt`), collapse to a ruin and rebuilding on a scaffold. [siege2] The house shader's `uHD` (cracks and a hole a stone knocked in the roof, 04j_batter.js); `GLSL_ROOFS`' `uRoofD` (rain through the hole) |
| `04f_rooms.js` | Furnished rooms: hearths and stoves with real fires, tables, benches, beds, shelves, barrels, rugs, lamps; furniture colliders; ruins and scaffolds |
| `04h_siege.js` | Siege engines: battering rams, mangonels and trebuchets built in code (their own charring shader patched from the building shader), built and crewed by warbands, pushed into range, worked (windlass, slings, counterweight), stones on real arcs that crack wall sections (`castleSiegeHit`) and batter gates; engines hacked apart or set alight (`siegeFireAt`); the commanders' choice of gate or breach, bands storming a breach, the garrison holding it (`ai.plug`) and sallying out of its gate (`ai.sally`); the player joining a crew or taking over an abandoned engine. Test hooks on `__hm.SIEGE` |
| `04i_siege2.js` | [siege2] The siege that uses the towers (version 13). The engines' mark: `siegeMarkScore(W, x, z)` (what is left of a whole section in curtains, a tower's 1.25 and its narrow way in weighed by `SIEGE2_TOWER_K` (1.3), and the distance of its face / 45 m) orders a castle's sections (`siegeMarks`) for `siegePlanFor` (from the band's leader, with a little chance in it) and `siegeRetarget` (from the engine): a cracked tower, or one much nearer than any curtain, is taken (`siegeTowerWayOk`: the field before its fallen face walkable and open). Storming a fallen tower: `siegeStormBand` (from `siegeBandAfter` while the band's breach is a tower) gives each member `e.ai.storm`, and `siegeStormStep` (from `thinkSoldier`) walks it along the way in (`siegeStormRoute`: `towerBreachRoute` with heights) point by point (`STORM_R`), waiting its turn on the field before the rubble in rows of three (`siegeStormWaitSpot`) until fewer than `STORM_IN` (2) are in the narrow part and the last is `STORM_GAP` (1.3 m) on (`siegeStormTurn`: the leader first, then whoever is nearest), stepping aside when it gets no nearer for 2.5 s and giving the way up after 16; it fights only foes on its own level close by (`siegeStormFight`: the garrison before the door, a guard in the store room) and leaves those up on the walls; through the door it holds the court by it until the band is in. The band's give-up (20 s without coming nearer) watches every member. Test hooks `__hm.siege2` |
| `04i_siege3.js` | [siege3] Nobody is stuck in the ruins (version 14). A band's member keeps its place behind the leader (thinkSoldier's formation) only where a body can stand: a place in rubble, a fallen drum or a wall is moved in toward the leader to the first free point (`bandSlot`, `bandSlotFree`: walkable, no collider, no masonry or rubble too tall to step onto); a member that gets no nearer its place for 2.2 s takes its own way (`bandUnstick`: along the leader's footsteps, `followTrail`, then a step aside). A storm through a fallen tower's courtyard door (04i_siege2.js) gathers inside the door while foes hold the court before it (`siegeStormHold`: up to `STORM_HOLD_T` (9) s, until `STORM_HOLD_N` (3) of the band are in the tower; `W.holding` lets a third into the narrow part meanwhile; the bows and staffs among them shoot out through the open door), then they go out together. And in siege2's turn (`siegeStormTurn`) the leader holds the rest back only while it waits on the field itself, and one let go that fights out on the field is not in the narrow part (either held the band on the field for good). Test hooks `__hm.siege3` |
| `04j_batter.js` | [siege2] Stones that strike a house break it (version 13). A siege stone that meets a house in its flight (`wallsStoneStep` gives its aim the house: `siegeImpact` asks `houseStoneHit`) or a meteor beside one (`houseBlowAt`) batters it by its might over the house's toughness (`BATTER_K`, `houseToughness`: a hall of stone most, a roundhouse least): its walls crack (the house shader's `houseBatter`, `uHD.w`), a blow from above knocks a hole in its roof where it struck (`houseRoofPoint`; `uHD.xyz`, the covering discarded round it, ragged, its broken ends dark), the rain falls in through the hole (`GLSL_ROOFS`' `uRoofD`, `houseRoofOver`, `houseHoled`) and wets the floor under it, and at `bat.k` 1 it falls to a ruin of rubble, not soot (`houseBreak`: `uHD.w` + 2; no smoulder, the debris in its own colours; whoever is inside is struck), built back as after a fire (`houseScaffoldStep` keeps a stone ruin's char low, `houseRestore` clears `H.bat`). The keeps' halls take it too. Arrows stuck in a door swing with it (`wallDoorRec` from `wallStick`, `wallDoorPose` from `drawWalls`: a house's leaf about its hinge by `smooth(open) * swing`, a tower's by its part's frame). Save part `world.x.batter`. Test hooks `__hm.batter` |
| `04j_rafters.js` | [siege3] The hole a stone knocked in a roof is cut in the houses' shadow pass as in their colour pass (`HOUSE_DEPTH_FS`: buildHouses's depth material), so the sun falls in through it; broken timbers lie across it (`houseRafterGeo`: on a pitched roof rafters 0.55 m apart down the slope under the covering, one snapped in two, a snapped purlin; on a round house rafters from the eaves to the point; charred ends), a small mesh a holed house built again when the hole grows (`houseRafterSync`, once a second from `updateBlaze`). Arrows stuck in the leaf of a door or a gate the player built swing with it (`buildDoorRec`, asked by `wallDoorRec`: kept in the leaf's pivot frame, gone when the piece burns or is pulled down). Test hooks `__hm.rafters` |
| `04j_rubble.js` | [look4] (version 15) A house brought down by stones lies in rubble (version 13's kept its walls whole to the eaves: a roofless shell, as a burnt one is). Its walls are broken off at a ragged line (`look4Stub`, 04d_look4.js: in the house shader and its shadow pass, 04e_houses.js and 04j_rafters.js), and here: the broken tops closed with a course of the wall's core and rough lumps on it, the steps' ends filled (`houseRubbleRuns`: each wall's run of half-metre segments, a box house's side faces leaving the corners to the front and back, `houseRubbleHoles`: the door below its head, a window between its sill and head, a gable above the eaves only under the roof's line), the masonry heaped along each wall where it came down (a low mound and broken blocks, lumps of plaster or daub, a log house's log ends; more where the wall stands lower; the doorway kept clear), the roof's timbers and its slates, thatch or shingles inside (`houseRubbleGeo`); the walls' colliders cut down to 1.6 m (`houseRubbleColl`) and the shots' walls to the broken line (07g_walls.js `wallHouse` asks `look4WallTop`). Built back: the rubble is cleared and the walls rise a course (0.25 m) at a time over the first 60% of the scaffold's time (`houseRubbleLevel`, the shader's `uHD.x`; the new work pale, uncracked), the mesh made again each course; none for a house that did not fall to stones. The houses' working scaffold (version 6's was poles, a plank a lift and a ladder no one could climb, 04f_rooms.js `houseScaffold`: only its poles' colliders are left, standing down and never raised, so what is placed at boot and the land's checks read the world as before): a `look3Scaffold` round the house a metre out from its walls (1.25 from a roundhouse's, under its eaves), the doorway clear, one lift (two or three for a hall) at working height with its rail and braces, a ladder up to it standing to a climber's reach above the boards, the standards solid (`c.hscaf`), the boards floors and the ladder climbed while it stands (`look3ScaffoldWorld` with the house as the frame), laid out the first time the house is put back and kept (`houseScafLay`, `houseScafSync` from `houseRubbleSync`); the new roof frame (rafters, ridge, battens) goes up on the walls once they stand (at once for a burnt house), the new covering waiting in bundles at its foot. The townsfolk at work stand inside it at the wall, at the first of a few places along the walls clear of what stands by the house (`houseScafWorkSpot`, `houseSpotClear`; from `houseWorkPlan`, 08f_townlife.js: they hammered at the air 1.7 m off). Not saved: it follows `H.bat.fell` and the house's state. Test hooks `__hm.rubble` [motion5] (version 16) A builder's work spot is `HAMMER_REACH` (0.705 m) from the wall's face, where its mallet meets the wall (13y_motion5.js; it was 0.5 m and the blow went 22 to 25 cm into the wall). |
| `04k_hall.js` | The keep's great hall: each keep's hall is a house record of the keep site (`planKeepHalls`, run after every town so town house numbers stay stable; `H.keepHall`), drawn by the houses machinery in its realm's style (Logres stone under slate, Ériu a great roundhouse, Thule a log longhall with a long hearth), furnished by `keepHallLayout` (war table, armourer's bench, basin, great hearth, high seat, long tables, hangings, torches, weapon rack, stores). `hallOffers`: the war table opens the map and grants command (`cwCanCommand` asks `hallCommandGrant`) to anyone of the realm holding the keep, the armourer mends (`repairAll`) and cleans, the basin washes (`washGear`), the hearth dries and heals, the stores sell building goods. Banner poles before the door (`keepHallPoles`). Test hooks `__hm.fn.halls()`, `hallOf`, `hallCommandGrant` |
| `04l_towers.js` | [land] Towers you can go into; [keeps] (version 11) every tower of every keep. A tower is data built by one kit: its shell, a drum of masonry (`towerShellDrum`: NS ring sectors from `twSector(RI, RO, NS)`, true arcs so a doorway's reveals are clean, one `wall` collider a piece stopping short of a doorway, lintels, thresholds, arrow slits) or a square shaft of logs on a stone footing (`towerShellSquare`: four walls, the ±z ones the full width, with doorways cut as columns, corner posts, wall plates, loopholes); its openings (`towerGap`, a door and its doorway and the floor through it: `towerEntry`, `towerDoor`, `towerThreshold`), floors in strips across the line of the ladder's hatch (`towerFloor`: flags, boards, slabs or a gallery's boards, overlapping a little so no body finds a crack; `T.levels`), ladders (`towerLadder`) and the hatches they climb through (`towerHatch`), joists, furniture and torches as item lists (`towerFurnish`: barrel, crate, rack, table, bench, arrows, wood, windlass, torch; a: the wall's angle, off: along it, r: out from the middle; each a `prop` collider in `T.props`), and its top: crenels on a corbel course (`towerTopCrenels`), a fighting gallery under a shingle pyramid (`towerTopGallery`) or a platform with a breastwork under a thatched cone (`towerTopThatch`, open where the walk comes in). The ladder and hatch measures follow the room (`twDims(RI)`; the corner drum's are `TW`). `towerFinish` cuts any curtain collider that ran through the rooms back into the masonry (`towerCutWalls`, the old cut ones marked `c.cut`), stands the old solid tower collider down, clears the grass and puts the tower in the rain shaders' roof list (`T.rain`, `T.shelterY`). The towers: Logres, four corner drums (`towerStone`: store, guard room at the walks with a door onto each, open top) and two gate drums (`towerGateStone`, `GATE_TW.stone`: the gate-ward's room, a room at the walks with a door onto the front walk and one onto the walk over the gate, an open top over the gate); Ériu, two gate drums of stone under thatch (`towerGateRath`: a store, a ladder up through a hatch onto the platform at the walks' height) and two side lookouts in the ring wall (`towerLookout`: the same, the walk running on through the platform); Thule, four corner and two gate shafts of logs (`towerTimber`: a store, a room at the walks with a door onto each walk that meets it, a fighting gallery under the shingles). Ladders are climbed as a motion (`e.motion.kind = 'ladder'`, `towerLadderStep` from `updateMotion`; body action 14, locked to the height climbed; on the rungs `ladderRungs` in 04m_ladders.js), started by walking into one (with a key or the stick held: a player shoved onto the foot does not climb), by E (`towerOffers`) or by a route (`castleFollow`, `castleClimbToward`: a route point with `ladder`; a body not yet at its start walks there); a shut hatch is a floor, pushed open as a climber comes up to it (`ladderPush`), E opens or shuts it (`Hh.held`); doors swing open for any body within 2.2 m. The doors and lids of a keep are one mesh moved vertex by vertex when one swings (`towerParts`, `towerPose`); what is only seen from inside is a mesh of its own for each castle, drawn only within 70 m of the camera (`towerInnerVis`, called each frame by `updateMountains`). The rooms keep the rain and snow out (`towerRoofOver` in `skyOver` and the splashes, `towerRoofsForRain`). `towerRoomOf(e)`: the tower whose rooms hold a body, and the floor (`lv`, `top`). Routes: `towerWayUp` (a corner drum from its side walk), `towerWayIn` (any tower from its courtyard door, up every ladder). Test hooks `__hm.towers` [motion4] `towerLadderStep` sets the climber's phase to the rungs' own cycle (2 pi for every 0.6 m from the ladder's foot), for `ladderLegs` and `ladderHands` (05a_rig.js). |
| `04m_ladders.js` | [keeps] Fighting on a ladder and at a hatch (version 11). On the rungs a body keeps one hand on them: its blows are weaker (`ladderWeak`, asked by `computeDamage`: `LADDER.blow` 0.6, `LADDER.ranged` 0.4 for a body that fights with a bow or a staff), it cannot raise a shield or parry (`canBlock`), draw a bow, cast a spell with a cast time, charge, leap, tumble or blink (`ladderAllows`, asked by `canUse`: 'ladder'); a foe in reach on the way holds the climber (`ladderFoe`, `ladderCanStrike`: within `LADDER.reach`, a foe on the floor above only once head and shoulders are through the hatch), who strikes it from the rungs at its auto-attack's pace (`ladderStrike`, body action 18). A heavy blow (`LADDER.knock` of its health, a smash, a blunt arrow, `opts.heavy`: `ladderOnHit` from `dealDamage`) or any stun (a bash, a freeze, a parry's: `ladderOnStun` from `addBuff`) knocks a climber off (`ladderKnockOff`, the motion's `st` 3): it falls to the floor below (`ladderFallStep`) and the fall harms it by the height, armour or not (`LADDER.fall`: none below 1.6 m, then 7.5% of its health a metre, at most 65%; winded 0.7 s). A shut hatch with a foe standing on it cannot be pushed open: the climber heaves at it every 1.3-1.8 s (`ladderPush`; the lid jumps, `Hh.bump`) and now and then it bursts open and throws whoever stood on it aside (`LADDER.push`: 20% against an NPC, 8% against the player; `ladderShoveOff`); friends standing on it step off. A defender at an open hatch drops the lid on a foe coming up through it (`ladderSlam`: E 'Slam the hatch on them', or a garrison body beside it, `LADDER.slam` a second): a heavy blow, so the foe falls. A garrison archer (`ai.wall`) steps onto a shut hatch while foes climb beneath it (its post moved there: `ai.hatch`) and goes back after 4 s without one. The player on the rungs: W or the stick up climbs on, S or the stick down goes back (a fresh press: `m.lock`), E 'Climb back down / up' (`ladderOffers`); a target in reach is struck from the rungs. Sight in the towers (`towerSightBlocked`, asked by `inSight`, `autoAttack`, `canUse` and `dealDamage`): a floor or a shut hatch between two bodies hides them from each other (through an open hatch, near it, they see: `towerHatchSees`); a body in a room and one outside see each other only through an open door or a slit at about the same height (`towerOpeningSees`, `T.slitsAt`); anyone on an open top is seen as on the walls. The Chronicle's war page explains it all (`towersChronHTML`). Test hooks `__hm.ladders` (`LADDER.stats`: blows, knocked, falls, slams, heaves, held, burst, holds) [motion5] (version 16) A body falling from more than 0.9 m takes the crouch (action 13) as it comes within 0.5 m of the ground and lands in it (`ladderFallStep`: it came down with its legs tucked and stood up straight in the frame it landed): war2's soldiers jumping down from a walk, a burnt walk's fall, a climber knocked off. |
| `04n_towerfire.js` | [keeps] A timber tower burns, falls in and is built again (version 11). The Thule shafts of logs catch from a fire spell or a burning arrow landing on them (`towerFireAt`, asked by `fireLandsAt`), the dragon's breath (`towerFireCone`, asked by `fireConeAt`) or a brand held to their logs (E, `towerFireOffers`); never from the fire of their own realm's soldiers (`towerFireSpared`; the player's always). [siege2] (version 13) The thatched cones of the Ériu gate towers and lookouts (look `rath`) burn the same way (`towerConeHit`: fire landing on the cone or the posts under it; a brand on the platform, 'Set the thatch alight'), their flames on the posts and up the cone (`towerFireSpots`), and fall in: the cone and the posts' tops are cut away above the breastwork (the shader's `towerCut`, a circle for a drum: `uTA.z` minus its radius), the charred thatch and poles heaped on the platform (`towerConeHeap`), which stands with its ladder; thatched again in peace. `towerIgnite`: the fire climbs the shaft (`TFIRE.climb` s to the roof) with real flames at spots up its walls, gallery and roof (`towerFireSpots`, `addFire` with a light), chars it, burns whoever is in its rooms or its gallery (3% of health a second at full heat) and drops embers round it; rain damps it and puts out a young fire. After `TFIRE.burn` s the gallery and the roof fall in (`towerFallIn`: the gallery's floors, breastwork and posts stand down (`T.topFloors`, `T.topColls`), its ladder is `gone`, a heap of charred timbers shows in the room below (`towerHeaps`), whoever was up there falls); once the keep has been quiet `TFIRE.quiet` s it is built again over `TFIRE.rebuild` s, rising course by course, the new timber pale for a while (`towerRebuilt`). The castle shader shows it (04g_castles.js: `uTA`/`uTB`/`uTR`, `towerCut` discards what has fallen, `tChar`, `tGlow` embers, `tFresh`; `castleTowerUpload`, which also gives the shader every tower's rooms (`uTS`: no snow lies and no wet film forms on a floor within a tower's outer face below its `shelterY`, `towerShelterK`)); the towers' inner rooms and moving parts are drawn with their castle's material for it. Save part `world.x.towerfire`. Test hooks `__hm.towerfire` |
| `04g_breach.js` | [walls] Stone towers breach and are mended (version 12). Every drum of masonry (the Logres corner and gate towers, the Ériu gate towers and side lookouts: 20) is a section of its castle (`towerBreachInit`, from `buildTowers`; appended to `C.sections` after the curtains', so saved walls keep their numbers): `kind: 'tower'`, centred on the drum (`across` its outer radius, `half` along its face, `hpK` 1.25), the breach facing out from the castle's middle (`aOut`, world `ox`/`oz`), the stump at the first floor above the store room less 0.45 m (`cutY`). The section's own machinery does the rest (`castleSiegeHit` wears and breaches it, `castleBreach`, `updateBreaches` mends it in peace, `castleRepairDone`, the land's save): `W.floors` (the rooms' and the top's floors, the thresholds at the walks), `W.colls` (the ring of masonry open in the V, its pieces above the stump, the top's crenels and pole, the furniture), `W.lower` (pieces cut down to the stump while it lies breached), `W.rubbleFloors` (a ramp up from the field over the fallen face and one down into the store room), `W.wreck` (`towerRubbleGeo`: the mound, tumbled blocks, the floor's broken beams, the broken ends of the drum) and `W.scaffold` (`towerScaffoldGeo`). `towerBreachUpload` (from `castleCutUpload`) gives the castle shader each tower (`uKA`/`uKB`/`uKC`: centre and radius and the height above which nothing stands, the breach's direction and the V's foot and half-angle, the inner radius and the rubble's top, wear and fresh stone; `towerBreachCut` in `TBREACH_GLSL`, part of `CASTLE_CUT_GLSL`: cracks spread over the drum as it wears, the upper works and a ragged V are discarded, the store room's contents above the rubble) and keeps the tower's own state with its section's (`towerBreachSync`, idempotent: `T.breached`, ladders `gone`, hatches and doors at the walks `gone`, the pieces cut down, the torches out, the banners on its top folded away). `towerBreachFall` (from `castleBreach`): whoever stands up there falls (the ladder fall's motion: harm by the height), the climbers on its ladders are knocked off. `towerBreachMended` (from `castleRepairDone`): the archer whose post is on its top walks up again. A breached tower is open ground (`towerRoomOf`, `towerRoofOver` and the shader's shelter leave it out). [siege2] (version 13) The engines take a tower for their mark when it is the weaker way in (`siegeMarks`, 04i_siege2.js), and a band storms a fallen tower one or two at a time through its store room (`siegeStormBand`, `siegeStormStep`: in version 12 a band of ten sent in over the rubble stood stuck in the store room and the gap for over two minutes); the garrison holds the court before its courtyard door either side of the door's line (`towerPlugAt`), and the player can storm it (`towerBreachRoute`). Loads before 04g_castles.js (its GLSL is part of the castle shader's). Test hooks `__hm.breach` [motion5] (version 16) The stones of `towerRubbleGeo` over the ways in lie sunk in a mound of broken stone over the ramps (`rubbleSinkY`, `rubbleMound`, 13y_motion5.js), so a stormer's feet are on the stones drawn under them. |
| `04g_burn.js` | [siege2] A burh's palisade burns as it is built (version 13). The palisades of the Thule keeps (`palisadeWall`, 04_props.js) in cells of `PAL_CELL` (1.5 m) along each wall, clear of the towers' log walls (`palisadeInit`, lazily: `C.pal`). A fire spell or a burning arrow landing on the logs, a brand held to them (E: 'Set the palisade alight', `palisadeFireOffers` from `towerFireOffers`), the dragon's breath (`palisadeFireCone`) or a burning tower beside them sets a cell alight (`palisadeFireAt`, asked by `fireLandsAt` with the caster: never the fire of the keep's own realm, `palisadeSpared`; the player's always, a soldier's only now and then, `PAL_T.npcCatch` 0.3; a spark with no caster never takes again in a charred stretch); it takes hold over `PAL_T.climb` s with real flames and smoke against both faces and along the top (`palisadeSpots`), chars the logs, spreads to the cells either side (`PAL_T.spread`, at most `PAL_T.reach` (3) stretches from where it took hold: `c.gen`; at a wall's end into the tower its logs meet), the keep's own people within 3.5 m beat it down (`PAL_T.douse`), burns whoever stands against it or on the walk behind it, sets the grass alight with its embers (never the wall itself: `PAL.sparking`), and rain or snow damps it (a young fire in a downpour goes out, `st` 2: charred, standing). After `PAL_T.burn` s a cell burns through (`st` 3): neighbouring burnt cells are gathered into a gap (`palisadeGapOpen`: a section-like record, kind `burnt`, `pal` set: `wx`, `wax`, `half`, `across`, `state` 'breach'), the palisade's colliders on that line are cut over it (`palisadeWallColls`, `castleSplitColl`: the middle stands down), a heap of charred stumps and fallen logs lies in it (`palisadeWreckGeo`), and it is a breach (`palisadeGaps`: `siegeOpenBreach`, `siegeDanger`, `siegeOnBreach` plugs it with the guards not already holding another breach, and tells of it once in 30 s a castle). In peace (`PAL_T.quiet`, and no fire within 6 m of it along its wall) its owners build it back behind scaffolding over `PAL_T.rebuild` s (`palisadeScaffoldGeo`, `palisadeGapClose`), and a charred stretch that stood is replaced; new logs pale for `PAL_T.fresh` s. The castle shader (`PAL_GLSL` in `CASTLE_CUT_GLSL`: `palisadeCut(vW, vMat)`, its logs, sharpened tops and loopholes only) reads up to `PZ_MAX` (6) zones a castle (`uPS`, `uPY`, `uPA`, `uPB`: runs of burning or charred, burnt-through, rebuilding or fresh cells, `palisadeUpload`): their char, embers and fresh timber as a burnt tower's (`tChar`, `tGlow`, `tFresh`), and above a gap's stumps it discards (a ragged top and ragged ends). `updatePalisades` from `updateSiege`. Save part `world.x.palisade`. Loads before 04g_castles.js. Test hooks `__hm.palisade` |
| `04g_blaze.js` | [siege3] Everything of timber in a keep or a temple burns as it is built (version 14). The burhs' wall walks (`castleTimber`'s decks and stair landings, `C.decks`) are cut into cells of `BZ_CELL` (1.6 m) along each deck, each with its own floor (`addFloorBox`, `f.deck`; the deck's one floor stands down at `blazeInit`); the timber hallow's log ring (`castleTemple`'s 16 segments, `C.ring`: its walk floor, its breastwork's colliders and its own wall collider from `buildTempleColliders`) is a ring of cells too, its two meshes moved onto the castle shader (`TEMPLE_MESH`, 04_props.js); the gates of planks of the burhs and the timber hallow (`blazeGateInit`: `G0.bz`). Fire lands on them through `deckFireAt` (from `fireLandsAt`, with the caster: `blazeSpared`, the keep's or temple's own realm's fire never; a soldier's takes only now and then, `BZ_T.npcCatch`; sparks with no caster not in boards burnt once nor from the walls' own embers, `BLAZE.sparking`, `PAL.sparking`), `deckFireCone` (the dragon) and a brand's E (`blazeOffers`: 'Set the wall walk alight', 'Set the log wall alight', 'Set the gate alight'). A burning cell (`deckBurnStep`) climbs in heat (`BZ_T.climb`), chars, keeps two flames (`BZ_FLAMES`: on its boards and under them; a ring's on its faces and breastwork), burns whoever stands on it (and less, whoever is under it), drops embers into the court, spreads along the walk (`BZ_T.reach` cells from where it took hold: `c.gen`), to the palisade cells it runs beside (`c.pal`) and the tower it meets (`c.tw`) only near where it took hold (gen 1 at most, and what it lights goes no further: a fire crossing back and forth would never stop), and the other way (a burning palisade cell or tower lights the walk beside it); rain and snow damp it; after `BZ_T.burn` s it burns through (`deckThrough`): its floor stands down (a ring's wall collider and breastwork too), whoever stood on it falls (the ladder fall's motion with a stand-in ladder `BZ_FALL_L`: harm by the height), charred boards (`deckWreckGeo`) or stumps and logs (`ringWreckGeo`) lie below in the engines' charring shader, and the news tells it once in 45 s. In peace (`BZ_T.quiet`, nothing burning there) a burnt-through cell is built back over `BZ_T.rebuild` s (the gap closes from its ends, `deckMended`), a charred one replaced; new timber pale. The castle shader (`BLAZE_GLSL` in `CASTLE_CUT_GLSL`: `deckCut(vW, vMat)`, `uDS`, `uDY`, `uDA`, `uDB`; up to `DZ_MAX` (8) zones a castle, `blazeUpload`; a hallow's ring in sectors, `uDY.y` 1, `blazeRingUpload`) chars the boards, joists and posts (wood and logs ids 1 and 13; a ring's logs 5 too) as a burnt tower's (`tChar`, `tGlow`, `tFresh`) and discards a burnt-through stretch's boards and joists. A gate (`gateBurnStep`): its leaves on the engines' charring shader (`siegeMat`) while it burns and stands charred, its strength going with its timbers (`BZ_T.gate` s), three flames on both faces, whoever stands in the gateway burnt; when its strength is spent it falls (`gateFalls`: `killEnt` with `D.burntDown`, so the news says it burnt down; the charred wreck), and the war hangs new leaves as for a broken gate; a hallow's gate takes fire only when its realm has lost a keep (`doorVulnerable`). The garrison fights fire: once a second `blazeCrews` sends the nearest free people of each keep and temple (not in a fight, not on a ladder, a route, a breach or a sally; a wall archer only to a fire on its own floor close by) to each fire on its palisade, walks, gate and towers (`blazeFiresOf`), two a fire and four in all, and `blazeFightStep` (from `thinkPosted`) takes them there (up by the castle's ways, `castleWaypoint`, then along the walk cell by cell, `deckNextToward`) to beat it with body action 21 (`BZ_T.beat` a second each; a palisade's own `PAL_T.douse` counts them there; a tower from the court at half the pace); a foe close by comes first, and a fire it cannot get nearer to in 12 s is given up for 30 s; the work over (`blazeJobEnd`), it goes back to the floor it was on (`e.ai.fireBack`, `blazeBackStep`: down from the walk by `castleWaypoint`; thinkPosted walks it the rest, as before: a guard of the court left on the walk walked straight for its post and stood at the walk's edge). The player beats flames with Use beside them (`blazePlayerBeat`: `BZ_T.beatP` a press). Field note `firewall`, the Sieges page's section, two loading tips. Routes over a burnt-through walk are left (`blazeRouteCut`, `blazeGapOn`, asked by `castleFollow` and `castleWaypoint`). Save part `world.x.blaze` (cells not whole: burning with their time, charred, burnt through and how far built back; burning gates). Also calls `houseRafterSync` (04j_rafters.js) each step. Loads before 04g_castles.js (its GLSL is part of the castle shader's). Test hooks `__hm.blaze` |
| `04g_castles.js` | Working castles: floors (`floorAt`, `floorSolidAt`), stairs, wall walks with crenellated parapets, open tower tops ([land] the stone keeps' corner towers are hollow and climbed from inside: 04l_towers.js; [keeps] every tower of every keep: the walks run in at their doors, Logres has a walk over its gate between the gate towers, Ériu's walk runs onto its gate towers' platforms, Thule's decks end at the towers' walls), gates with hinged leaves and portcullises (open for their own side, shut against enemies), garrison posts and walking routes (`castleRoute`), breachable and repairable wall sections (`castleSiegeHit`), crack and breach shader patches |
| `05a_rig.js` | Characters built in code: skinned humanoids and beasts. [motion3] `HUMAN_GAIT`: `footPath` lands a foot at the stance's pace (standing still over the ground) and steeply, `humanStepCap` (short steps on a steep flight); a leg swung out for the lock is turned by the ankle's real depth under the hip (where the leg cannot reach that deep and `legIK` lifts the ankle, by the depth it reaches: `rL`, `upL`); the toes point down in the air no faster than the foot rises (the toes 0.13 m ahead of the ankle and 0.09 below it rise at least three quarters as fast as the ankle) and the ankle comes forward over them as the foot turns (`pvL`), so they leave the ground from where they stood; a body leaning into a turn has each ankle first set where the lean (about the ground under its middle) will carry it to its place (`lnK`); the ankle undoes the leg's turns in the reverse of their order (the thigh's and knee's pitch, then the hip's swing out and the lean), then gives the foot its heading (the lock's: a planted foot keeps its own while the body turns over it) and its pitch; `FOOT_LOCK` has 1024 rows, its fourth channel 10 (20 on a stair's tread) plus half that heading's turn [motion4] (version 14) `armIK` (the arm's two-bone IK in the body's own plane), `rungLimb`, `ladderLegs` and `ladderHands` (each foot on a rung while it bears the climber, its sole on the rung's top and the ball of the foot over it, each hand round a rung while it holds, then the limb reaching up past the next rung for the one above, the diagonal pair together, on the rungs' cycle: the phase from `towerLadderStep`, the rungs `LADDER_AHEAD` 0.33 m ahead, in the world's metres over the body's own size) in actions 14 and 18; a blow, a flinch, a reach, a smash and a parry (1, 4, 5, 8, 11) shift the weight over feet that stay (the legs reach them by IK from `fL.x - stepZ`); the boat's shove (19) steps each foot into the stance, lifted, over the floor the lock gives, the hips sinking as far as the legs need; at the oars (16) the hands reach the grips where boatsDraw has the oars (`OAR_GRIP`, `OAR_LOCK`; no horse's seat motion for a rower), each arm swung in at the shoulder as its grip comes in toward the keel (`uaLy`, `uaRy`: a turn about the chest's upright, applied after the shoulder's pitch and roll, none unless set); the kneel's front foot on the floor (`legIK(0.5, -0.402)`). [motion5] (version 16) A walker's heel rises before its toes leave the ground: the ankle pitches the foot about the ball (0.14 m ahead of the ankle and 0.088 below it) through the last `hoD` (0.3 of the duty walking, 0.24 running) of the stance, up to `hoP` (0.62 rad walking, 0.5 running), and on into the swing's toes-down, so the point it stands on stays put while the sole's middle rises and rolls forward (a walker stood flat-footed to the last frame and the foot left the ground all at once); a mallet in the right fist (`WB.hammer`, bit 22) and body action 23 (hammering at a wall); a crouch (action 13) keeps each foot where it stands (the lock's place, on its own floor) with the legs reaching it wholly as the hips sink (it set the feet at fixed places, and its legs, mixed by their angles with the gait's, stood a body landing from a fall 10 cm in the ground); a shover's hands on her gunwale (action 19: the gunwale's height and her keel's offset come packed in the action's value, `motion5ActValue`, 13y_motion5.js; the hips as low as the hands need, the body following her for her first 0.3 m). |
| `05b_actors.js` | Instanced, vertex-animated crowds; particles; glows |
| `05c_beasts.js` | Wolves, boars and bears on a three-joint leg rig. [polish] `beastBodyGLSL(K)`: the gait (`bbGait`, `bbFoot`: the foot meets the ground and leaves it still going back under the body, the retraction kept to the ends of the swing, lifted and set down steeply), the target of each leg's low joint with the hoof or paw level under it (`bbTarget`, `bbTuck`), the vault (`bbVault`: the forequarters and hindquarters come down as far as their planted legs need to reach the ground, so straight-legged beasts no longer float their feet at the ends of a stride) and the body's turns (`bbBodyFwd`, `bbRot`); in `beastVS` every turn and drop of the body (lean, bob, lunge, vault, pitch, the back's flex) is undone along the leg before its IK, and the hoof or paw keeps its rest angle (`lowA`); the planted-foot lock (`footLock`, 13s_footlock.js) moves a foot's target along and across (the leg swings out about its top, the hoof rolls back level). The same chunk with the horse's joints gives `horseSeat` to the humanoid shader (`HORSE_SEAT_GLSL`, set here, then `humanoidMat` is built again): the saddle and the rider move with the horse's back. [motion3] (version 12) A foot rises its first `BB_LIFT` m (5.5 cm, once the beast moves at all) in the last `BB_CLEAR` (0.15) of its stance still standing where it stood, and comes down its last ones onto its landing place in the first of the next (`bbFoot` returns its clearance in z; `bbLift` adds it to the swing's arch), so no foot moves over the ground near it; the walk is the lateral sequence in four even beats (hind left, fore left, hind right, fore right: it was the diagonal one), the trot the diagonal pairs, the gallop hind right, hind left, fore right, fore left spread by its own duty (`gp` 0.12 between the pairs, the fore right `gq` = 0.8 of the duty after the hind left, so it is down before the hind left lifts: no leap with the legs flung fore and aft, only the gathered one after the fore left), the gallop's legs reaching to 0.46 of their length (0.42); the vault reaches where the lock holds each foot along the body (`bbVault`'s `dz`: a foot held back as the body sets off dragged up off the ground); a foot to the side turns the leg about its top by the angle that sets the low joint over it (`Tr.y - tg.y`: the hock and the fetlock hang straight below, so from the top the hind feet went half as far as the lock asked) and steps in under the body to 0.85 of the way to the middle. The test uniform `uBbDbg` is gone [motion4] Each foot's paw or hoof is built with its sole on the ground (y 0) wherever the rig's foot point is (`sole` by the kind of foot): a wolf's and a hound's pads stood 1.3 to 1.6 cm over it, a deer's hooves 0.9, a boar's and a pig's 0.7 to 1 cm and the cattle's 1.7 to 2 cm into it. [motion5] (version 16) The gallop's stance is longer (duty 0.3 of the stride, at most 0.58 of the leg's reach: `dG`; the pairs `gp` 0.14 apart, `gq` 0.8 of the duty) and the trot's too (at most 0.5 of the reach), so a horse at 11 m/s is in the air for a third of its stride, once, gathered (the leap was 0.48 of it); the hind feet's stance is set back under the body by `hb` (what the longer stance would carry them past the hip, `bbVault` and the foot both: the hind feet dragged 1.7 to 3.7 cm as they left the ground without it); `flBeastGait` (13s_footlock.js) mirrors it. |
| `05d_fire.js` | Fires, braziers, flashes; `gatherLights` picks the point lights for each frame |
| `05e_weather.js` | Rain, storms and lightning, snow where it is cold (by `farmTempAt`: the season and the place agree), mist |
| `05k_seasons.js` | The year across the world, from `SEASON` (08h_farming.js): `seasonCurves(k)` (everything that changes through the year), the uniforms each frame (`seasonsFrame`), the season map (8 m texels: each place's own warmth, and the snow lying there, built where the air is below freezing and melted in the warmth, rain and spring thaw: `seasonLieStep`), `uSnowCover`/`uSnowK` (see Materials), `seasonCrownK` (the canopy map's bare winter crowns), lake ice (`SICE`: every lake and pond texel but the running rivers freezes by its own temperature and thaws from the shallows; `seasonIceAt`, `seasonIceY` (a floor at the surface when 0.3 thick), swimmers lifted onto it, the water mesh's `aW.w`), leaves drifting down in autumn, the hole in the ice to fish through, the year's light (a pale winter sun, a mellow autumn: `uLightColor`, `uAmbient` and the grade's vibrance, after the time of day), `seasonBirdK` (fewer birds in winter; 05b_actors.js shows the fireflies on summer nights only, 08b_wildlife.js the butterflies in spring and summer), the field notes `snowlies` and `lakeice`, `seasonsChronicleHTML` (on the Fields page), test hooks `__hm.seasons`. `updateSeasons` runs at the end of `simStep`; a jump of the year's clock (a test, a load) settles the snow and the ice (`seasonSettle`) |

| `05i_aurora.js` | The night sky's own lights, a sky layer drawn after the sky and before the world from shared uniforms only: the aurora (long east-west curtains standing to the north, a sharp bright foot and fading rays, wandering and folding lines, green below, violet in the tall rays, a pink fringe when they flare; overhead over Thule, faint and low on the northern sky elsewhere; hidden by cloud, rain, snow, storm and mist, gone at dawn; three curtains with depth on high, three thin ones on medium, two without the per-pixel cloud mask on low) and shooting stars on clear nights. `tick` calls `updateNightSky(dt)` |
| `05h_wear.js` | Weather on bodies (`e.wet`, `e.snow`, `e.dirt`, `e.blood`, `e.batter`; shelter from roofs and tree crowns, heat from fires); gear wear (`it.dur` 0–100, `it.dirt`, `it.blood`; `wearOnHit`, `repairAll`, `washGear` at wells, troughs and lakes); lightning on tall things near the player (`strikeNear`, `strikeAt`); drying and melting rates; `wearVisual` packs the actor shader's `iWear` |
| `05j_farmbeasts.js` | Farm beasts on the beast rig: sheep (fleece curls, shorn), cattle and bulls (horns, udder), pigs (snout), hounds (collar), wild and saddled horses; a second kit merged onto `buildBeast`; the beast shader patched at anchors (fleece 30, horn 31, udder 32, face 33, beak 29; coat patterns; a lying-down rest pose, act 9); the hen's own rig (planted legs, flapping wings, a bobbing, pecking head; the neck stretches so the beak meets the ground; [motion5] her legs walk by `BIRD_LEG_GLSL`, 05j_birdlegs.js, her shank built through the hock with `birdLegTubePts`). Looks travel in `iColA.w` = scale + 4 × pattern + 16 × seed (`e.visCode`), the crowd in `e.crowd` |
| `05j_birdlegs.js` | [motion5] (version 16) Birds' legs that walk: the hen's (05j_farmbeasts.js `henVS`) and the game birds' (09v_birds.js `birdVS`). Each leg is two bones in its own plane by IK (`blLegPose`: the drumstick from the hip down to the hock, the joint bent back as a bird's is, and the scaly shank from the hock to the ankle; the rest joints per kind in `BIRD_LEGS`, (z, y) in the bird's frame; the geometry built through them, `birdLegTubePts`, rings close at the hock so it bends cleanly), reaching for where the gait puts the foot (`blGait`: duty from 0.62 walking to 0.45 running by the Froude number, capped to what the leg reaches, 0.4 of its length either side; `blFoot`: planted through the stance, rising its first `lc` (0.11 of the leg) still standing where it stood in the last 0.18 of the stance and coming down its last onto its place in the first of the next, the swing leaving and meeting that height at the stance's own pace as `bbFoot` does, the toes curling in the air and spreading before they land, `blToes`: their tips rise at least 60% as fast as the ankle). The body comes down as far as its planted legs need to reach their feet (`blNeed`, the vault: the bird rides up over each planted leg), the waddle rolls the body about the hips and not the legs (a mallard's most), and a hen's or a pheasant's head holds still in the air for 0.55 of each step while the body walks on under it, then thrusts forward (at most 7 / 6.5 cm; none for a mallard, none running). Birds and hens have no foot lock: their feet keep pace by the gait alone (`beastStride` gives the phase the same stride as `blGait`). `uLegJ` (the hock and the ankle) on the birds' material |
| `06_data.js` | Classes, abilities, monsters, names, progression (`LEVEL_CAP`), realm ranks |
| `06b_items.js` | Item quality, slots, generation, stats, how gear looks |
| `06d_goods.js` | Goods: everything carried that is not gear (materials, food, drink, seeds, herbs, tools, building parts), kept by count in `INV.goods`, listed in the bags, dropped and picked up like loot, saved with the character. The shared catalogue of ids every system uses (`log`, `plank`, `stone`, `iron`, `hide`, `wool`, `meat_raw`, `bread`, `water`, `herb_*`, `seed_*`, `tool_*` …). Save parts: `registerSavePart('char' or 'world', key, { save, load, key })` |
| `06k_build.js` | Building: placed structures (`BP`: foundation, plank/stone walls, doorway, window, thatch and shingle roofs, upper floor, stair, fence, gate, campfire, workbench, bed, chest) on a 3 m grid shared by a building (`bSnap`: frame of the nearest piece, cells and edges, heights from foundations and wall tops), refused off your realm's lands, near sites, camps, roads, water and on steep ground (`bValidate`, `bLandWhy`). Goods taken on placing, then raised with a hammer (`BUILD.work`, the piece's `uRise`); the piece shader is the building shader with rise, char/glow and shelter (`uPc.w` keeps rain and snow off what is under a roof). Real colliders (tag `build`, `c.build`) and floors (`addFloorBox`), doors/gates/shutters on pivots, roofs in `houseRoofOver` and the rain shader's roof list, fire (`buildFireAt`/`buildFireCone` from `fireLandsAt`/`fireConeAt`), spreading, falling to charred heaps (`bFall`), `bSupport` (nothing floats), repair at half cost, `buildDemolish` (half back), the cover map marked at runtime (`coverBox` + `COVER.tex.needsUpdate`). Bed = return point (`buildBedSpot` in `releasePlayer`). Save part `world.x.build`. For other systems: `buildFenceLine(x0, z0, x1, z1, { gateAt })`, `buildWorkbenchNear`, `buildHasRoof`, `buildPieces()`. Builder UI: G or the hammer button, `#buildbar`; test hooks `__hm.build` |
| `06s_survival.js` | Survival data: hunger and thirst (`SURV`, `NEEDS`; `needsEat(h)`, `needsDrink(t)` add 0..1 of a full need; `needsMult()` scales health, power and endurance regeneration in `updatePlayer`: well fed faster, hungry or thirsty slower, never death), skills that grow with use (`SURV.skill` craft, cook, fish, forage; `skillGain`), ward draughts (`survivalHarmMult` in `dealDamage`), the new goods (pasties, roast mushrooms, the cooking pot, potions, the four arrow goods if absent), the stations (`STATIONS`) and the book of recipes (`RECIPES`: `make`, `gear`, `act`; `cls`, `min`, `waste`, `pot`), gear odds by skill (`craftOdds`). Save part `char.x.surv` |

| `09u_depth.js` | Depth (version 9): the version 8 systems taken further. Forage plants burn (`dpIgnite`: from the burning ground under them, fire spells via `fireLandsAt`, a brand's E offer, the dragon via `fireConeAt`; real flames with `addFire`; they spread to grass, plants, trees and roofs), char, and grow back (`p.char`, `p.grow`, `p.regrowAt`); bushes are cut down with a knife or an axe (`p.cut`, collider off); apple trees are chopped, fall about their stump on a physical arc (`p.fall`), lie as a log (`p.log`, a `post` collider) and are cut into firewood and a log; stumps (nature's, apple and bush) are grubbed out with an axe or a pick (`dpGrubTree`, `p.gone`: collider off, dug earth on the nature map, never regrows), so the ground can be built on and tilled. The forage shader is patched at anchors (`depthForageVS/FS`, attribute `iSt`: char, fall angle, fall yaw, flag + fire). Game birds: pheasants in the meadows, ducks on the lakes (entities of role `bird`, faction neutral, `game`, `fixedY` set by `dpBirdStep`; their own bodies and flight in `09v_birds.js` (version 10): the pheasant's long barred tail, red wattles and green neck, the mallard's flat bill, green head and white collar; cocks and drakes in colour, hens in browns): they flush, fly off and land again; shot they fall and give meat and feathers; hens are plucked and moult feathers in a paddock. Crafting to item level 50: goods `grave_iron` (the Barrow's dead, the Hollow King), `dragon_scale` (Cinderwing), `trophy` (camp champions) from `depthOnKill`; recipes with `lvl` (level needed) and `ilvl` (the item level they reach: 36 or 50), nine named masterworks (`mw`: a weapon and a coat for each calling, a cloak for any), the master's bonus stat (`it.mw`, kept by `packItem`/`sanitizeItem`); the new herbs' draughts (Marshgold cure, Moonfern, Stonebloom) and farm produce (cheese, fried eggs, bean stew). Building: `roofHip`, `roofLean`, `well` pieces, leaded windows (`B_MODEL.window` replaced); built doors and gates open for townsfolk and soldiers of the player's realm and shut behind them, nobody idles in a doorway (`dpDoorsStep`); a well waters paddock troughs (32 m) and beds (24 m) and gives drink. Fire's caster (`fireLandsAt(x, y, z, r, by)`): the garrison's own fire never lights its town, keep, hall or the player's building (`depthSpares*`). Animals: a paired hen lays a clutch in the nest box (`depthClutch`, `DEPTH.nests`, drawn by `drawDepth`), taken with E or hatching in 150 s; eggs laid with a cockerel near are fertile and hatch if left (`L.hatch`); a hound herds stray sheep into their paddock, working each from behind until it is through the gate (`depthHerdStart`, cmd `herd`, `depthHerdThink`). Save part `world.x.depth` (grubbed trees, changed plants, nests). Test hooks `__hm.depth` |
| `09v_birds.js` | [motion] The game birds' own bodies and flight (version 10). `birdBuild(kind, lod)`: body, head, tail and legs, and real wings built spread (bones 3 and 6 the arm from shoulder to wrist with its coverts and secondaries, 4 and 7 the hand's fanned primaries, 5 and 8 the coverts on the flank; feathers are thin vanes with `aUV` across and along them); `birdVS` folds the wings back into the flank on the ground (the spread wing drawn in under the coverts), beats them (a quick downstroke, the hand lagging and flexing back on the upstroke), holds them out bowed in a glide and swings them forward to brake when landing, tucks the legs back under the tail in the air and reaches them down to land, pitches and banks the body about its middle and rides it on the beat; walking, pecking and the head-bob as the hen's. `birdFS` patches the farm beasts' fragment shader: flight feathers (material 34: dark shaft, pale edges, a pheasant's buff bars) and a mallard's speculum (35). Flight travels in the instance attributes (`birdPack`): the action is 17 (`BIRD_ACT`) with the wings' spread as its progress, `iAnim.x` the wingbeat's phase, `iAnim.y` 3 + its strength, `iAnim.w` 2 + pitch + 64 bank + 4096 legs + 65536 patter (a bird shot down keeps its death value under 2 and falls as before). `birdFly` (from `dpBirdFly`): a pheasant rockets up on whirring wings (11 beats a second) and levels out, flies in bursts of beats between long glides on set wings with a little weave, a mallard first runs pattering across the water (splashes and rings) then beats steadily; both bank into turns, glide in, flare nose up with legs forward and braking beats, touch down (a mallard on the water with a splash) and fold their wings over a moment (`birdSettle`). A landing on the wrong ground (water for a pheasant, a camp) goes round again. | `07d_dive.js` | [water2] Under the water. A swimmer in water at least `DIVE_MIN` (1.9 m) deep dives (U, or the pad's Jump, which reads Dive while you swim, Up while you are under and Dive in while you sit in a boat, going over the side: `diveToggle`, `diveStart`, `diveEnd`): 09_player.js hands the diver to `diveSteer` (through `water2Steer`), which swims the way the camera looks (`CAM.pitch` decides up or down), rises on Space or Up, drifts up when still (buoyancy), rises along the bed where it shelves, keeps the body (`DIVE_PIVOT` 1.3 m over its root) off the bed and under the surface; the current carries a diver through `updateWater` as it does a swimmer. Breath (`P.breath`, 0..1, `DIVE_BREATH` 32 s; faster when sprinting) runs down under the water and comes back in the air (the breath bar `#breath`, in `HUD_ORDER` after the builder's bar and the plant card: under the water nothing else is up (a dive closes the builder) and it takes the top middle; catching your breath out of the water it waits out of sight when there is no room, `HUD_HIDE`); without it the water takes 8.5% of health a second and kills (`DIVE_WATER`, "drowning"). Winter ice is a ceiling except at a hole, and so is a hull overhead (`boatKeelOver`: a diver comes up against the keel) (`DIVE.holes`: cut on thick ice with E, `diveCutHole`, or left by fishing through it; they refreeze; `seasonIceY` is null in them, the water shader opens the ice there through `uIceHoles`). The view: `U.uUnder` (on, surface, murk, depth) and `U.uUnderCol` drive `underFog` inside `applyFog` (02_render.js), the water surface from below (03b_water.js), shafts of light (`shaftMat`, instanced), motes and the diver's bubbles, the composite's wobble and deeper vignette (patched at its own lines), and the sound muffled (a low-pass on the master, `AUDIO.comp`) with a low rumble. Fish (`DIVE.schools`, one instanced mesh, `dvFishMat`: perch, trout, carp, char, pike by realm; they school, wander and scatter from a diver or a boat) in every water near the camera. Finds on the beds (`DIVE.finds`: lost arrows, old blades, purses, sunken chests in two of the three lakes, in the rivers' deep pools and the deepest ponds; models built with `Batch` in the building shader): E beside one takes it (`diveTake`: arrow goods, a weapon, gold, a chest's items and coin); they come back elsewhere on the same bed after 15-45 minutes; drawn within 70 / 55 / 40 m by quality. Save part `world.x.dive` (what is taken and when it comes back). Action 15 (`water2ActValue`: the fraction is the body's lean). Test hooks `__hm.water.dive` [motion5] (version 16) The underwater stroke's pace follows the diver's speed (`P.swimPh` turns 0.6 rad a second and 2.86 more for each m/s): a stroke carries about 2.2 m as at the surface (motion5.py's dive: 2.0 m at 2.5 m/s; it was 4.2 m, the arms in slow motion). |
| `07e_boats.js` | [water2] Boats. A jetty on each town's lake (`buildJetty`: out from the shore nearest the town until a boat floats alongside, clear of houses and colliders; a deck floor with a ramp from the shore, posts in the bed, bollards) with two rowing boats moored at its berths, and boats drawn up on the lake shores and the rivers' banks (`buildBoats`, from 13_main.js's "Raising the keeps"). Each boat is a clinker hull of overlapping strakes built in code in its realm's colours (drawn within 160 / 115 / 75 m by quality, the oars within 90 / 70 / 45 m; a hull is about 2,600 triangles) (`boatHullGeo`; oars `boatOarGeo` on pivots at the thole pins), drawn with the building pieces' shader (`B_MAT`: char and the fire's glow in `uPc`). `boatPhys`: strokes (`BOAT_STROKE` 1.55 s, thrust in the drive only), glide, drag along and across the keel relative to the stream (so the current carries her), the wind, swinging to a stream; `boatHit` against the shallows, land, ice, jetties, posts and other boats: she grounds (`grounded`), slides off and never crosses land. A hull is solid to bodies: `boatShove` (asked by `stepTo` after the colliders) sends a walker, wader or swimmer round it (an ellipse round the strakes; not from under the keel or over the gunwale), and `boatShoveBodies` shoulders aside the bodies in the way of a boat under way. A body that may swim (`e.swimOK`: a chaser, a companion) goes off a jetty's deck into deep water rather than being held at its edge (`jettyJumpIn`, asked by `stepTo` after `castleStepBlocked`). No grass grows up through a boat drawn up on a shore: `boatsCover` (asked by `buildCoverMask`) marks the beached ones, and a boat that comes to rest aground marks its own place (`boatCoverMark`). `boatPose`: heave, roll and pitch on a spring (waves, strokes, turns, knocks, aground, burnt and low). E steps in (`boatBoard`; a following hound jumps into the bow), out ashore, onto the jetty or into the water (`boatLeave`, `boatLanding`), ties up (`boatMoor`); from the water, climb up onto a jetty. The rower sits on the thwart (`e.seat`, `boatSeatY` through `waterFloorY`) facing aft and rows with W/S/A/D or the stick (`boatSteer`, action 16 with the stroke as its fraction; the oars sweep and dip in time); fishing from a boat (`boatFishSpot`, anchored while the line is out). Fire: `boatFireAt`/`boatFireCone` from `fireLandsAt`/`fireConeAt`, a brand's E; burning throws the rower out, chars and burns out; rebuilt with 6 planks and a hammer (`boatRebuild`), a town's own in five minutes. The towns' fishers (`boatLife`, asked by `houseLife` in 08f_townlife.js): in the morning one townsman walks out along the jetty, rows out, fishes (rod, line and float drawn here), rows back, ties up and brings the catch to the town's stores (`FARM.granary.fish_raw`, which the farmers' dialog shows). Companions: `water2PetWait` (asked by `anPetThink`) keeps a hound in the boat and makes companions wait at the surface while you dive. Save part `world.x.boats` (where each boat lies, its state and char). Test hooks `__hm.water.boats` [polish] A body in the water (wading or swimming: the player, a companion, a soldier, a fisher) that walks into a boat lying free pushes her (`boatPushed`, from `boatShove` in `stepTo`): she takes the push by the body's weight against hers (`boatBodyMass`, `BOAT_MASS`), turns about her middle by the push's lever, and goes on through the water's drag in `boatPhys` (never through the shore, a post, a jetty or another boat; bodies in her way are shouldered aside, and slow her a little); moored, beached, burning and rowed boats do not give. At the end of a boat drawn up on the shore (or aground) that lies up the beach, E or the pad's Use shoves her off (`boatShoveOff`, `boatShoveEnd`: the player leans into her, body action 19, and she grinds down into the water, `boatLaunchStep`, then drifts on free). A boat that drifts or is pushed in against the shore, a post or another boat only stops going further into it (no bounce unless she comes hard or is rowed) and, nobody at the oars and barely moving, lies still (`B.still`) until something moves her. Test hooks `shoveOff`, `shoveEnd`, `pushed` [motion4] The oar's grip `BOAT_GRIP` (0.59 m, `OAR_GRIP` in 05a_rig.js) inboard of the rowlock: at 0.86 m the grips met over the keel at the catch and the hands rowed 25 to 50 cm off them. The rower's arms reach the grips by IK and swing in at the shoulder as the grips come in (11 cm from the keel with the oars square to the boat): a hand is 2 cm from its grip on the median, 3.6 at most (`actions.py` row). [motion5] (version 16) `boatShoveOff` notes where her gunwale lies for the shover's hands (`P._shoveH`, `P._shoveX`: `motion5ShoveH`, 13y_motion5.js). |
| `07g_walls.js` | [walls] What flies stops at what it hits (version 12). `shotHit(ax, ay, az, bx, by, bz, o)`: the first thing a step of a flight crosses, exactly: every active collider near it (`collidersNear`) between its own heights (`y0` or `baseY` for those standing on the ground, less 2.5 m, to `+ h`; trees, bushes and fire pits let a shot by), every floor (`FLOOR`: a stair's slope, a thin floor's thickness), each house as walls to its eaves (a gable end to the roof's line) and its roof as a shell (`wallHouse`, `wallInRoof`; a window lets a shot in while its shutters stand open), the roofs of what has been built (`wallBuiltRoofs`, the shape `buildCamSolid` has), and the ground. A prop's or furniture's box stops a shot only below 1.25 m (it is a box round the whole piece). Openings: a crenel above the breastwork (`c.cren`, set by `castleParapet`: merlons, the sill), an arrow slit (`T.slitsAt`: the archer at it shoots out; from outside only a shot into the slit itself goes in), a built window's glazing, an open door (its collider is down). The shooter's own embrasure (a parapet within 1.4 m of where the shot was loosed, a ledge or a floor it stands on or at) and the crenel a foe at a parapet shows itself at let a shot by. Materials (`wallMatOf`: stone, slate, iron glance; logs, wood, plaster, daub, thatch, cloth, earth hold). `wallsProj` (from `updateProjectiles`, before a projectile moves: its drawn path, the arrows' arc too; one look covers two steps of the flight, so a shot found clear skips the next step's look, `p.wallClear`) and the drawn arrows (`stepArrows`: a body beyond the wall is not struck) and the engines' stones (`wallsStoneStep` from `siegeStonesStep`: struck early, `castleSiegeHit` at that point) stop where they meet something: `wallStrike` (an arrow sticks, `WALLS.stuck` (96, two and a half minutes, falling with a door that opens, a wall that falls, a roof that burns) or glances off stone, keeping some pace along the face and springing back off it weakly, and lies where it lands, near the wall's foot, `WALLS.fall`; a spell bursts, fire through `fireLandsAt` with the caster, `wallFire`). Aim: `wallsAimY` (a body up behind a parapet from below at its head and shoulders). NPCs: `wallsShotClear(e, t)` in `autoAttack` (and `npcAbilities` for a spell that flies): no shot into a wall, and `ai.wallShot` moves a ranged fighter for a moment (`fightTarget`: in toward a foe on the ground, off from one up on a wall); `pickTarget` and the posted and warband thinks prefer a foe the shot can reach; `wallsStepToCrenel` takes a wall archer to the crenel facing its foe. Dust and grit are lit as the light is (`wallLit`). `drawWalls` pushes the stuck and falling arrows to the arrow mesh (`ARROW_MAX` 240). Prints where a foot comes down (`tracksUpdate`, from the gait's phase as the wading splashes are): boots, hooves (a rider's horse too), paws and birds' feet (`TRK_BEAST`), in lying snow, in the mud after rain and on the wet sand by the water (`trkSurface`); siege engines cut two wheel ruts (`trkRuts`). New snow fills prints (`TRACKS.snowAcc` against `TRK_FILL`: about 25 s of a blizzard, a minute or more of light snow), fine weather dries them out of the mud and sand (`dryAcc`), snow melting takes them, a print laid on an earlier one cuts it (`trkLay`'s cells). Under a full cover of lying snow the grass is buried out of sight (03_terrain.js's blades, marked [water2]), so the prints show. Drifts: wedges of snow against the windward faces of walls, houses, fences and gates (`buildDrifts`, chunks of 64 m), raised in the vertex shader by the snow lying there, drawn only near the camera where the season's map has snow. Test hooks `__hm.water.tracks` [polish] The prints still showing near the player (the newest 320 within 90 m) are kept with the world (save part `world.x.tracks`: centimetres, each position after the first as a step from the one before, with the snow fallen into each or how long it has dried, and its age; its `key` changes once in forty prints laid), laid again on loading and filling or drying on from there (`tracksSave`, `tracksLoad`; drifts need nothing: they rise with the snow, which the seasons settle on loading). In snow a print's quad is drawn wider than the print, for the trodden, kicked-up snow round it, the dent deepening to a blue shadow at its floor and the tread faint: the prints join into a trail that reads from the camera's usual distance (they were specks). Each drift's face is also rasterised into `DRIFT_MAP` (03_terrain.js, 1 m texels, its height at full snow, toe and all: `driftMapFace`), which the grass reads with the snow lying, so no blade shows through a drift.
