| `07c_balance.js` | [balance] The balance of the callings. Bots that play a calling (`e.bot`, `botMake(e, 'simple' or 'skilled', home)`): an NPC bot has the role `bot` (no other think runs for it; `updateNPCs` still ticks its buffs, swings, power and footing) and is played by `botStep` from `npcClassStep`; the player with `P.bot` is played from `updateClasses` (the Hollow King check in `barrow.py`). The simple bot presses 1-8 in turn and lets the auto-attack run; the skilled one parries on the glint, spends momentum and changes stance (warrior), keeps range, kites, snares, picks arrows and draws fully (archer), plays the combos, minds attunement and blinks from blades (mage), heals early, keeps a renewal up, shields, hallows the ground, prays when safe and smites (priest). `fairGear(cls, level)`: a full set of the quality a soldier of that level wears (fine below 20, rare to 40, epic above). Diminishing returns on crowd control (`ccAdjust`, asked by `addBuff`): for players, bots and champions (`ccChampion`: camp champions, keep lords, hallow and muster wardens, warband leaders) a stun (a freeze counts as one), root or snare within 15 s of the last of its kind lasts half, then a quarter, then 6 s of immunity; a parry's stun goes through it for anyone (`{ ccAll: true }`); shown on the class panel (`classDrHud`). An allied priest near the fallen player offers to raise them (`raiseOfferStep`; the death screen's `#death-raise` beside the release): accepted, the priest walks over, prays if devotion is short, and raises you (`raisePlayer`). Tracked quarry on the minimap (`drawTrackedMinimap`). [balance2] The callings' answers: the mage's ward takes a stun (`wardTakesStun`), the warrior's Charge frees it for 2 s (`chargeFree`), the priest's own Renewal steadies its spells (`priestSteadfast`, `breakCast`) and its own healing falls with the square of its rank (`priestSelf`), the archer's Tumble slows the foes it leaps from (`tumbleSlow`) and its broadheads' wounds mend slowly (`healWound`); a melee blow sets a cast back (`castPushback`). The skilled warrior raises its shield against arrows and bolts in flight (`botIncoming`), the skilled priest keeps a reserve of power with more than one foe on it and renews before it casts with a blade in reach. Test hooks on `__hm.bal` |
| `07c_balance4.js` | [balance4] The callings within the aim for skilled and simple play (version 12). **Charge rushes**: since version 5 its reach (28 m) kept it out of the melee branch of `executeAbility` that held its dash, so it never moved the warrior, struck or slowed (it spent its power and cooldown, freed the warrior and gave momentum, and every version's balance was measured with it so); now `executeAbility` hands it to `chargeRush`, which sets a rush (`e.motion` of kind `leap` with `rush`, so everything that waits on a leap waits on it: `canUse` says busy, no shield, the foot lock leaves the feet to the run), stepped by `chargeRushStep` (from `updateMotion`): along the ground at `RUSH.speed` (18 m/s) through `stepTo`, homing on the foe, the shield raised across the body over the running legs (body action 10), dust kicked up; it stops at a wall or a tree (no headway), at deep water, at a drop or a climb (the floor changes by 0.9 m), when a root takes it (a snare in its path, a frost nova as it lands) or a stun, and after `RUSH.maxT`. Landing (`chargeLands`): a blow (80%) and, until Leap Strike is learned (level 30), a slow of 30% for 2 s (`RUSH_SLOW_EARLY`; Charge's own 45% for 3 s stays off, `RUSH.slow`); the warrior's abilities wait `RUSH_FOOTING` (0.3 s) and Shield Bash `RUSH_BASH` (3 s; after a Leap Strike too, `rushShare`). Charge waits 16 s, 12 s until Leap Strike is learned (`RUSH_CD_EARLY`). No rush on a horse, swimming, in a boat, from a fixed perch, at a foe on another floor or, for a guard at its post, at a foe beyond its leash (`rushWhy`, asked by `classCanUse`: garrison troops hold their posts). **Mend weighs the wound** (`mendWound`, asked by `priestView`: all of its measure from half health down, 30% on a scratch). **A seasoned mage**: its spells 7% stronger for each rank past the first (`mageRankK`, asked by `mageView` with the attunement). The bots' view of the rush (`rushComing`, `rushing`; `snareOnLine`: a snare on the rush's real way, which stops at reach short of the foe, counted if it will be armed by the time the rush passes it). `RUSH.onEvent` (tests): each rush's start and end, with why it ended (`duels.py trace=N` prints them). The priests' realm ways evened out (`PRIEST_REALM`, read by `classHarm` and `blessing` in 07b_classes.js: the Shield of Logres 8% and 8%, the Well of Ériu 2% a second, the Hearth of Thule 15%; their texts in `CLASS_REALM` from it). A skilled priest bot of rank I holds its mace from a warrior's guard (`e.bot.holdMace`, set in `botPriest`, asked by `classNoAuto`). The realm's way set aside for a measure (`e.plainCalling`, read by `realmFlavour`; `duels.py kind=flavour`). What a heal that ticks tells the tests (`TAP_TICK`). The tooltips of the rules of version 12. Test hooks `__hm.bal4` |
| `07c_balance5.js` | [balance5] The callings on clean ground, nearer the aim, and mastery that pays (version 13). **The measures' ground**: `balFlattenCraters` (`__hm.bal5.flattenCraters`, called by `classes.py`'s `CB.wipe` before every phase of `classes.py balance` and `duels.py`) undoes every crater and puts out every grass fire: a meteor leaves a bowl 2 m deep with a rim of thrown earth and a Leap Strike a dent, and a run's later duels were fought in pocked arenas where a bow's or a staff's shots struck the rims (Holy Fire, which does not fly, did not care): a skilled archer won 23 of 24 against a priest at 50 in a fresh arena and none late in the full table, so every table of versions 9 to 12 at 30 and 50 was measured on that ground. **Holy Fire flares** (`holyFlare`, asked by `priestView`): cast at a foe within `FLARE_REACH` (10 m) in the middle of a spell, the spell is lost (`breakCast`: not a steadfast priest's, not a boss's), the priest's interrupt. **A fresh Renewal's rule, tried and set aside** (`renewFresh`, `RENEW_FRESH_ON` false: Renewal on yourself steadying only when none of your own Renewals still mended you; the priest stood steadfast so seldom that a seasoned mage's storm broke four times as many of its spells). **Holy ground dazzles** the foes on it (`hallowDazzle`, from `stepGrounds`: the `dazzle` buff of the priest's rank for 1.3 s at a time, src `'hallow'`, the motes too). **Crippling Arrow wounds** for as long as it slows (`crippleWound`, from `updateProjectiles` after its snare: the `wound` buff at the archer's measure, 30%: an archer shooting plain arrows had no answer to a healer). **Whirlwind heals in the press** (`whirlHeal`, from the spin's cuts in `executeAbility`: `WHIRL_HEAL` 2% of health a foe a cut, up to five). **The realm's ways** (`ARCHER_REALM`: Logres draws 15% sooner (25%), Ériu's shot from hiding 65% harder (80%), Thule's arrows drift half as much in the wind (not at all) and its snares hold 4 s (4.5) and it strikes chilled foes 10% harder (15%); `PRIEST_REALM` changed again: Logres 6% and 6%, Ériu 2.5% a second, Thule 17%), their texts in `CLASS_REALM` from them. The skilled bots: out from under a falling meteor (a mage blinks, an archer tumbles, letting its own spell go; on foot only from near its edge, else it finishes its spell), out of a blizzard and out of burning grass, a spell finished first (`botDodge`, from `botStep` before `botMove`); the priest flares a heal or a long spell (`flareWorth`), keeps Holy Fire's burn off a caster in its reach that is not steadfast, renews before a long cast when a foe's flare is ready (`flareThreat`) and presses a bow (to its mace before Tumble's level, within its flare's 8 m after: from casting range it lost every duel against a bow on clean ground at 30 and 50, at its mace it won 23 of 24 at 50); the warrior walks round a snare on its rush's way behind a raised shield; a mage turns to storm against a mage soaked by its steam. Test hooks `__hm.bal5` |
| `07c_balance7.js` | [balance7] The callings within the aim for skilled and simple play alike (version 15). `B7T`: every rule of this version's measure in one object (the tests set them between runs: a rule against its absence in one boot), read by small marked edits where the rules live. **The archers' realm ways as edges on their own ground** (`ARCHER_GROUND`, `archerWayHere(e, realm)`): Logres from a wall, a tower or any perch (`elevatedEnt`: full draw a quarter sooner, `archerDrawK` from `drawFull`; drawn shots 20% harder, `archerHighK` from `looseArrow`), Ériu among trees (two standing within 7 m, `archerTrees`, cached a quarter second on the calling's state: hiding in half the time and walking hidden, `archerHideT`, `archerHideWalk` from `updateHiding`; a shot from hiding 80% harder, `archerAmbushK` from `classHarm`), Thule in the cold (`archerColdAt`: snow lying there, `seasonSnowLieAt` 0.25 or more, or the air at freezing, `farmTempAt` 0 or less: arrows the wind does not move, `archerWindK` from `looseArrow`; snares that freeze and hold 4.5 s, `snareCold`, `snareHold` from `stepSnares`), and anywhere Thule strikes chilled and frozen foes 10% harder (`ARCHER_REALM.thuleChill`). **Charge every 12 s at every level, its landing blow slowing the foe** (`B7T.chargeCdLate` from `chargeRush`, `B7T.chargeSlowLate` from `chargeLands`, 07c_balance4.js: from Leap Strike's level it waited 16 s and its blow did not slow), and **one rush of 5 s**: Charge and Leap Strike hold each other back 5 s (`B7T.rushShare`, read by `rushShare` in 07c_balance3.js, and the tooltips and the Callings page from it; `RUSH_SHARE` 10). **Plain shafts by rank**: 5% harder for each rank past the first (`B7T.plainRank`, `plainArrowK`, from `classHarm`: an arrow without a special head). **Mend on a scratch** (`MEND_WOUND.floor` 0.15, 07c_balance4.js: 0.3). The skilled bots: a warrior after its foe goes through burning grass (`botFireThrough`, from `botDodge`), everyone but a warrior steps out of a foe's volley before its second wave, a drawn arrow loosed and a spell finished first (`botVolleyAt`, from `botDodge`; `aoeMarker` now carries the volley's faction and start, 07_entities.js), and a priest in a duel of rank I mends 0.15 lower and smites instead (`B7T.priestRace`, in `botPriest`). Tried and set aside (`B7T`): Sanctuary on yourself weighed by the wound (`sanctWound`), a priest's bot smiting through a ready bash (`priestBait`), renewing for steadiness only when held (`priestSnareRenew` false), a skilled archer volleying a foe that stands (`archerVolley`), Leap Strike's landing slowing (`leapSlow`), deep frost not holding Charge (`deepFrost`), light feet at 25% (`lightFeet`), NPC priests keeping the devotion they come to the field with (`npcDevotion`, from `csOf`: an NPC priest's `devAt` of -99 fades its 20 to 50 devotion at the first look; kept, the priests won 72% of their duels at 50, skilled and simple). Test hooks `__hm.bal7` |
| `07c_balance6.js` | [balance6] The callings within the aim, measured in arenas far apart (version 14). **The measures**: `balBreakFires(centres, r)` (a firebreak: grass burning farther than r from every arena's middle is put out, `CB.breakFires` each simulated second) and `balCleanArena(x, z, r, gone)` (an arena made whole when its duel ends: the two fighters' snares, runes, holy ground, steam, arrows and bolts in flight, and within r the ice, blizzards, craters and burning grass), for `classes.py`'s `CB.spotsWide` and `duels.py`'s per-arena queue (`DS.queue`, `DS.step`). **Renewing by rote costs power** (`renewRoteCost`, asked by `priestView`: Renewal on yourself while your last one still has more than `RENEW_ROTE_LEFT` (2) s to mend costs `RENEW_ROTE_K` (2) times the power); a priest's own wounds mend at 76% of a heal's measure at rank I (`PRIEST_SELF`, 07c_balance.js). Tried and set aside (`STEADY_NEXT` false; `steadyCast` from `useAbility`, `castSteady` from `breakCast`, `steadyNext`): Renewal steadying only the next spell begun. **The ward keeps the spell** (`wardTakesBreak`, asked by `breakCast`: while a mage's Arcane Ward holds, a blow that would break its spell breaks on the ward for a quarter of what it has left; `WARD_GUARD`). **The seasoned mage's step** (`MAGE_RANK`, `mageRankOf`, read by `mageRankK` in 07c_balance4.js: 12% from rank II, 2% more a rank after; it was 7% a rank). **A held rush comes round sooner** (`rushHeld`, from `chargeRushStep`'s end 'blocked': half the Charge's wait). **The Well of Ériu's own heal** (`WELL_SRC`: the blessing's heal over time has a source of its own, so it runs beside the Renewal's instead of merging into it; `PRIEST_REALM.eriuHot` 1.2% a second). **The archers' realm ways evened out again** (`ARCHER_REALM`). The skilled bots: an archer takes a priest for a blade at its throat only at its mace from Tumble's level (`archerPresser`) and casts its long shots with a priest close by when the priest's flare is spent (`archerCastsBy`); a mage meets a staff at its elbow with its nova, then a blink away (`botMageElbow`), keeps its meteors and blizzards for a foe that cannot step out of them (`mageAoeWorth`); a priest keeps a warrior dazzled from rank II, keeps its Holy Fire for a flare at most `HF_HOLD` (3) s, and the bots see a warded mage's spell as a steadfast one (`spellGuarded`). Test hooks `__hm.bal6` |
| `05j_classfx.js` | The callings' visuals and sounds: ice sheets (drawn at their own water's surface, `uWY`), iron snares, frozen shells, steam, arrows in flight and in the ground, the parry glint, `classSfx`; rune and holy-ground marks are disc kinds in `05g_spells.js` (a rune is two discs: `runecut`, its strokes cut dark into the ground, and `rune`, their glow; a rune laid out of the camera's reach gets them when the camera comes near: discs carry an `owner`, cleared when the pool hands a disc to another spell); guard, parry and kneel are actions 10–12 in `05a_rig.js` |

[motion] Its airspeed eases (`dragonSpeed`, 5 m/s each second: no jump from a hover to full flight); it slows as it comes in to land, stalls in over its perch with its body levelling and its legs reaching straight down for the ground, and takes off by rearing on its hind legs with the wings raised (the hind legs turned back so the feet stay put), springing 3 m on the first great downstroke and climbing away forward as its speed builds (version 10) |

| `08h_farming.js` | Farming, herb lore and plant breeding. `SEASON` and `seasonNow()` (spring, summer, autumn, winter: two days each, 2.4 hours a year; read by foraging and anything seasonal), `seasonCold()` and `seasonWeatherOdds()` (05e_weather.js asks), `farmTempAt(x, z)`. Beds (`FARM.plots`, own `'p'`): tilled with a hoe or by hand, 4 x 6 slots, each slot one plant with traits (`FARM_TR`: yield, hardiness, drought, speed, colour, height); growth through stages by season, warmth, water (`P.moist` from rain, lost to sun and heat), light, weeds and soil (`P.fert`, tired by harvests, rested, beans and `manure`); frost (by hardiness), drought, trampling, storms laying tall crops flat, grass fire, craters. Strains (`FARM.strains`): seed lots as goods of their own (`seed_grain_s104`), made by keeping the seed of one ripe plant (its traits blended with its pollen parent's: plants of a kind in flower within 3 m cross); harvests give seed of the strain sown. The town fields (`W.fieldRects`, own `'t'`): strips with a `stand` (one crop, rotation, sowing windows) tilled, sown, weeded and harvested by Brackenford's farmers (`farmLife` and the `'farm'` plan step in 08f_townlife.js), or by the town itself when nobody is near enough to watch; the granary. Wild herbs (`FARM.herbs`, `FARM_HERBS`: eight kinds by habitat) examined into the herbal, gathered (they grow back), dug up (a knife lifts the root whole) and planted. The farmer's dialog (seed, hoe, knife, manure; buys produce). The plant card (`#farmcard`): what you look at, its traits and parentage, and the actions; E does the first. Hooks for others: `farmGrowAt(x, z, r, k)` (growth magic), `farmEatAt(x, z, r)` (grazing). Saves: world part `farm`, char part `herbal`. Test hooks on `__hm.farm` |
| `08i_farm_draw.js` | Farming drawn: every crop, weed and herb is a model built in code (`FARM_MODELS`, a near and a far model for each crop) whose parts grow out of their joints as the plant grows (`aPart`: kind, place along the part, the growth it appears at and is full at), one instanced mesh per kind with wind, wilting, nodding ripe ears, trampling, frost rime, snow, rain and char; flowers drop after flowering; night-glowing herbs. Beds are one merged furrowed soil mesh (ridges with the plants on their crests) whose shader reads each bed's state from a small texture (water, manure, ash, how far it is tilled, stubble, its crop's colour from afar). The dashed outline of a bed about to be tilled. Map patches (`drawFarmMap`) |
| `08g_campaign.js` | The campaign: commanders' plans with announced reasons (`planWar`, `announceOrder`), routes and order badges on the maps and nameplates, player orders to warbands from the map, claims (4 of 6 keeps for 5 min, or both enemy hallows for 4 min), renown, the long-war rules, victory, peace and reset, campaign history in the world save |
| `08j_war2.js` | [war2] A war that keeps moving (version 15). Measured with `tools/campaign_soak.py` (every band looked at every 2 s: fought, got nearer its next point, waited on purpose or stood still; every stall written down) and fixed at the causes it found. Off the walls: a band's soldier up on a keep's walk, stair, tower or gate walk with nothing to fight goes down by the castle's ways (`bandDownStep` from `thinkSoldier`: `castleWaypoint`, then down the way it was on to its foot; with no lower for 8 s, a stretch of walk cut off by burnt gaps, it jumps down into the court, the ladder fall's motion, hurt by the height); one in a tower's ground room goes out at its courtyard door (`bandOutOfTower`); one left in a fallen tower's footprint and not storming it (its band going in by the broken gate beside a fallen gate tower: siege2.py's stormall found one standing 84 s in the drum) goes out by the stormers' way, through the store room's door into the court where its band is or goes, else back over the rubble (`bandRubbleOut`). Out of a court: a band's way starts at the court of the walls its leader stands in (`bandStartNode`, from `wbPlan`: the nearest node in sight was often one outside the wall), and a leader or member in a court whose way lies outside goes out by the gate (a gate of its own realm opens for it while no foe stands within 19 m of it; with foes before it the band waits inside: `bandLeaderExit` at the end of `updateWarband`, `bandGateOut`, `bandExitPoint`); a band in the court of the keep it attacks takes the court for its goal, not the gate behind it (`bandInsideKeep`, in `updateWarband`). A band by its engine from far off follows its way to the gate until the engine's place is in sight (`bandLeaderExit`, 'bombard'). A leader that gets no nearer its next point for 8 s with nothing holding it finds its way round on a 1 m grid toward it, at most 60 m on (`bandLeaderDetour`: `localPath` with `gates`, so a castle's gate and a wall standing from the ground are walls), else its way is planned again. A bow, a wand or a staff whose shots at a gate strike the wall beside it (`ai.wallShot`) stands before the gate on its line (`bandGateShotStep`, from `fightTarget`). A climber (not the player) held on a ladder by a foe ahead for 25 s goes back the way it came (`ladderHeldTurn`, from `ladderRungs`). A band cut down to fewer than three (not bearing a hallow, not under the player's order) no longer holds its place at the muster (`cwBandsCounted`, from `planWar`: at low a realm musters two bands, and a lone soldier stuck at a far keep held one for ten minutes), and the next band to muster takes them in (`cwMergeRemnants`). A stone that lands beside a house cracks it (`houseBlowAt` from `siegeImpact`'s ground). The long war: a claim (four keeps, both enemy hallows) grows up to 1.6 times as fast from 15 to 40 minutes (`cwClaimRate`, `CLAIM_LATE`; the news and the countdowns follow it). Engines: one that gets no nearer its place for 25 s is settled where it stands (a lob lands wherever its mark is) or given up, one that rises no further for 45 s with a crew is given up (`siegeEngineGuard`, from `siegeEngineStep`); a band's engine at work, rolling on or rising is its progress for the 150 s watchdog (`warbandEngineBusy`). A member that gets no nearer along its leader's footsteps for 3 s leaves them for 6 s (`bandTrailStep`: `bandUnstick` acts); a firefighter on its way back down that gets no lower for 4 s forgets its way (`backStepUnstick`, from `blazeBackStep`). A foe the leader could not reach (`ai.unreach`) holds no band engaged (`wbEnemiesNear`). Storms: a band goes in at a fallen tower only with half again the keep's fighting garrison (archers on the walls at half, the lord in full), with a second band of its realm, or after a minute (`siegeStormReady`, from `siegeBandAfter`), and the commanders send a second band to a keep with a breach open (`cwChoose`'s reason 'wall', `cwOpenBreach`). The timber hallow's ring burnt through is a way in once the temple may be raided: a raid at its gate goes round to the gap (phase 'gap', `raidGapStep`) and the hallow may be taken while the gap is open (`hallowOpen`, asked by `relicTakeable`); out again by the gap (`bandExitPoint`). Townsfolk fight their houses' fires: a house's people and neighbours within 45 m run to it (`houseCrewCall` from `houseLife`, the 'beat' step of `lifeRun`, five at most, each at a place under the eaves nearest the fire's front: `houseCrewSpots`) and beat it (body action 21): every beater pushes the burning front back (`HCREW.k` 0.1 m/s a beater, half again in rain: `houseCrewDouse` from `houseBurnStep`), the flames beyond it go out, and a fire beaten to nothing is out (`houseQuench`: the house stands, black under its eaves for a few minutes, `H.w2scorch`); a fire that has taken the whole roof is past saving and they stand back. The player beats a house's flames with Use beside its walls (`houseBeatOffers`, `housePlayerBeat`, 0.3 m a beat). `HCREW.off` leaves fires to burn and the townsfolk run from them as before (tests). Broken gates (rammed, battered or burnt down) are kept with the world (save part `world.x.gates`: which, whether it burnt, seconds since). A new campaign begins with every keep's garrison whole, not only those of the keeps that changed hands (`restoreKeep`, 08g_campaign.js: a realm whose garrisons the last campaign had thinned lost both its keeps in the next one's first three minutes). Test hooks `__hm.war2`; `tools/tests/war2.py` |

It makes its own styles and markup; `startGame` calls `loreOnStart`, `tick` calls `updateLore`, saves carry `char.lore` (`done`, `seen`, `pro`, `pv`) |
| `10e_guide.js` | [guide] Players know what to do (version 13). The next step's place (`guideMarkOf(step)`: the herald, the bounty's creatures, the merchant, a fire to cook at, the tanning rack or the alchemy table, the portal stones, a keep of yours to climb, a warband, an enemy keep (one under siege first), an open temple or a loose hallow, the Barrow, a champion of your level, Cinderwing; a place in town while you are on the frontier is the muster's stones home, a place on the frontier while you are at home the town's stones), drawn as a gold gem on the minimap or an arrow on its rim (`guideMinimap`, from `drawMinimap`) and on the map with the step's name (`guideMapMark`), how far and which way in the tracker (`guideWhere`, `guideWhereLine`, `guideShortWhere`). Field notes for the systems since version 6 (`FIELD_NOTES` += needs, stones, station, ladder, tower, breach, engine, walls, gear, brand, burning, storm, bands, champion, gift, ranks, fish, crops, dark) and their watches (`GUIDE_WATCH`: each a function of the player and the prompt's text, called by `guideWatch` from `loreWatch`; slower looks once a second in `guideScan`); `guideNoteIndex`: the first-steps note before any other, nothing over a fight but a note with `fight: true`, a note with `ok()` only where it means something (lake ice only where the lakes freeze); `guideQuiet()` (other hints wait out a new character's first note: 08h_farming.js asks); `guideMade(rec)` (09t_craft.js: a meal cooked, a thing made). The late game's aims (`guideAims`: the next level, realm rank, the campaign), shown in the tracker once the path is walked and on the Chronicle's Aims page (`chronGoals`: what opens at 20, 30, 40 and 50, with what you hold now); the Gear page (`chronGear`: grades, item levels, wear, mud, wet, masterworks), the Sieges page (`chronSieges`: gates, engines, breaches and `towersChronHTML`) and the Fire and land page (`chronLand`: felling, brands, grass fire, houses that burn and are rebuilt, craters, lightning, tracks), Gear, Sieges and Aims each with `CHRON_EXTRA[page]` for other modules' sections; How to play (`guideHelpHTML`: the keys of a desk or the controls of a phone, then `GUIDE_HELP`, every system's block in the order a player meets them); loading tips for every system. Test hooks `__hm.guide` |
| `10f_guide2.js` | [guide2] Players know what to do, on a phone first (version 15). **The Chronicle folded** (`chronFold(sec)`, called by `renderChronicle` once a page is in place): a page taller than 1.3 of the sheet on a phone (2.5 on a desk) folds into its summaries: its lead stays, each top-level `<h3>` section folds under its heading (`details.cf`, the `<h3>` itself moved into the `<summary>`), a tall card (`.card` over 0.42 of the sheet: a calling, a realm) under its head and first paragraph (`cf-in cf-card`; the first lines clamped while shut), a tier (a `<div>` whose first child is an `<h4>`) under its heading, a long run before the first section under the page's word for it (`G2_INTRO`, else 'Read more'); the lead, the figures and what the page says of now and its links at its end (`G2_KEEP`: `.now`, `.acts`, `.fineprint`, `.tide`, `.live`, `.skills`) stay open; `data-cf-open` on a section's `<h3>` or a tier opens it by default; a fold opened or shut stays so while the game runs (`G2.open`); Open all / Fold all (`.cf-all`); a page of a lead and one block (the herbal's herbs, a list) is left as it is. At each page's end, See also: the pages a reader goes on to (`G2_SEE`, `chronSee`, where the page does not link them already). Heights on a 390x780 phone (the sheet 746 px), natural against folded, See also included (`G2.stats.pages`; `guide2.py` prints them all at 390x780, 360x640 and 844x390, `tools/out/guide2_pages.json`): Callings 7,020 against 1,021, Living 3,902 against 915, Sieges 3,136 against 680, War 2,819 against 1,450, Orders 2,786 against 586, Fields 2,764 against 719, Building 2,637 against 539, Animals 2,283 against 559, Realms 1,947 against 855, Water 1,957 against 804, March 1,929 against 1,174, Gifts 1,690 against 823, Aims 1,711 against 1,126, Creatures 1,626 against 447, Your path 1,996 before its own new shape against 667 (before this work: Callings 6,831, Living 3,839, Sieges 2,605, Orders 2,723, Building 2,574, the realms 21 px wider than the sheet at 360x640). **The field notes' page** (`chronNotes`, `chronAdd('notes', ...)`): every note seen, the newest first, each folded under its title; a note shrunk to its title on a small screen opens it, itself first and open (`G2.noteFocus`, from the note's click in `showNextNote`). **Notes for what came since** (`G2_NOTES`, added only where no module has the id: an id given twice replaced the other's note): `hall` (it was the hall's toast, 04k_hall.js), `farmers`, `ripe`, `herb` (the farmers' toasts, 08h_farming.js `farmNotices`), `scaffold` (a scaffold's ladder or a mason near), `setwall` (a brand's prompt to set timber alight), `battered` (a house cracked or holed by stones near you), `renew` (a priest's Renewal on itself), `ward` (a mage's Arcane Ward up), `charge` (a warrior's rush), and why something happened to you in a fight: `dazzle`, `wounded`, `chilled`; the lakes' note (`lakeice`) waits for ice near you (`g2LakeIceOk`, set each frame: 05k_seasons.js gives the note at boot, replacing the one 10e_guide.js had guarded, and queues it when ice lies anywhere, so a player in Ériu or Logres in spring was told of the ice); a garrison near you running to a fire is told as it happens (a toast, once in 90 s); their watches in `GUIDE_WATCH`, the slower looks once a second (`g2Scan`: `G2.near`); a help line, a Sieges section (scaffolds and masons, stones on houses) and loading tips. **A place or a pointer for the steps done anywhere** (`g2MarkOf`, asked first by `guideMarkOf`): the late levels a champion of your level, else a fight of your realm's warbands, else the keep it fights for (`g2LevelMark`); a command below 20 and realm rank 3 a war table of your realm's (`g2WarTable`), from then none; the realm's gift before it opens where to grow, then none; a masterwork what you lack first (a champion's trophies, the Barrow's grave-iron, Cinderwing's scales), then its station (`g2MasterMark`); a campaign the keep your realm fights for. With no place, the button that does it glows (`g2Pointer`, `.g2-point`: the map button for an order, the gift's buttons) and the tracker says how (`g2PointerLine`). **The war panel's line for your aim** (`guideWarAimHTML`, from `updateWarPanel`): your path's next step while it is a deed of the war, else your realm rank (or the first aim) with a meter; a click on the line (on a touch screen a tap anywhere on the panel) opens it in the Chronicle. **No tap lost on the HUD** (`g2TapHeld`, asked first by `hudLayout`): a touch's click comes after the finger lifts (0.1 s and more) and lands on whatever is under it then; the layout pass moved a panel under a finger now and then, so it waits from the finger coming down on the HUD until its click lands (or 1.2 s after it lifts with no click). Why `touch_flows.py`'s companion bar failed (three runs in five on version 14's builds, and here twice): the bar, placed below other panels in the column of an upright phone, moved 18 to 24 px up and down every few seconds as a panel above it gained and lost a line, and the page's own frames ran a layout pass between the test's measure and its touch, so the finger came down on the button below Stay: Call, which sets Follow (logged: Stay measured at 484, at 460 when the finger came down, the click on Call); the hold was then let go by the late click of the flow before (a tap on the pad's Use). Now the layout holds from a finger coming down on a panel until that press's own click (`G2.tap.pending`: any other click leaves the hold alone; keeping a moved panel where it stood against small moves was tried and set aside: the layout came to depend on its history, and a panel that fitted in one pass waited out of sight in the next), and `touch_flows.py`'s tap waits for the last tap's click before it measures, holds the layout until its own touch, and reads the world only once its click has landed. Test hooks `__hm.guide2` |
| `11_audio.js` | Synthesised sound effects and ambience (Web Audio) |
| `11b_music.js` | The score: an original procedural score synthesised in Web Audio (no samples, no borrowed tunes). Each mood (title, the three homelands, frontier, battle with layers that follow the fighting, the Barrow, night) is a small generative piece: voice-led chords on weighted progressions in the mood's mode, melodies grown from motifs that are stated, sequenced, varied and cadenced, a new texture every eight bars; stingers for a level, a keep or hallow won or lost, victory and defeat. Hooks (marked [music]): `updateAudio` and `sfx` in 11_audio.js, the settings menu (10_ui.js) and save (12_save.js), `campaignVictory` (08g_campaign.js); `tools/tests/music.py` renders moods offline |
| `12_save.js` | Saves: `claude.use('db')` document `data/users/<uid>/save`, falling back to localStorage `hallowmarch.save.v1`; `validSave()` sanitises |
| `12b_worldstate.js` | The world remembers: `landSave()` writes `world.land` (felled trees, logs, stumps and saplings with their clocks; the nature map as 4-bit run-length tiles; burning grass; craters as height deltas in cm; burning, ruined and rebuilding houses; worn, breached and repairing walls; firewood piles) and `landApply()` restores it at boot. Also: loot settles when the ground moves under it, and the woodcutter's axe is in hand while chopping (`updateLand`) |
| `13_main.js` | Boot, `startGame`, `simStep(dt)` (fixed step), `tick(dt)` (frame), quality switching, `__hm` |
| `10p_hud.js` | The HUD on every screen. `hudLayout` (from `tick`, only when a panel shows, hides or changes size): the frames, the minimap and its buttons, the phone pad with its round buttons, the stick, the hotbar and the log stay put; the builder's bar, the target frame, the class panel, the companion bar, the war panel, the plant card, the path tracker, the bounty and the toasts keep their own place when it is free, else take the first free place in the column their screen prefers (`HUD_PREF`: an upright phone, a phone on its side, a desk), shrink to their essentials (`hl-compact`) or, on a touch screen, wait out of sight (`hl-off`) until there is room; the prompt and the cast bar rise above what they would cover. On a desk the log stops short of the hotbar. [guide] While a field note shows, the banner lane comes before the panels that stay on a phone (`HUD_ORDER_NOTE`, `HUD_ORDER_NOTE_WIDE`); older banners wait (`hl-old`) when the lane will not fit, and the lane shrinks a note to its title (`hl-compact`) when even the newest will not. `npSeen`: a name shows only when no wall, closed door or stone stands between the camera and the body (the Barrow keeps its own rule). [guide2] The pass waits while a finger is on a panel it moves until that press's click lands (`g2TapHeld`, 10f_guide2.js). Test hooks `__hm.hud` |
| `13p_perf.js` | Measuring. Under a test driver every mesh remembers the function that added it to the scene (`userData.perfFrom`); `__hm.perf.frame()` renders one frame counting draw calls and triangles by what drew them and by pass; `perf.timers()` wraps every update and draw function for JS time (`perf.times()`); `perf.boot()` gives the boot's steps and the heap |
| `13q_lod.js` | Level of detail. Terrain chunks at full, half and quarter detail by distance, always with full-detail rims (neighbours never crack; craters show at every level); far chunks drawn four at a time (`terrainBlockGeo`, rebuilt after a crater). Far tree chunks swap to the far model (`treeGeometry(kind, 1)`) and, farther, to the crown's big lobes only (`treeGeometry(kind, 2)`, beyond `TREE_FAR2_D`: 200 m on medium, 64 m on low); on medium and low the chunks round the camera are split tree by tree (`treeSplit`): their trees go into three bands per kind, one draw each (`treeBandsFill`, [motion] version 10): the full model within 60 / 36 m, the far model to `TREE_FAR2_D`, the lobes beyond, each tree culled against the view (with a margin for the camera's turn) and sorted again when the camera moves 3 m or turns 5 degrees, the quality changes or a tree falls or grows; the near band's shadows come from a copy with every near tree all round (band 3, on a layer the camera does not draw); trees the near-tree set draws in full (04c_trees.js) are left out; far chunks cast no shadow (they lie outside the shadow box). Rocks beyond reach, the far glass of towns and halls and the furrowed soil of far fields are not drawn on medium and low. People beyond `HUMAN_FAR` (90 / 60 / 38 m) go in a third crowd with the farthest model (`buildHumanoid(2)`, `crowdHumanFar`, no shadow). `updateLod` from `tick`. The phone's quality (`phoneQualityPick`: a phone starts at low, a big tablet at medium; a saved setting left on auto keeps the pick). Test hooks `__hm.lod` |
| `13s_footlock.js` | [polish] Planted feet. The walk shaders put a foot where the gait says for its phase, which keeps it planted only while the body goes straight along its facing; here each foot is followed on the CPU (`footLockStep`, from the end of `updateAnim`): when it comes down its place on the ground is kept (taken where the body is next drawn, `footLockSlot`: the player moves after its gait steps), and while it bears weight the shaders get how far that place lies from where the gait would put it, along the body and across it (metres in the body's frame). In the air the difference eases to where the coming stance should start (half of what the body's own motion will move the foot in the body's frame over a stance: a body going sideways steps out to that side), so nothing jumps. Beyond 0.42 m a planted foot gives way and is dragged (a long shove; [motion3] it takes a quick step instead, below); a jump (a teleport) plants it afresh. People's two feet and a beast's four (and the horse under a rider: `e._flH`, from `e.mountAnim`), rows of `FOOT_LOCK` (05a_rig.js: a 4 x 256 float texture; [motion3] 4 x 1024) filled each frame by `drawActors` (`footLockBegin`, `footLockSlot`, `footLockEnd`). Each foot's floor (`flFloor`: on a flight the tread, `floorTreadAt`) goes in the row's third channel as its height over the body's (a beast's less what its tilt with the slope gives it), the fourth is 2 where a foot stands on a tread (level there; [motion3] now 10, or 20 on a tread, plus half the turn that keeps a person's planted foot on its heading). The JS mirrors of the gaits (`flHumanGait`, `flBeastGait`) must agree with `HUMAN_GAIT` and `bbGait`. Test hooks `__hm.footLock`. [motion3] (version 12) Bodies within `FL_NEAR` (75 m) of the camera are followed and get a row, all of them (`FOOT_LOCK.H` 1024: version 11 held 256 a frame and the rest of a crowd walked unlocked); farther off nothing is followed (`footLockStep` returns at once, so a big battle costs less than before). Across, a stance is kept within what the leg reaches (`lo`/`hi`: in under the body 0.12 m past a person's own line or 0.85 of a beast's way to the middle, out to 0.45): a foot that will travel in comes down farther out, one that travels out as far in as it may. A stance is placed `FL_MARGIN` (1.5 cm) inside that reach, and a person's with room for the foot to stand still on the ground as it comes down and as it rises while the body moves on (`pre`, `post`: 12 and 15% of a swing). A planted person's foot is held in under the body to `FL_HOLD_IN` (0.25 m past its own line, the legs crossing, as when the body turns hard over it; the shaders' limit too). Past the hold by `FL_GIVE` (4 mm: the shaders hold a foot across only that far, so one held past it was dragged), or about to be by the next step, or beyond `FL_MAX` (0.42 m), a planted foot takes a quick step (`o.hop`: lifted `FL_HOP_H` 7 cm and set down again where the rest of the stance can be spent, over `FL_HOP_T` 0.16 s, drawn from the place it leaves on the ground, `o.hwx`, `o.hwz`) instead of being dragged (a 92-degree cut at a sprint slid it 10 cm); only past `FL_JUMP` (1.5 m: the body was set down somewhere new) is it planted afresh. A swing that leaves from a place past the hold is lifted clear at once (`clear`), and a landing place is kept within it. The two ends of a swing are places on the ground, not on the body: where the foot lifted off (`o.ax`, `o.az`: where it was last drawn, kept by `footLockSlot`) and where it will come down (`o.lwx`, `o.lwz`: where the coming stance should start, from where the body will be by then), both seen again from where the body is drawn; a person's foot goes from the one to the other over the ground (along and across, `o.k` = `_flSS(0.12, 0.88, w)`: it rises in place and comes down in place), a beast's across only (along, its own swing: the vault reads it), and it is planted where the swing held it (`o.land`). A person's planted foot keeps its own heading (`o.yw`, `o.yo`, in the fourth channel) while the body turns over it, and comes round to the body's in the air or in a quick step. A person's foot may stand on a stair's tread up to `FL_UP` (0.8 m) over the body (other floors 0.6, as before; up a steep flight a foot lands 0.5 to 0.75 m over the body, and a tread past 0.6 was passed over: the foot stood 27 cm into the riser), and its height is let up to there (0.45 before). A swinging foot's floors (`o.hT` where it will land, `o.hN` under it now and 0.12 and 0.24 m ahead for a person) are looked for in `footLockSlot`, where the body is drawn, from the body-frame places `footLockFeet` leaves (`o.lT`, `o.lN`), and the landing by where the body will be by then, its climb too (`e._flVy`): looked for a step behind and where the body was, they were a tread short up a flight at a run and the foot came up into the next riser [motion4] (version 14) What the leg cannot draw is lifted, never dragged: where the body is drawn (`footLockSlot`, after the player has moved and turned), a person's planted foot that the hip's swing cannot reach across after the lean (`flCanReach`) or that would have to turn past `FL_YAW` (0.9 rad) to keep its heading, and a quick step still in its first frame there, start a quick step at `FL_HOP_LIFT` (clear of the ground at once); a quick step rises by `flHopH` (`FL_HOP_POW` 0.35: 4.7 cm in its first frame, it was 4.0); `footLockFeet` looks for the planted place where the body's turn as well as its motion will carry it by the next step (`e._flDy`). A person's swing keeps the place it comes down on and its heading in the world from `FL_LATCH_W` (0.85) of the swing on (`o.latch`, `o.ywL`: worked out afresh each step, they swung round with a body turning 0.2 rad a frame, and the foot came in over the ground); where the leg could not reach that place the foot is held `FL_AIR_H` over the ground (`o.air`, with the gait's own lift: `flHumanLift`) and comes down by a quick step from the air, and a landing refused at touchdown is taken the same way. A quick step goes over the floors on its way: from the floor it left to the floor where it will come down, never under the floor beneath it now (on a flight it was carried at the height it left onto the next tread). A body that halts (its gait's phase stops) keeps a foot in the air where it was drawn and sets it down there (`o.frozen`; if the gait goes on, the foot swings on from where it was held). On a ladder the lock stands down (the rungs hold the feet: `ladderLegs`); a Charge's rush runs with its feet held (`rush`). While a boat is shoved off (action 19) each foot's floor is the one under it on its way into the stance (`o.dy`). A sharp cut at a sprint slid a planted foot 2 to 24 cm at one cut in five across the stride; now 0.6 cm at most in 130 cuts (`gaits.py` cuts). [motion5] (version 16) `flBeastGait` follows the longer gallop and trot (`dG`, `gp`, `gq`, the hind feet's `hb`); `footLockBegin` brings the rubble's height maps up to date (`rubHfSync`, 13y_motion5.js) and `floorTreadAt` asks `rubbleFootFloor` first: a foot on a fallen tower's or house's rubble stands on the stone drawn under it, not the ramp the body walks. |

| `13r_motion.js` | [motion] How bodies move, measured (tests), and `humanSlopeYaw` (in play: the floor's slope and the lean into a turn for the walk shader, from `drawActors`). The probe draws a crowd once more as points with its own vertex shader (its `main` renamed and wrapped) under a camera whose view and projection are the identity, so what the shader writes is the world position; each chosen vertex (found by its rest position) of each chosen instance (found by its place) lands on its own texel of a small float target that is read back (`mprobeRun`). `motionParts`: the soles of a person's feet, the knees and the top of the head, a beast's four hooves or paws, a bird's toes and wing tips. `motionRun(ents, sec)` drives bodies, steps the world at 60 Hz and looks at them each step (`motionDraw` fills the crowds as a frame would); `motionMeasure` gives the slide of a planted foot (cm), its slip as a share of the body's speed, how high a swinging foot rises, where the lowest foot is over the floor (`floorAt`) and the steps a second. `motionDragon(how, sec)` flies Cinderwing through its circuit, a take-off or a landing and reads its bones directly (it is skinned on the CPU). Test hooks `__hm.motion`; `tools/tests/motion.py` [motion4] `motionParts` gives a person's hands too (`hands`: a fist's own points, none another vertex shares: the probe finds a vertex by its rest place, and a held grip drew over the fist's). [motion5] A bird's `head` (the tip of its beak) and a person's `tool` (a mallet's head, bit 22, point by point). [motion5] A person's `soles` (the five sole points of each foot one by one: the point a foot stands on as it rolls, motion5.py's heel). |

### Conventions worth knowing

**World**
- y is up. The map is a square centred on the origin.
- Each realm sits on a bearing: Logres 150°, Ériu 270°, Thule 30°. `rp(R, along, lat)` places a point on a realm's axis, measured from the centre.
- Per realm: two keeps at `along` 118 (lateral ±64), a temple at 170, the muster at 214 and the town at 378.
- The frontier is inside `R_RING`.
- The Barrow's dungeon lives off-map at x > 1000. `heightAt`, `isWalkable` and `zoneAt` defer to its grid there.

**Materials**
- Every material is a `THREE.ShaderMaterial` made with `mat()`. All of them share the live uniforms in `U`: time, sun, fog, wetness, snow, night and so on.
- Weather and time of day therefore reach every shader through `U`. New shaders should read the same uniforms.
- Weather on a surface goes through the `GLSL_WEATHER` chunk in `02_render.js` (`wetAlbedo`, `wetSheen`, `rivulets`, `snowAmount`, `snowAlbedo`, `snowSparkle`), called with `uWet` and `uSnowCover` scaled by how sheltered the surface is; pass `gPL` as the heat so snow melts by fires.
- Snow lies where it is cold (the seasons, `05k_seasons.js`): `uSnowCover` is the snow lying at the camera (or 0.02 when snow lies only elsewhere in view), so CPU readers (grazing, survival's cold) get the local amount; a shader that should show snow lying elsewhere multiplies it by `snowHere(wp)` from `GLSL_SEASON` (terrain, grass, trees, buildings and houses, rocks, ice do). A shader that does not still shows the camera's own snow.
- The year on a surface: `GLSL_SEASON` (02d_season.js). Turf and blades call `seasonGrass`, broadleaf foliage `seasonLeaf` and drops its cards by `seasonFallen` (bare cards show `twigCard`), flowers fade by `uSeasonC.x`; pines (`uNeedle`, `uDecid` 0) stay green and carry snow. Anything new that grows should read the same uniforms. `uRain` is the rain falling right now (rings on the water). Characters take their own amounts from the `iWear` attribute; beasts from `iColB.w` (the hit flash in the fraction, wet and snow in whole sixteenths above it: `beastWx` in `05h_wear.js`, which now soaks and dries beasts as well).
- World maps every shader can read through `GLSL_WORLD` (built on the first frame by `updateCanopy` in `03_terrain.js`, 2 m texels unless noted):
  - `uCanopy`: rgb are the meadow's patches (`meadowPatch`: dry straw, lush clover, tall stands; ground, blades and flowers all use them), alpha is the tree-crown cover (`canopyAt`). The cover follows felled, burning, leafless and regrowing trees every 1.5 s. Ground and grass under crowns stay drier in rain, gather less snow and lie in a little shade.
  - `uGroundAO` (1 m): contact shade on the ground at the foot of walls, houses, gates, fences, props and posts, baked from `COLL` (`groundAO`); [look2] its second channel the towns' footpaths (`groundPath`, 13u_look2.js).
  - Test hooks: `__hm.visual.canopy(x, z)`, `.meadow(x, z)`, `.groundAO(x, z)`, `.refresh()`.
- `meadowHeight` sets the turf height (short sward, taller stands, long in fields, trimmed by roads); flower heads ride on it. `GRASS_SEGS` gives each quality its bends per blade layer: medium and low spend their vertices on more, simpler blades.
- Fog is exponential height fog (`applyFog`): as thick as before at the camera's height, thinner up the slopes.
- The final composite grades by time of day: the `gSat` (vibrance), `gCon` (contrast about the mid tones) and `gWarm` keys in `TOD_KEYS`.
- [look] The cover map is also how trodden the ground is: under the solid threshold (0.3) a value shortens the blades and the flowers' stems (`1 - 0.72 * smoothstep(0.03, 0.27, cover)`: a town's green is 0.25, a third of the meadow's height), from 0.3 nothing grows. Anything that wants short turf writes a value under 77 of 255 (and never lowers a texel: solid stays 255); the water is covered (200) once the herbs are placed, so no blade or flower stands in it; the map's own bilinear ramp gives every wall and post a short fringe at its foot. CPU readers keep their threshold of 100 (`tillWhy`, the herbs' placement).
- [look] No blade stands on rock: blades fade out where the rock mask is 0.6 to 0.9 (they stood straight out of the cliff faces as dark spikes).
- [look] Foliage near the camera or between it and the player (`viewBlock`) parts by whole leaf cards (the near trees) and by clumps of the crown in the world (`vnoise` of the world position: the hearts and the chunk trees' lobes), never through a screen-door pattern (a 4 x 4 Bayer of dots read as dithered green blobs against the sky). [look2] The hand-over between the near and the far tree sets does the same (`GLSL_TREE_HAND`, 04_props.js: `treeNearHidden`, `treeFarHidden`): across the 3.5 m band at the near trees' reach the near tree's leaf and needle cards come in whole, card by card (by their seed), its heart and the far crown by clumps of one field in the world (`treeClumpK`: where one is drawn the other is not), and the trunk changes over at the band's middle. Only the bark's part of the parting still dithers.
- [look2] A crown drawn as the chunk trees' lobes near the camera (every crown within 30 m at low, which draws no near trees) is drawn leaf by leaf (`treeLeafAt`, `GLSL_TREE_HAND`): cells of a jittered grid on the plane the face looks along, each an elongated leaf turned its own way with its own shade and tilt, dark gaps between them, the outline broken leaf by leaf; faded out by 30 m and once a leaf would be under about three pixels. A noise of `(x + y, z + y)` of a 3D point is constant along a line: on a round lobe it streaks diagonally (the lobes' clumps were); cross two planes instead.
- [look2] Fine relief bumped through the screen's derivatives (`bumpN`) is shaded in 2 x 2 blocks, so any detail bumped that way fades before it is under about five pixels (the roads' pebbles and grit, `pebF`, `gritF` in the terrain shader: their outlines broke into a network of dark angular cracks, and the roads read as crazy paving in a phone's or a small window's view).
- [look2] Trodden ground under snow (where the road channel `A.r` says the ground is trodden: roads, plazas, paths) is packed grey slush with darker tracks along it, and the earth that shows is wet and grey; the snow takes only a fifth of what it did off a road (`rdS` in the terrain shader). The road showed through the snow as dark brown mud.
- [look] A house wall (`holedWall`, 04b_town.js) is one slab on a grid cut at every opening's edges, sills and heads: each solid cell gives its front and back faces and only the faces that are really there are closed (the openings' reveals, the wall's ends, top and foot). Built of boxes side by side it had T-junctions (a piece over a door ending on the middle of the tall piece's edge beside it; their vertex shades, linear between each box's own corners, disagreed too) and end faces inside the wall flush with the front, which won MSAA samples along the seams at high: a thin light line ran up each side of every door and window to the eaves. Anything else built of boxes side by side in one plane has the same fault.
- [look] What is drawn by distance from the camera is refreshed at once when the camera jumps more than 20 m (a journey, a release, a portal, a fixed view), not on its next tick: the forage plants (`drawForage`, every 0.4 s: an orchard's apple trees were missing and the old place's bushes drawn), the far glass of towns and halls and the fields' soil (`lodFar`, every 20 frames). The trees, the near trees, the rocks, the crags, the farm and the bands already were.
- [look] Thatch is combed down the slope: its strands are laid across the axis the face looks along (`thw` from the normal's x and z), not summed over both axes (a plaid weave on a round house's lining), and its finest strands fade by the pixel's size.

**Water**
- `waterAt(x, z)` gives `{ y, depth, fx, fz }` (surface, depth over the ground, flow in m/s) or null. Lakes stand at `WATER_Y`; rivers fall along their course; ponds stand in their hollows at their own heights. Use it, never `WATER_Y`, to ask about water.
- `W.walk` is 0 walkable, 1 blocked, 2 deep water. `isWalkable` stays `=== 0`; `stepTo` uses `swimPassable` for a body with `e.swimOK` (the player always; people and beasts chasing something or getting out; never townsfolk or posted guards).
- A body in water has `e.wd` (depth), `e.wadeK`, `e.swim`, `e.swimK` (0..1, for the shaders), `e.wl` (how high it is soaked). `groundY` and the player float swimmers through `waterFloorY`. The actor shaders get swimming as a negative `iAnim.w` (death stays positive) and the soaked height in whole 5 cm steps of `iWear.x` (the fraction is the wetness).
- Anything that should ring the water calls `rippleDrop(x, z, r, depth)` or `splashAt(x, z, size)`; spells that leave an AoE mark on water splash by themselves.
- Drinking: at any lake, pond or river the water module offers the drink and survival's `drinkAt(spot, kind)` does it (you drink your fill and every `waterskin_empty` becomes a `water`). Drinking a `water` from the bags, or using one in a recipe, gives back a `waterskin_empty`. Dirty gear is washed first unless you are really thirsty; with a rod and no thirst, fishing comes first.
- A mage's ice (`freezeWater`, 07b_classes.js) lies at its water's own surface (`s.wy`) as a floor; `onIce` keeps bodies on it out of the water, and swimmers caught where it forms are lifted onto it.
- Winter ice on the lakes and ponds (05k_seasons.js): `seasonIceY(x, z)` is the ice's top where it carries a body (0.3 thick or more); `floorAt`, `onIce` and `isWalkable` ask it (check `SICE.on` first, it is cheap). No splashes or leaping fish on the ice; fishing through it cuts a hole. The rivers never freeze. [water2] A hole cut in the ice (`DIVE.holes`) is open water: `seasonIceY` is null there; `seasonIceLift` leaves divers under the ice.
- [water2] Under the water: a diver has `e.dive` (`t`, `up`, `pitch`) and moves itself (09_player.js returns early through `water2Steer`); `e.swim` stays true (no casting), `updateWater` leaves its strokes and surface splashes alone. The eye under the water (`DIVE.under.on`, the camera below the surface at it) sets `U.uUnder.x`: `applyFog` then fogs anything below the surface with `underFog` and anything above it by the water its ray passes through before it leaves the water (`underFogD`; the sky does the same), and the water surface's back faces (it is double-sided now) draw the view up through it (the window, the silver beyond it, the ice from below), letting through unfogged what shows in the window (it is fogged already) and closing on the bank at the waterline. A shader that does not use `applyFog` is not fogged under the water. A body standing more than 0.1 m above the water's surface (a jetty's deck, the air) is not in the water.
- [water2] In a boat: `e.boat` (the boat), `e.seat` (the thwart: drawn seated), `waterFloorY` gives the seat (`boatSeatY`), `updateWater` skips the body, NPC passengers have `fixedY`. Boats keep off land, ice and jetties by `boatHit`. Bodies keep off boats through `boatShove` in `stepTo` (boats move, so they are not in `COLL`).

**Mountains** ([land], 03c_land.js)
- [heights] Everything is placed on version 10's ground, then the whole mountain is shaped (`mtnHeights`, 03d_heights.js): `mtnShape` (version 10) still only touches mountain texels with no walkable texel within two, so the placements in `buildWorld` (trees, rocks, camps) and after it (forage, herbs, workstations, boats, the orchard) read exactly the heights, masks and cover they did and come out where they were (the saved land names trees and plants by number). `mountains.py` checks the land before the shaping (`HTS.H0`/`walk0`) against version 9 (`tools/tests/mountains_base.json`, unchanged), and after it: what anyone could reach, the roads, camps, colliders, the trees there were and the rocks.
- What the shaping may change: heights on the mountains away from what is kept (roads and their verges, sites, camps, water, ponds' hollows, colliders, plots; a wall rising over a road or a site is shaped above its foot); the walk mask only where texels no one could reach turned too steep (blocked: about 4,600 texels on the faces and crests, of 56,600 shaped); the rock, grass and flower masks where the slopes changed; the heights of trees, rocks, plants and herbs (settled with their ground). It never blocks a texel anyone could reach, never opens a blocked one, never leaves a closed hollow on the shaped ground. Anything placed later on the mountains (a new kind of plant, a camp) should read the final ground, after `mtnHeights`; anything that must keep a placement made before it on version 10's ground goes into `htsKeep` or `objs`.
- [look] No shaped texel stands up out of all eight round it by more than 0.6 m (`HTS.despiked`, about 150 at boot, right after `htsApply`): a 2 m spike drew a smooth, sharp-faced pyramid seen from the slopes below it. Brought down to 0.6 m over the highest of them it stays the top, so no hollow forms and every slope only eases.
- The crags are placed once the shaping is done (`mtnHeights` calls `mtnPlaceCrags`; `buildWorld` no longer does), from the walk mask as it is then (`mtnFreeAgain`).
- Hardy trees are added after every tree there was (`HTS.trees0` is the first); anything that counts trees for a placement made before `mtnHeights` (forage's shade, the herbs' shade) never saw them.
- The pond fill dithers the heights it raises with a fixed hash (`hash2(k, 4111)` in 01b_water.js), not `Math.random`, so the land is the same on every boot.
- Anything new that draws rock should share the look: `landRockTone`, `landFacets`, `landStreaks` and the snow line (`landSnowLine` with `landSnowHold`) from `GLSL_LAND`.
- Cost (`perf.py`, 640x360, SwiftShader; triangles / calls, version 9 against version 10): the mountain view from the Logres orchard at low 700k / 122 against 696k / 123 (crags and reshaping, before the towers), medium 1.49M / 171 against 1.47M / 171, high 2.46M / 207 against 2.48M / 202; the keep courtyard at low 711k / 129 against 672-721k / 130 with the towers (their rooms are drawn only within 70 m). Totals move by about 60k between runs at low as the farm soil and the crowds come and go: compare the per-mesh lines, not only the totals.
- [heights] Cost of the whole mountain shaped (`perf.py render`, 640x360, SwiftShader; triangles / calls, version 10 against version 11): the one mesh that comes into new views is the crags' (`mk`: more crags, and the outcrops; +13k triangles and one draw at low in the forest view, two draws at high); the hardy trees join the chunk meshes that already draw pines or birches there (no draw added); the terrain is the same grid. At low, less the farm soil (61k and one draw, which came and went between the runs): town 652k / 104 against 644k / 105, keep 654k / 120 against 651k / 120, forest 695k / 164 against 698k / 164, the mountains from the orchard 673k / 120 against 647k / 119, river 625k / 134 against 626k / 134. Medium: keep 1.26M / 149 against 1.29M / 151, forest 1.50M / 204 against 1.50M / 204, mountains 1.44M / 166 against 1.42M / 164; high: keep 2.05M / 192 against 2.13M / 194 (more crags near the keep, and their shadows), forest 2.59M / 240 against 2.59M / 238, mountains 2.54M / 211 against 2.53M / 209.
- A pattern in a shader never multiplies a world position by something that varies across the ground, nor projects it on a direction that turns with the normal: hundreds of metres from the origin either one stripes the ground with moire a few centimetres apart (both were found up close in version 10). Warp the pattern's grid by an offset instead, and pick fixed axes (`landAlong`).

**Buildings**
- Buildings are merged geometry with a per-vertex material id (`MAT` in `04_props.js`: stone 0 … rock 15, drystone 16).
- The building shader branches on that id. To add a surface type, add an id and a shader branch.
- [look3] A scaffold is built with `look3Scaffold` (04d_look3.js): pass the standards' places along the face and the way to the wall at each, the lifts' heights, the ground (`gy`) and a `clear` test (`look3Clear`); then `look3ScaffoldWorld(W, S, [info])` makes its boards floors and its ladders climbable while `look3ScafSync(W, on)` says it stands (a scaffold made anew for each repair calls `look3ScaffoldDrop(W)` when it comes down: its floors leave `FLOOR` and its ladders `LOOK3S`). Stone, logs or anything else waiting at a scaffold's foot lie on the ground or on each other, never a step up in the air.
- [look4] A scaffold rises with the work: its standards from the start, each lift (ledgers, putlogs, boards, rail, braces and the ladder up to it) as the work comes up to it (`look4LiftStep`, 04d_look4.js; a castle section's, a tower's and a palisade gap's). A builder of a new scaffold that places things on its top lift places them on the highest lift that is up (`Math.min(lifts.length, LOOK4.lifts)`), and one that would rebuild its mesh outside look3's machinery keeps `look3ScaffoldWorld` from laying out its floors and ladders twice (`LOOK4.geoOnly`) and gives `look3Scaffold` the places its standards stood (`LOOK4.prev`, each run's `ok`): the clear test, asked again, refuses the places under the scaffold's own boards. A scaffold's ladder stands upright, its rungs `LADDER_AHEAD` in front of the line its climber climbs on and every 0.3 m from where the climb starts (`fy`: its own foot), up to a climber's reach (1.95 m) over the boards it leads to, through an opening left in the next lift's boards. A house being built back has a scaffold of its own (04j_rubble.js `houseScafLay`).
- [look3] A crack in coursed stone is drawn with `look3Crack(uv, k)` (the stone branch's `planarUV`), one in plaster, daub or timber with `look3Craze(p, k)` (04d_look3.js), on upright faces only. A line that follows the mortar joints exactly hides in them (the joints are dark): let it cut through some stones too, and give it a lit lip.

**Body actions** (`setAct(e, id, seconds)`; the humanoid shader's `atype`): 1 one-handed strike, 2 bow draw, 3 spellcasting, 4 a flinch, 5 using something (reach, pick, drink from a skin), 6 in the air, 7 whirlwind, 8 overhead smash, 9 leap strike, 10 guard, 11 parry, 12 kneel (07b_classes.js), 13 down on the haunches to reach the ground (picking, drinking at a lake; 09s_survival.js; [motion5] each foot where it stands, the legs reaching it wholly as the hips sink; a body falling from more than 0.9 m lands in it, 04m_ladders.js), 14 climbing a ladder (hand over hand, its cycle locked to the body's height: 04l_towers.js; [motion4] on the rungs' own cycle, the phase set by `towerLadderStep` to 2 pi for every 0.6 m from the ladder's foot, each foot on a rung and each hand round one: `ladderLegs`, `ladderHands`; [look2] what the hands carried hangs on the back meanwhile, in the shader: the shield over the cloak, a blade, mace or axe across it from the right shoulder, a staff across it, a bow over the quiver), 15 swimming under the water (in the swim block of the shader; the fraction is the body's lean, -1.2 rising .. 1.2 head down; 07d_dive.js), 16 at the oars (in the seated block; the fraction is the stroke; 07e_boats.js; [motion4] the hands on the grips where boatsDraw has the oars: `armIK` and the shoulder's swing, `uaLy`), 17 a game bird in the air (09v_birds.js: its progress is the wings' spread; birds only), 18 a blow from a ladder (held on the rungs as in 14, the right arm drawn back and cutting; 04m_ladders.js; [look2] the weapon in the right hand, the shield and the bow on the back), 19 shoving a boat off the shore (07e_boats.js: leaning in with both hands on the gunwale, the back leg driving, both feet flat; [polish]; [motion5] the hands on her gunwale by her end and following her down the beach for her first 0.3 m, the body bent as low as her gunwale lies: its height and her keel's offset come packed in the action's value, `motion5ActValue`), 21 beating out flames (04g_blaze.js: braced on bent knees and bent toward the fire, both arms swung up over the head and brought down hard in front, twice an action; what the hands carried slung on the back as on a ladder; [siege3]; [look4] a fire beater in the right fist, a wet leather flap on an ash pole, the part bit `WB.beater` set by drawActors while the action lasts), 23 hammering at a wall ([motion5]: the builders at a house's scaffold, 08f_townlife.js, and the masons, 13v_look3.js: the left hand braced on the wall, the right raising a mallet over the shoulder and bringing it down onto the wall before the chest at about 1.35 m, once in 1.05 s, the feet standing; what the hands carried slung on the back as on a ladder; a mallet in the right fist, `WB.hammer` (bit 22), while `e._hamUntil` is ahead: the work steps refresh it; the blow lands `HAMMER_REACH` before the body). Actions 7 and up own the whole body. Take the next free number for a new one.

**Characters**
- Characters are instanced crowds. The per-instance attributes are in `HUMAN_ATTRS`: `iPos`, `iAnim`, `iColA`, `iColB`, `iMisc`, `iStyle`. A person's `iPos.w` is the yaw plus whole turns that carry the floor's slope and the lean into a turn ([motion], `humanSlopeYaw`: n = slope steps + 41 x lean steps); `humanVS` takes them off (`yawR`) before its sine and cosine, so anything new that reads a person's `iPos.w` must do the same. A wild or farm beast's `iPos.w` carries whole turns for its lean (`beastLeanYaw`), taken off in `beastVS`; [polish] and the slope of the floor along it: k = lean steps (0.04 rad, -5..5) + 11 x slope steps (0.05 of rise a metre, -20..20, eased in `e._bslope`; within 70 m of the camera), and the horse under a rider the same, which the rider's shader reads too; game birds and hens get the plain yaw. People are drawn by three crowds by distance: `crowdHuman` (near, `HUMAN_LOD`), `crowdHumanLo` and `crowdHumanFar` (beyond `HUMAN_FAR`, 13q_lod.js).
- [look] A body in a room is lit by the room: the people's fragment shader includes `GLSL_ROOFS` and sets `gRoom` (declared in `GLSL_ACTOR_LIGHT`, 0 by default) from `underRoof(vW)`: no direct sun or moon, the sky's ambient at 0.6, no sky in `env`, and a warm fill (stronger at night). `underRoof` knows the 8 roofs nearest the camera (houses, built roofs, the towers' rooms: `houseRoofsForRain`), so a body in a far house is lit as before. A beast's shader leaves `gRoom` at 0.
- [look] Cloth over the legs (the bits `robe`, `tabard` and `skirt`, below the hips, not on the arms) is pushed out ahead of a leg that has come forward and behind one that has gone back (`humanVS`, after the legs' skinning: the leg's line at the vertex's height from `thL`/`thR` and the knees, plus 0.1 m, a tabard 0.125 so it stays over the robe), weighted by how near the vertex is to that leg across the body. The middle of a robe follows neither thigh, so a striding or guarding leg came through it from the hip to the boot. A new garment over the legs should carry one of these bits, and a layer further out more room.
- [polish] `iStyle.z` is 'female' (0 or 1) plus twice the body's row of planted feet (`FOOT_LOCK`, 13s_footlock.js: 0 is none; [motion3] up to 1024 rows); `humanVS` reads `mod(iStyle.z, 2.0)` for 'female'. A beast's `iColB.w` carries its row above its weather (+ 256 x row; hens and birds never get one, so their shaders read it as before).
- [look2] What a hand carries is authored in its fist (05a_rig.js: a blade's grip tilted 1.85 rad, a staff's 0.22, the shields and the bow at the left fist with their own offsets). On a ladder (actions 14 and 18) `humanVS` takes each such part out of the fist (that tilt undone) and lays it on the back, carried by the chest (bone 1): a new weapon, shield or carried thing (its bit) must join that list in the [look2] block before the skinning, or it stays held up in the hand off the rungs.
- `iColB.a` packs flags:

  | Bits | Meaning |
  |---|---|
  | 1 | bone |
  | 2 | troll |
  | 4 | hunched |
  | 8 | mounted or seated |
  | 4–6 | glow kind |
  | 7–8 | glow strength |

**Gaits** ([motion], version 10; measure any change with `tools/tests/motion.py`)
- The walk phase turns half a cycle per step, and the CPU (`updateAnim`: `humanStep`, `beastStride`) and the shaders must agree on the step length, or planted feet slide. A planted foot then moves back under the body exactly as fast as the body goes forward, at every speed. A person's swinging foot leaves the ground and meets it again already moving back under the body at half that pace (`footPath` in 05a_rig.js): a plain smoothstep brought it down moving with the body, and it skated in to land (a sprinter's planted foot now moves under 1 cm). The beasts keep the smoothstep: the same curve sent a galloping horse's hooves past their reach and into the ground.
- People: below about 1 m/s the steps shorten rather than slow (`humanStep` keeps at least 1.6 steps a second; the shader's and 07_entities.js's must stay the same function). The ankles keep a planted foot flat on the ground; in the air it rolls off the toes and reaches heel first (not in the air, swimming, mounted or dying; a kneeling back foot keeps its toes tucked). Turning on the spot, a person steps round (`e.anim.turn`, sent to the shader as a negative walk amount by `drawActors`). On stairs, ramps and hillsides each foot comes down on the floor's slope along the body (`humanSlopeYaw`, 13r_motion.js, samples `floorAt` 0.35 m ahead and behind; a drop beyond a step is no slope), the hips sink a little so the lower foot reaches, the chest leans into a climb, and a planted foot pitches part way with the slope. A runner leans into a turn from the feet (`e.anim.lean`, `updateAnim`: the turn's pull, up to about 14 degrees); so do beasts and a horse with its rider (up to about 11 degrees, the rider about the horse's feet: `beastLeanYaw`). Kneeling, crouching, the guard and the leap's landing set the planted feet by IK (`legIK`), so they stand on the ground instead of sinking into it.
- Beasts (wild and farm, `beastVS`): each leg reaches its foot by two-bone IK from the top joint through the middle one (`beastLegIK`, the feet's rest places in the uniforms `uFF`/`uBF`), so a planted foot stays down all through the stance. `beastStride(kind, amt, sc)` holds the pace (half cycles a second) between about 1.6 and 4 at a horse's leg length (quicker for shorter legs: `sqrt(1.06 / leg)`), in the world at the beast's scale; the shader does the same from `uGait` and the scale in `iColA.w`. A bird or hen without a `front` leg gives its leg length as `BEASTS[kind].legH`.
- [polish] Beasts vault over their legs (`bbVault`, beastBodyGLSL in 05c_beasts.js): the forequarters and the hindquarters come down as far as their planted legs need, so a planted foot reaches the ground for its whole stance (the horse's front legs are straight at rest: before, the feet floated at the ends of a stride and the body's flex, pitch and bob moved them, 8 cm a stride at a gallop, 23 cm at a bear's trot). Every turn of the body is undone along the leg, the hoof or paw stays level, and its swing lands and lifts steeply while still going back under the body. A swinging leg asks the vault for what its foot would need on the ground below it, not raised (a raised foot asked less, the body rose as a leg lifted, and the straight leg swung its foot forward along the ground). Anything new that moves a beast's body (a pose, an action) should go into the chain `beastVS` undoes (`tg` in the leg block), or the feet move with it.
- [polish] Planted feet are held where they came down by the foot lock (13s_footlock.js), whatever the body does over them: a shove, sliding along a wall, a sharp cut at a sprint, turning on the spot. Each comes down on the floor under it (`flFloor`: a stair's tread, a deck, a bump; a drop of more than 0.6 m is no floor for it; on a castle's flight the tread, `floorTreadAt`, not the ramp the flight's floor is, which joins the treads' back edges, and a person's foot stands level there: `w` 2 in its row; a person's foot takes the highest floor under its sole, heel to toe, `FL_SOLE`: by a riser it stands on the next tread with its heel out over the edge, never with its toes in the stone), the leg reaching down or up for it (a person's at most 0.2 m down: a foot left far below as the body climbs is drawn up), and in the air it passes over what lies under its path (a stair's nosing) on its way to the next. A beast stands tilted with the slope along it (`beastLeanYaw`) and each foot takes what the tilt leaves of its own floor's height. A new gait or a change to `HUMAN_GAIT` or `bbGait` must be mirrored in `flHumanGait` / `flBeastGait`, or the lock pulls the feet to the wrong places.
- [motion3] (version 12) No foot moves over the ground while it is near it. A beast's foot rises its first 5.5 cm still standing where it stood and comes down its last ones onto its place (`BB_CLEAR`, `BB_LIFT` in 05c_beasts.js: within the stance's ends, so the lock holds it there); a person's swing leaves the ground at half the stance's pace and meets it at the whole pace, standing still over the ground, its last centimetres steep (`footPath`: at half the pace a runner's foot came in at a third of the body's speed); near the camera the lock goes further: a person's foot rises in place and comes down in place, its two ends places on the ground (13s_footlock.js), its toes pointing down no faster than it rises and turning about the toes (05a_rig.js), its heading kept while planted. A beast's walk is the lateral sequence (hind left, fore left, hind right, fore right), its trot diagonal pairs, its gallop hind right, hind left, fore right (before the hind left lifts), fore left, then one gathered leap. Up or down a steep flight a person's steps are no longer than 0.9 m (`humanStepCap`, the same in the shader, `updateAnim` and `flHumanGait`, by the slope the shader is given: `humanSlopeQ`). Anything new that moves a foot sideways should turn the leg so the joint above the part that hangs straight is over the foot (`abd`, `abL`), not the top of the leg, by the depth the leg really reaches; anything new that turns a whole person (a lean, a tilt) after the legs are posed must set the ankles first where it will carry them (`lnK`) and undo it at the ankle, or the planted feet move with it.
- [motion4] (version 14) A planted foot the leg can no longer draw where it stands is lifted in that very frame, not dragged: `footLockSlot` asks `flCanReach` (across, after the lean: humanVS's `lkL.x` within the hip's swing) and the heading (`FL_YAW`, 0.9 rad) where the body is drawn, after the player has moved and turned, and starts a quick step at `FL_HOP_LIFT` (already clear of the ground); a quick step rises `FL_HOP_POW` (0.35) of the way at once (4.7 cm in its first frame); the lock's own look ahead (`footLockFeet`) carries the planted place by the body's turn too (`e._flDy`). A person's swinging foot keeps where it comes down and its heading in the world from its last frames in the air (`FL_LATCH_W`, `o.latch`, `o.ywL`); a landing the leg cannot reach is taken in the air (`o.air`: held `FL_AIR_H` over the ground with the gait's own lift, `flHumanLift`, then a quick step). A body that halts between two steps (its gait no longer turns over) keeps its foot in the air where it was drawn and sets it down there (`o.frozen`). Anything new that moves a body at once (a snap to a place, a turn of more than the leg's reach in a frame) is covered by the draw-time lift; anything that shifts the body over its feet in a pose (`stepZ`, a lunge) should reach the feet by IK from where the gait and the lock have them (`fL.x - stepZ`), not pose the legs by fixed angles: actions 1, 4, 5, 8 and 11 do so now, and the boat's shove (19) steps its feet into the stance over the floor the lock gives under each one (`o.dy` while act 19 lasts).
- [motion4] A shader that reaches for something in the world (a rung, an oar's grip) works in the body's own size: divide the world's metres by the body's scale (`rsc0`), or a body of 0.92 reaches 14 cm short. `armIK(ty, tz)` (05a_rig.js) is the arm's two-bone IK in the body's own plane (the shoulder's pitch and the elbow's bend from the rest pose, the elbow down and back), reached for in the frame before the chest's lean (undo `chestX` about the spine first). A hand that must go across the body (toward the middle or out) swings the arm at the shoulder about the chest's upright (`uaLy`, `uaRy`, 0 unless an action sets them) and reaches in the arm's own plane: the shoulder's pitch and roll alone cannot carry a hand held at the shoulder's height across. The probe's hand (13r_motion.js: a fist's own points) lies 3 to 3.6 cm from `SK.fist` toward the wrist and behind the knuckles: reach that much beyond and above a grip or a rung.
- [motion5] (version 16) Birds and hens walk on two-bone legs by IK (`BIRD_LEG_GLSL`, 05j_birdlegs.js): a foot is planted for its stance and rises and comes down in place as a beast's does (they have no foot lock: `blGait`'s stride is `beastStride`'s, so the gait alone keeps them planted), the toes curl in the air, the body comes down over its planted legs (the vault), a hen's or a pheasant's head holds still in the air for most of each walking step; a new bird reaches its legs through `blLegPose` with its rest joints in `BIRD_LEGS`. The gallop and the trot stand longer on each foot (05c_beasts.js `bbGait`: the gallop's duty 0.3 of the stride, at most 0.58 of the leg's reach; a hind foot's stance set back under the body by `hb`), mirrored in `flBeastGait`. A person's foot rolls from heel to toe (05a_rig.js: the heel rises about the ball through the stance's end): measure a planted person's foot by the point it stands on (motion5.py's heel: its lowest sole point, frame to frame), since the sole's middle (motion.py's feet) now moves about 1 cm as the heel rises. A pose that sinks the hips over planted feet (a crouch, a landing) reaches the feet by IK wholly at every point of it: mixing the legs' angles with the gait's while the hips sink drove the feet into the ground.
- [motion5] An action's value (`animActValue`, iAnim.z: the action and its progress) can carry more in its fraction for the shader: the shove's (`motion5ActValue`) packs its progress in 256 steps with two more numbers in 18 bits (19 + n / 2^18 is exact in a float: an action under 32 takes five of its 24 bits); humanVS unpacks it for that action before anything reads `at`.
- GLSL: never `atan(y, x)` with a y that can be exactly zero (a stance foot's reach is +0.0 or -0.0 by the phase): SwiftShader (and maybe other drivers) gave pi for `atan(-0.0, x)`, and a standing person's planted leg pointed at the sky. Use the one-argument form with a positive denominator, or add a small offset to y (`bAng` in `beastVS`).

**Lights**
- Real point lights are capped at 12, 8 or 4 depending on quality, chosen each frame by `gatherLights`.
- `addFire(x, y, z, size, ghost, until, light)` takes an optional light reach `{r, k}`.

**Collision**
