- `COLL` holds boxes and circles in a spatial grid. The Barrow also uses it for line of sight.
- [land] A stone keep's curtain walls (their meshes and colliders) end inside its corner towers (`KEEP_WALL_END`, 01_world.js); the towers' old solid drum colliders (tag `tower`) are inactive (`c.hollow`) and the rings of `wall` boxes of 04l_towers.js stand in their place. A route point may carry its height and a ladder (`[lx, lz, ly, ladder]` in a post's route; `castleRouteWorld`).
- [walls] A castle's sections (`C.sections`) are the curtains' breachable stretches and then (version 12) its stone towers (`W.kind === 'tower'`, 04g_breach.js); code that walks the sections meets both (`castleCutUpload` gives the towers their own uniforms; the siege's plans and the bands' breaches leave the towers out). A hatch or a tower door with `gone` set stays down whatever its state (`updateTowers`).
- Siege engines are entities of kind `door` with `siegeEngine` set (not actors, so nothing auto-targets them; defenders and sallies go for them on purpose). `python3 tools/campaign_soak.py 60 low` runs an AI-only campaign and reports how each one ended.
- Colliders marked `vert` block only at their own height range (walls below their top, parapets only up on the walk). `stepTo` passes the body's height to `resolveCollisions` and asks `castleStepBlocked` about stair sides and raised edges.

**What flies** ([walls], version 12; `07g_walls.js`)
- Anything new that flies takes its step through `shotHit` (from where it was to where it is) and stops at what it returns (`R.t` along the step, the point, the face's normal, `R.mat`, `R.what`: the collider's tag, 'house', 'roof', 'floor' or 'ground'); `wallStrike(kind, R, ...)` does what an arrow or a spell does there. Pass `ox, oy, oz` (where it was loosed: the shooter's own embrasure) and `tgt` (the body aimed at: its crenel; a gate or an engine is struck as itself). An NPC that would shoot asks `wallsShotClear` first. An NPC with a bow, a wand or a staff weighs its foes by it too: `pickTarget` takes the first of its three best it can strike (with none clear, the first in sight as before), and a top threat behind a wall or a breastwork gives way to a foe it can strike (`pickTarget(e, r, true)`, in `thinkPosted` and the warbands' think); `wallsShotClear(e, t, arc, false)` counts those looks apart (`stats.picks`, `picksWalled`). A held shot moves the shooter (`fightTarget`): toward a foe on the ground (round the wall or the house), but away from one up behind a breastwork (to about 20 m, where its crenel shows, not under the wall); a wall archer steps up to the crenel facing its foe, along its own floor and within 2.4 m of its post (`wallsStepToCrenel`, from `thinkPosted`), and walks back to the post when the fight is over; a bow or a staff of a defending band does not take an engine through its own wall (`siegeFoeEngine`).
- A new collider gets the right material from its tag (`wallMatOf`; `c.wm` to set one), and a parapet built by `castleParapet` its crenels (`c.cren`). A new opening in a wall that a shot should pass needs its own rule in `wallColl` (as the slits, windows and crenels have).
- Trees, bushes and fire pits let shots by (the March's fights in the woods keep their pace); rocks, tents, fences' rails, furniture and props stop them.
- (version 13) The ground is no wall to a homing shot at a body: where its way runs into the ground (a crater's bowl, a low rise) it goes over, by the point 0.6 m above the highest ground in the way, when that is within 5 m of the line (`wallsGroundVia`; `wallsProj` sets `p.via` and `updateProjectiles` steers by it, then on to the mark), and `wallsShotClear` holds a shot only for a hill. A shot at a point (a volley), a gate or an engine still strikes the ground in its way.
- [siege2] (version 13) A held shot (`autoAttack`) goes through `wallsHeldShot` (07h_lines.js): another foe the shooter can strike from where it stands, else it holds its fire at that foe without looking again for a moment (`wallsHeld`, released when it or its foe moves) while `wallsSeekLine` (from `thinkPosted` and `fightTarget`) takes it to a place with a line (a crenel, a slit or an edge along its own floor; back from the wall or aside on the ground). A new shooter that should hold its fire the same way calls `wallsHeld` before looking (`wallsShotClear`). Arrows stuck in a door swing with it (`wallDoorRec`, `wallDoorPose`). Measured in a running war (`soak.py 10`, ten minutes; version 12 against 13): the share of the NPCs' looks before a shot that are held, 66% against 48% and 50% in two runs; shots loosed 1,188 against 1,360 and 1,047 (each run's war goes its own way: compare shares, not counts); of 569 held shots 11 took another foe, and 459 searches found a line 26 times (most held shots are at a foe up behind a breastwork from below, which no place on the ground shows, or at one below a wall archer's breastwork line). In `siege2.py`'s scenes the old way and the new: a wall archer with a foe close below off its tower's side did 1,105 harm against 2,039 (with the breastwork first in its search; 1,825 against 1,156 before), an attacking archer under a curtain 406-1,189 against 613-1,162 over six runs.
- Cost (`perf.py low medium high views=keep,battle`, against version 11's main on the same machine, 640x360, SwiftShader, loaded by other runs; triangles / draw calls): the keep courtyard low 757k / 131 against 768k / 133, medium 1.32M / 153 against 1.35M / 157, high 2.21M / 195 against 2.20M / 197; the battle low 667k / 110 against 672k / 110, medium 1.24M / 148 against 1.20M / 143, high 2.03M / 191 against 1.93M / 184 (the stuck arrows share the arrow mesh; a tower's rubble and scaffold are drawn only while it lies breached or is mended); programs 86 against 87 at medium and high. JS in the battle: the projectiles' step 0.14 against 0.36 ms (their swept tests), the NPCs' thinking the same within the noise (the steps as a whole moved by more than that between runs: other systems doubled and halved with the machine's load). A swept test costs 2-8 us in the open and 15-110 us along a keep's walls (`walls.py`'s cost check). The same war simulated in turns with the walls on and off (`WALLS.on`, four turns of 10 s each, the projectiles looking two steps ahead): 5.04 against 5.03 ms a step, with 1,300 to 6,200 tests in 10 s. In a running war (`soak.py 10`) the NPCs look before about 2,800 shots and hold about 2,100 of them in ten minutes.

**Floors**
- `groundY(e)` and the player use `floorAt(x, z, y)`: the highest walkable surface at or one step (0.62 m) above `y`, else the ground. Floors are boxes, discs or ramps (wall walks, tower tops, stair treads, timber decks). [polish] A castle's flight of steps is one ramp joining its treads' back edges (`f.flight`); `floorTreadAt(x, z, y)` (13s_footlock.js) gives the tread over a point instead (feet, the motion measures).
- [look3] A floor that bridges a way through (a walk over a gate, a gallery over a passage) needs its thickness (`th`, and `y0` just under its top): with none (`y0` -1.2, the default) `floorSolidAt` takes it as masonry from the ground up, and from version 11 to 13 no body walked out through a Logres keep's gate under the walk over it (stepTo refused every step into the gateway).
- NPCs never walk off a raised edge; the player can, and falls.
- [v15] A body standing on the ground with its own place inside a raised floor's solid (a rath's bank, a ramp's or a stair's side: shoved in by its fellows or set down there) may take any step that leaves no more of its footprint in the solid (`castleStepBlocked`, `castleSolidN`: the five points round its feet): every step was refused, and a band's leader stood inside Ráth Brannagh's bank for the rest of the war. Not a body up on a stair or a walk (a crew on a stair under a walk's boards rebuilt over it wandered under them and did not come down: `siege3.py`), and a body outside the solid is held at its side as before.
- [keeps] Bodies on different floors: NPCs push each other apart only within 1.5 m of height (`updateNPCs`); a melee blow or ability needs the foe within 2.6 m of height when either is up on a wall or a tower (`autoAttack`, `canUse`), and nothing is seen, struck or harmed through a tower's floor, a shut hatch or its wall (`towerSightBlocked`, 04m_ladders.js; `dealDamage` ignores a blow across one, not a dot). A raised collider (`c.y0`) blocks the camera and names only at its own height (`cameraRoom`, `npSeen`).
- [keeps] A keep's posts (`C.posts`): the first three are its garrison archers' (`fillGarrison` takes `slice(0, 3)`): the two gate towers' tops, then the right front corner tower's (Logres, Thule) or the lookout (Ériu); the rest are more ways up for those who climb after them (`castleWaypoint`). [walls] An archer's post stands within reach of its outer crenels or breastwork (a shot goes out through the shooter's own embrasure only within 1.4 m of it: `wallParapet`). `castleInside` counts a castle's tower rooms, and `castleAt` reaches 25 m (the Thule shafts' outer rooms).

**Fire and the reactive world**
- Fire spells and the dragon's breath call `fireLandsAt` / `fireConeAt` (in `04e_houses.js`), which light roofs, trees and grass.
- `fireLandsAt(x, y, z, r, by)`: pass the caster (an entity, or anything with a `faction`). Fire cast by a soldier, guard or townsman never lights its own realm's town, keep, keep hall or (for the player's realm) what the player built; only the player or an enemy does (`depthSpares*` in `09u_depth.js`). Forage plants catch through `depthFireAt` / `depthFireCone`.
- Lightning calls `natureStrike(x, z)`. Use `igniteHouse(houseAt(x, z))`, `igniteTree(i)` and `igniteGrass(x, z, r)` to set things alight.
- [look] A camp's layout (05d_fire.js `buildFireProps`: the fire pit, the bandits' tents, log and rack, the troll's boulders, the dead's ring of stones) stands clear of the trees, which were planted first: its centre moves up to 4 m off a trunk (`c.fireAt`; the camp itself, its home and its beasts, stays where it was), a tent turns about the fire until it is clear, and a stone, a boulder, the log or the rack that would stand in a trunk is left out. A moved collider carries where version 11 had it (`c.lookWas`) and one left out stays as an inactive collider, so `mountains.py` reads the camps as they were and every later placement (plants, herbs) comes out where it did.
- Meteors, the leap strike and the dragon landing call `natureImpact`, which leaves craters. Meteors also call `castleSiegeHit` and ([siege2]) `houseBlowAt`.
- [siege2] (version 13) Walls burn as they are built: a burh's palisade (04g_burn.js: cells along its walls; `palisadeFireAt` from `fireLandsAt`, `palisadeFireCone` from `fireConeAt`, a brand's E) and the thatched cones of the Ériu towers (04n_towerfire.js, look `rath`) catch from fire with its caster (the keep's own realm's fire spares them), burn, spread, char (the castle shader's `tChar`/`tGlow`/`tFresh`, set by `towerCut` and `palisadeCut`) and are built back in peace; rain and snow damp them. A palisade stretch burnt through is a breach (`palisadeGaps`); a new breach of any kind that is not a section of `C.sections` gives `siegeFace` what it needs (`wx`, `wz`, `wax`, `waz`, `across`, `half`, `C`, `state`) and calls `siegeOnBreach` / `siegeOnRepair` itself. In a running war (`soak.py 10`) the burhs' palisades caught 13 times (7 of them by spreading), 9 stretches burnt through and 3 gaps were built back in ten minutes (before a fire's reach was limited to three stretches either way and the wall's own sparks kept off it: 109 caught and 91 burnt through, whole palisades burnt down).
- [siege2] Stones break houses (04j_batter.js): anything heavy that lands on a house calls `houseStoneHit(H, x, y, z, mult, src, fromAbove)` (`H.bat`: how battered, the roof's hole, whether it fell to stones); code that walks `HOUSES` and looks at a ruin should know a ruin may be rubble (`H.bat.fell`) rather than a burnt shell. [look4] (version 15) A house that fell to stones has its walls broken off (`look4WallTop(H, lx, lz)`, 04d_look4.js: in its frame, the stub or the level they are built back to; its wall colliders' `h` is cut to 1.6 m while it lies so, the old one kept in `c.h0`), so anything that asks how high a house's wall stands (a shot, the camera, a line of sight) asks `look4Fell(H)` first.
- [siege3] (version 14) Everything of timber in a keep or a temple burns as it is built: a burh's wall walks (cells with their own floors: a body on a burning cell is burnt, a cell burnt through drops whoever stands on it and leaves a gap no route crosses), its gate of planks (burns, chars, falls: a broken gate's rules), the timber hallow's log ring and its gate (04g_blaze.js). Anything new of timber in a castle that should burn becomes cells of `BLAZE` (or zones of its own) and is fought by the garrison through `blazeFiresOf`; a new floor that can burn through stands down with `f.active` and its routes are left (`blazeRouteCut`). Fires that light each other (the walk, the palisade, the towers) cross only near where a fire took hold and light what goes no further (`gen`): a fire that can cross back and forth never stops. The walls' own sparks (`BLAZE.sparking`, `PAL.sparking`) light only grass and what lies on the ground.
- [siege3] The garrison fights fire (`blazeCrews`, `blazeFightStep` from `thinkPosted`: `e.ai.fire`, then `e.ai.fireBack` back to the floor it came from): a keep's and a temple's people go to a fire on their palisade, walks, gate or towers and beat it out (body action 21), leaving their posts and their watch, and come back down to their posts after; the player beats flames with Use beside them. A fire a band sets is a real diversion. [war2] (version 15) Townsfolk fight their houses' fires: a house's people and its neighbours within 45 m run to it, five at most, and beat the flames from under the eaves nearest the fire's front (body action 21); each beater pushes the burning front back (`HCREW.k`, half again in rain), and a front beaten back to nothing is out: the house stands, black under its eaves for a few minutes (`houseQuench`). Once the fire has taken the whole roof (its fall begun) it is past saving and they stand back. The player beats a house's flames with Use beside its walls (touch: `touch_flows.py only=housefire`). Anything that sets a house alight for a test that wants it to burn down sets `__hm.war2.HCREW.off` (houses.py, worldstate.py).
- [siege3] In a running war: `war.py`'s hour-long campaign on this branch told of the burhs' walks burning through three times (Ulfsborg twice, Hrafnsgarth once: the news tells it once in 45 s a keep) and their palisades four times, and of a palisade built back; `soak.py 10` ran without an error or a non-finite position (nothing reached a burh's timber in its ten minutes; the soak now prints `blaze:`, what burnt and the crews' work). In `siege3.py` the crews reach a palisade fire in about 4 s and beat it out in 15-32 s, a fire on the walk in 10-16 s (there at 7.5 s), and come back down to their posts' floor in about 30 s. `campaign_soak.py 60 low`: both runs on this branch were won by renown after 60 minutes with 1 and 2 keeps taken, the war standing nearly still after its first 10-15 minutes (61 and 12 bands that got nowhere for 150 s; no engine built after the first ten minutes), where main's one run on the same machine was won by keeps after 38 minutes (9 keeps taken, 34 stalls). A probe of both builds after 20 minutes of that war (`HM_HTML` for main) found most bands' leaders standing still for 30 s on both (5 of 6 here, 4 of 7 on main: a raid on its way, a band at a gate it does not strike, one on a burh's walk over its goal in the court), so the war's stalls are main's too; whether this branch makes them more common (the Logres gate passage now open, the garrisons leaving their posts for fires, the burhs' walks and gates that burn) is not settled by one run of main.
- [siege2] Cost (`perf.py low medium high views=keep,battle settle=16`, version 13, 640x360, SwiftShader, on a machine loaded by three other builders' runs; triangles / draw calls): the keep courtyard low 685k / 123, medium 1.32M / 154, high 2.19M / 197; the battle low 585k / 109, medium 1.21M / 147, high 1.95M / 199 (version 12's own measure: keep 757k / 131, 1.32M / 153, 2.21M / 195; battle 667k / 110, 1.24M / 148, 2.03M / 191; the battle is wherever the biggest fight is, so it moves from run to run); programs 84 at low, 87 at medium and high. Nothing is drawn while all is whole: a palisade's zones are uniforms of the castle shader, a gap's wreck and scaffold, a house's hole and cracks (uniforms of the house shader) only while they are there. JS: a step 4.3 ms in the keep and 4.2 ms in the battle; the palisades, the storms and the held shots are not among the top systems (`updateNPCs`, `thinkSoldier`, `updateWater`, `updateCastles`).
- [siege3] Cost (`perf.py low medium high views=keep,battle settle=16 render`, version 14, 640x360, SwiftShader, the machine shared with three other builders; triangles / draw calls, against version 13's own measure above): the keep courtyard low 697k / 125, medium 1.34M / 156, high 2.22M / 199 (685k / 123, 1.32M / 154, 2.19M / 197); the battle low 586k / 112, medium 1.19M / 143, high 1.97M / 201 (585k / 109, 1.21M / 147, 1.95M / 199); programs 85 at low and 88 at medium and high (84, 87: the houses' shadow pass has its own program now, `HOUSE_DEPTH_FS`). Nothing is drawn while all is whole: a burh's walk and the hallow's ring are uniforms of the castle shader, a burnt-through cell's wreck, a burnt gate's wreck and a holed roof's rafters are small meshes only while they are there. JS (`perf.py low views=keep,battle js`): a step 8.9 ms in the keep and 13.6 ms in the battle on a machine at a load of 11-12 on 2 CPUs (the top systems as before: `updateNPCs`, `thinkSoldier`, `updateClasses`, `updateWater`, `updateCastles`); `updateBlaze` walks the cells once a step and the crews once a second, and a firefighter looks up its fire each step only while it has one.
- The land is saved with the world (`world.land`, see `12b_worldstate.js`). Anything new that changes the land for longer than a minute should be added to `landSave` / `landApply`, with times saved as seconds left and every list capped.

**Indoors**
- `setIndoor(true)` hides the outdoor group, swaps the lighting and turns off shadows and light shafts.
- `regroupOutdoors()` runs every frame so that rebuilt grass stays in the outdoor group.
- Meshes that must stay visible in both modes set `userData.keep`.

**Entities**
- Factions: 0–2 are the realms, 3 is `FACTION_MONSTER`, 4 is `FACTION_NEUTRAL`.
- Roles scale stats through `ROLE_MULT`. Gear is applied in `setupStats`.
- Standing stat adjustments go in `e.tweak` (`hp`, `dmg`, `rangedRange`, `swing`, `meleeRange`, `speed`). `setupStats` reapplies them, so they survive a level change.
- `setupStats` applies `e.gear` (the totals of `gearTotals`) for the player and for bots (`e.bot`), never for other NPCs.
- `e.motion` moves a body by itself: `spin` (whirlwind) or `leap` (a parabola to a landing spot). `updateMotion` runs for the player and for NPCs.
- A tumble, a dash back or a blink goes no farther than `dashReach(e, ox, oz, dx, dz, maxS)` (07_entities.js, version 12) lets it: over the ground or the floor the body stands on in quarter-metre steps, stopping before anything solid at its height, water it cannot walk (ice carries it) and any drop of more than a step. A melee foe presses an archer or a mage (their leap back, a nova) only at about its own height (within 2.2 m).

**Gear and the late game** ([balance2], `06e_gearcurve.js`)
- Item levels follow the level to 50 (`makeItem` caps at `LEVEL_CAP` unless `lvlCap` says less): camp foes and soldiers drop gear of about their level (`dropIlvl`: ±1), a camp champion's spoils one above it, keep lords theirs (+4), Cinderwing's hoard two above you, the Barrow's burial chest at your level and the Hollow King's hoard two above, merchants and path rewards at yours. Grades are as before (`DROP_WEIGHTS`). `itemReq` is the item level less 3.
- `itemScale(ilvl)` grows 0.4 an item level to 24, then 0.25 (06b_items.js): a level 50 piece is about 1.6 times a level 24 one, and an epic set at 50 stays under the armour cap (39 of 40). A named masterwork's bonus stat is 0.32 of the item's measure (0.22 for the master's touch on other crafted gear): about 15% over a drop of its grade and level.
- A foe past level 24 is as tough against a body in gear as the gear of its level makes that body stronger (`gearLateHarm` in `dealDamage`, by the foe's own level, to 50). Bodies without gear (the armies, beasts, the dead) are untouched among themselves, so the war's pace is what it was; `tools/tests/loot.py` measures blows to kill and to die with the old curve and the new (within ±7%).
- A body in gear (the player, a bot) draws its power past level 20 from the power stat, not the level (`gearPowerBase` in `setupStats`): abilities cost the same at every level, so a long fight drains power at 50 as it does at 20. NPCs keep `basePower(level)` (their own cadence spends it).
- Old saves keep their items as they were: each carries its own item level and stats (`sanitizeItem`); junk keeps its level to 60.

**Crowd control** ([balance], `07c_balance.js`)
- Every stun, root and snare passes `ccAdjust` in `addBuff` (after the realm gifts' `giftBuffAdjust`). Players, bots and champions (`ccChampion`) have diminishing returns per kind: full, half, a quarter within 15 s of the last, then 6 s immune (`e.ccdr`). A control refreshed by the same source while it still holds does not count again. Pass `{ ccAll: true }` as `addBuff`'s extra to put an ordinary body under the rule (a parry's stun does). A freeze is a stun: `addChill` asks `ccAdjust(e, 'stun', dur, 'frozen', true)` for champions (ordinary bodies keep the five seconds of thaw); the frozen buffs themselves come with the source `'frozen'` and are not counted twice. An immunity (the third control's, or Divine Shield's) holds for anyone.
- New controls should use these three buff kinds so the rule covers them.
- [balance2] Answers to control, each a calling's own: a mage's Arcane Ward takes a stun in the mage's place (not a freeze) and loses half of what it has left (`wardTakesStun`, asked first by `ccAdjust`; the ward is the `eward` buff with the mage's own `shield`); a warrior's Charge leaves no root or slow taking hold for 2 s (`chargeFree`, as an immunity in `e.ccdr`); a priest's Renewal on itself loosens roots and makes it steadfast for 4 s (`priestSteadfast`, the `steadfast` buff): nothing but a stun breaks its spells (`breakCast`, use it wherever a blow or an arrow ends a cast) and blows do not set them back (`castPushback`: a melee blow sets a cast back 0.35 s, twice at most).
- [balance2] Healing passes `healWound` in `healEnt`: a broadhead's wound (the `wound` buff, 6 s) takes off healing on it ([balance3] 30%, 45% from an archer of rank II: `woundOf`; [balance5] 30% at every rank). Holy damage (`opts.kind === 'holy'`, Smite) is not turned by a raised shield.
- [balance3] (`07c_balance3.js`) Abilities a calling forbids for a moment go through `classCanUse` (asked by `canUse`, after `busy`; the player sees "Frost holds you fast" for `'held'`): no Leap Strike out of a frost root (a root buff with `frost`), no Charge either out of a seasoned mage's (`deep`, rank II: `frostRootExtra`) or out of a blizzard (the blizzard's snare carries `blizzard`). A new root that should hold the same way passes `frostRootExtra(src)` as `addBuff`'s extra; a Charge still drops every other root and snare. Charge and Leap Strike are one rush from level 30 (`rushShare`, from `warriorView`: using either holds the other back 10 s). Pursuit: every non-melee, non-dot blow on a warrior takes a second off Charge (`pursuitOnHit`, from `classOnHit`). Holy light dazzles (`dazzleOnHit`: Smite and the Pillar, not holy ground or Holy Fire's burning, not Cinderwing): the `dazzle` buff's value is the share of weapon blows (`opts.kind === 'melee'`) that miss (`dazzleMiss`, asked by `classHarm` after the steam's blindness), by the priest's rank (`DAZZLE.miss`: 0.05, 0.17, 0.26, 0.35) for 3 s, and a warrior dazzled at 0.15 or more cannot parry (`dazzled`). Guarded, a raised shield costs a warrior no pace (`SHIELD_WALL_PACE` in `classSpeedMult`): the skilled bot (`shieldWallWanted`) and NPC warriors (`npcWallWanted`, from `classNpc` and `npcClassStep`) hold a shield wall against a bow out of reach while no rush is ready. A priest's own healing (`priestSelf`) is 0.75 of a heal's measure at rank I and a steady 0.55 of rank I's from rank II (0.55 / `rankMult`). [balance5] 0.7 at rank I.
- [balance3] `opts.pierce` in `dealDamage`: `true` ignores all armour (lightning), a number goes through that share of it (`BODKIN_PIERCE` 0.5: bodkins and Deadeye).
- [balance4] (`07c_balance4.js`) Charge is a real rush now (see the module row): everything tuned in versions 9 to 11 to stand in for it is set aside (pursuit, `PURSUIT_CD` 0; the moment free of roots and slows after it, `CHARGE_FREE` 0) and Charge waits 16 s (10), 12 s until Leap Strike is learned. A rush is an `e.motion` of kind `leap` with `rush` set: code that asks about a leap (busy, no block, the foot lock) treats it the same, and `chargeRushStep` (from `updateMotion`) moves it. A root ends it where it is (a snare laid in its line, a frost nova): the archer's and the mage's answer to it. Shield Bash waits `RUSH_BASH` after a Charge or a Leap Strike (a rush, a bash and the blows in the stun's shadow killed a caster before it could answer). A raised shield turns no volley (`classHarm`: the archer's `aoe` falls from above, as holy light does). A rush arrives on the step that brings it within reach (`chargeRushStep` checks after it moves: a foe walking away is reached at once). Rallying Cry's heal does not grow with rank (a body's own healing does not: a priest's own heals at 0.55 of rank I's from rank II, `priestSelf`, which the Pillar of Dawn now honours too). The dazzle stops parries at every rank (`DAZZLE_NOPARRY` 0.01) and takes 15/18/21/24% of weapon blows by rank (`DAZZLE.miss`). Mend heals by the wound (`mendWound`). A mage's spells grow 7% a rank past the first (`mageRankK`). Guarded, a raised shield still costs no pace (`SHIELD_WALL_PACE` 1). New rush-like abilities: give them `dash` and a reach, and they rush through the same code.
- [balance5] (`07c_balance5.js`) An interrupt of the priest's: Holy Fire cast at a foe within 10 m in the middle of a spell breaks it (`holyFlare`, through `breakCast`, so a steadfast priest keeps its spell). Holy ground dazzles the foes standing on it (`hallowDazzle`). Crippling Arrow wounds as a broadhead does for as long as it slows (`crippleWound`). Whirlwind heals 2% of health for each foe each of its two cuts strikes (`whirlHeal`; not the Ériu feat's whirling finisher). A broadhead's wound is 30% at every rank (`WOUND_HEAL_LATE` 0.3: the 45% from rank II was set on cratered ground). A priest's own healing is 0.7 of a heal's measure at rank I (`PRIEST_SELF`; 0.65 of rank I's from rank II was tried and set back to 0.55). A new interrupt goes through `breakCast` (a steadfast caster keeps its spell). Anything a test measures between phases should leave the ground as it found it: `CB.wipe` flattens craters and puts out grass fires (`__hm.bal5.flattenCraters`).
- [balance6] (`07c_balance6.js`) `breakCast` also asks `wardTakesBreak`: a mage behind its Arcane Ward keeps its spell from any breaking blow, for a quarter of the ward (a stun still breaks it: a stun clears `casting` itself). Bots see a spell no blow can break through `spellGuarded` (a steadfast priest's, a warded mage's): a new bot that would interrupt asks it. A priest's own Renewal costs `RENEW_ROTE_K` (2) times the power while its last still has more than `RENEW_ROTE_LEFT` (2) s to mend (`renewRoteCost`, from `priestView`); a priest's own healing is 0.76 of a heal's measure at rank I (`PRIEST_SELF`). A Charge that ends 'blocked' (`chargeRushStep`) comes round in half its wait (`rushHeld`). A heal over time that should stack with the Renewal it comes with needs a source of its own (`addBuff` refreshes a buff of the same kind and source: the Well of Ériu's merged into the Renewal; `WELL_SRC`). A measure's arenas: duels in `CB.spotsWide` (far apart, dry), the pack in `CB.spots` with `CB.packOpen` (open ground within 140 m of the player); `CB.breakFires` each simulated second; a duel's arena made whole with `__hm.bal6.cleanArena` when its duel ends.
- [balance7] (`07c_balance7.js`) The rules of version 15 read their measures from `B7T` (a test sets one between runs to measure a rule against its absence; a text that names a measure is built from it, as the rush share's are). Charge waits 12 s at every level and its landing blow slows at every level (`chargeCdLate`, `chargeSlowLate`); Charge and Leap Strike hold each other back 5 s (`rushShare`); an arrow with no special head strikes 5% harder a rank past the first (`plainArrowK`); Mend on a scratch is 0.15 of its measure (`MEND_WOUND.floor`). A realm's way that is an edge on its own ground asks `archerWayHere(e, realm)` (the archer's; a new one for another calling would ask the same kinds of ground: `elevatedEnt`, `archerTrees`, `archerColdAt`). A measure's duels: open arenas (`CB.spotsWide(n, gap, true)`), the grass grown back after each (`CB.wholeGrass`), and the side made first acts first (`CB.altOrder`).

**Animals** (`08h_animals.js`)
- Kept animals are entities with role `animal` and a record in `e.tame` (`owner` `player`, `town` or `wild`; `k` hound, pig, horse, deer, sheep, cattle, hen; `cmd` follow, stay, guard, attack, pen; traits in `tr`). `updateNPCs` hands them to `thinkAnimal`; hounds fight through `fightTarget` and `autoAttack` like soldiers (faction of the player); the others are `FACTION_NEUTRAL`.
- Which crowd draws a beast is `e.crowd || e.kind` (shorn sheep, bulls, saddled horses), and `e.visCode` rides above the scale in `iColA.w`; only the farm crowds (05j) decode it, so leave it 0 for the original crowds.
- Wild wolves and boars stay camp mobs until tamed (`ai.tameHold` pauses `thinkMob` while one eats from your hand); deer stay critters (`animalsSparesDeer`).
- Your own horse: `horseLook` asks `animalsRideLook`, `toggleMount` asks `animalsQuickMount`, `dismount` and `killEnt` hand the horse back through `animalsDismounted`; its speed trait scales `MOUNT_SPEED` (`animalsMountSpeed`).
- Pens are rectangles in their own frame with the gate on the front (+z); `anSegClear` clips paths against them exactly (thin fences slip between `segmentClear`'s samples).

**Scaling past 20**
- `warLevel()` is the player's level, clamped to 20–50.
- Realm units keep their offset from it in `e.wlOff`: soldiers −5 to 0, guards 0, lords +4, hallow-wardens +2, muster wardens +10. Gates are +2, and their HP scales with damage.
- Barrow monsters keep `e.baseLevel` plus `barrowBoost()`. The dragon is `max(24, warLevel + 4)`.
- `rescaleWar()` runs at game start and on every level-up past 20.
- Ability potency rises by rank: `rankMult(level)` adds 12% for every ten levels past 20.

**War AI**
- Each realm commander issues orders: `attack`, `defend`, `raid`, `carry`, `retrieve`, `patrol`, and `rally` (a band following the player). `commanderThink` hands planning to `planWar` in `08g_campaign.js`. Orders the player gave hold until done (`retask` skips them).
- A warband works through the phases `travel`, `siege`, `assault`, `hold`. With a catapult (`04h_siege.js`) a band of the usual size (fewer than eight besides the crew) stands guard by it (`bombard`) until the wall falls, then storms the breach (`breach`); only a big band goes on to the gate as well, and a wall that has not fallen after four minutes of work is given up for the gate.
- Keep doors are entities that let their owners pass. Relics are called "hallows" in game.
- Nothing stands still for the rest of a war (version 12): a fighter that gets no nearer a foe it chases for 6 s gives it up and leaves it alone for 20 s (`fightChase`, `e.ai.unreach`, asked by `canReach`); a band storming a breach that gets no nearer the next point of the way in for 20 s goes to the gate instead and leaves breaches alone for two minutes (`O.bprog`, `O.breachBan`); a band waiting by an engine that has not begun to work 150 s after it was planned gives it up for the gate (`O.bombFirst`); and a band that has got nowhere for 150 s, replans or none (its leader has not moved 6 m and its blows have not landed: `wb.realProgress`, `wb.hitAt` from `dealDamage`), hands its order back for a new one, unless it is waiting on purpose (holding a keep it took, defending its own, patrolling, or by its engine while it works). `WAR.stats.chasesDropped`, `WAR.stats.bandStalls`, `SIEGE.stats.breachGiveUps` count them.
- A new order moves a band at once (version 13): a soldier striking a gate leaves it as soon as its band's order is no longer the siege of that gate (`thinkSoldier`). A band at a gate given a new order by the player or its commander kept striking the old gate until it died there (`war.py`'s order to march on another keep found it: the soldiers stood at 151 m for the whole minute).
- The war's life over whole campaigns (`campaign_soak.py 60 low`, two runs of each build in fitting version 14 together, an hour of war each): version 13 won by hallows after 41 minutes and then by keeps after 9 (13 keeps taken in the hour, 26 bands stalled 150 s, 679 chases given up), and by keeps after 18 minutes and then stood at two keeps each for the rest of the hour (6 taken, 27 stalls, 1,102 chases given up); version 14 won by keeps after 47 minutes and was near the next (17 taken, 39 stalls, 1,502 chases given up), and once only by renown at the hour's end with 13 keeps taken back and forth (54 stalls, 1,589 chases given up). Runs of one build differ more than the builds do; read several before tuning.
- [siege2] (version 13) The engines' mark is the weakest way in from where the band stands (`siegeMarks`, 04i_siege2.js): what is left of each whole section (curtains and towers alike), a tower's narrow way in weighed against it (`SIEGE2_TOWER_K`), and how far its face is; so a cracked tower, or one much nearer than any curtain, is battered down. A breach a band storms may be a curtain's, a fallen tower's or a palisade's burnt gap (`siegeOpenBreach` takes all three, a tower's at 1.5 times its distance); a band standing guard by its engine (`bombard`) goes for the breach it made from up to 95 m (any other band from 60 m, as before: a trebuchet's band stands about 75 m out and never went in). Through a fallen tower every member walks the way in itself (`e.ai.storm`, `siegeStormStep` from `thinkSoldier` before its own targets): one or two at a time through the store room and its door, the rest waiting on the field before the rubble, fighting only foes on their own level close by, the foes on the walls left for when they are through; through the door they hold the court by it until the band is in. A member that gets nowhere for 16 s gives the way up and goes with the band; the band's own 20 s give-up watches all of them.
- [siege3] (version 14) Nobody is stuck in the ruins (04i_siege3.js): a member's place in its band's formation is moved off rubble, a fallen drum or a wall toward the leader (`bandSlot`), and a member that gets no nearer it takes the leader's footsteps, then a step aside (`bandUnstick`). The walk over a Logres keep's gate has a thickness now (04g_castles.js; look3 found the same and the merge kept its bridge half a metre thick): it was solid down to the ground in the gate passage, so nobody (bands, the player) walked in through a Logres gate, broken or open, since version 11 (a band sent through a broken Logres gate stood before it; `siege3.py gatepast`: 0 of 10 in the court on main, 10 on this branch). A storm through a fallen tower's door gathers three inside the door before it goes out into a held court (`siegeStormHold`), and siege2's turn no longer holds a band on the field for good behind a leader or a member that is fighting elsewhere (on main a band of ten storming a Logres corner drum against its garrison sent one through in 150 s, twice). Measured with `siege3.py only=stormcost reps=2` (a band of ten against the whole garrison of six and its lord, the tower already fallen; through: the most of the band in the court at once; left: attackers alive at the end; the defenders 7 before), main against this branch:

  | Tower | main: through, left, defenders after, taken | this branch, a build before the last storm rule | this branch, final |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Logres corner drum | 1, 9, 6, no · 1, 7, 5, no (the band held on the field) | 1, 9, 6, no (held on the field) · 4, 0, 4, no | 2, 0, 6, no · 3, 0, 6, no |
  | Logres gate drum | 10, 10, 0, taken · 1, 9, 6, no | 9, 7, 0, taken · 1, 0, 6, no | 3, 2, 0, taken · 3, 0, 6, no |
  | Ériu lookout | 8, 0, 4, no · 2, 0, 7, no | 7, 0, 4, no · 9, 0, 4, no | 8, 0, 4, no · 8, 5, 0, taken |
  | Ériu gate drum | 6, 10, 0, taken · 3, 0, 4, no | 2, 5, 0, taken · 6, 5, 0, taken | 5, 0, 6, no · 4, 9, 0, taken |

  Taken 2 of 8 on main, 3 of 8 on this branch (both runs), defenders killed 3.0 against 4.0 and 3.5 a storm on average, and a band held on the field for good on main twice (the Logres corner, the Logres gate), once on the build before the last rule (a leader shooting from the field counted as ahead of the rest: it held them all; one of its members stood at the rubble's foot while the rest waited their turn) and never with the final rules (every storm ends with the band through or dead: 29-92 s): costly, not hopeless; runs of two move a row by half the band.

- [war2] (version 15) The war over whole campaigns, band by band (`campaign_soak.py`: every band looked at every 2 s, whether it fought (a blow landed), got nearer its next point, waited on purpose (holding a keep it took, defending, on watch, its engine at work) or stood still; every band still for 30 s and every order given back written down with where its leader stood and what was round it). In version 14 what stood still was, by those notes: soldiers left up on a keep's walks, stairs and towers after a fight (a band's leader up there held its whole band: most of the stalls), soldiers in a burh tower's ground room, leaders whose way began at a node outside the walls they stood in (they walked into the wall), a band in the court of the keep it attacked going back out to strike the gate behind it, engines that could not be pushed to their place and the bands waiting by them, bands held by a foe nobody could reach, bows and staffs whose shots at a gate struck the wall beside it, members that could not follow their leader's footsteps, climbers held at a hatch, bands cut down to one or two holding their realm's place at the muster (at low a realm musters two bands: one soldier stuck at a far keep held one for ten minutes), a leader at the outer face of a temple's round ring counted inside it (castleInside's square box), and late in a long war claims too slow to be won before the hour. Each is put right at its cause (08j_war2.js). The runs (`campaign_soak.py 60 low` unless said; runs of one build differ a lot, so read several):

  | Build | How the campaigns ended | Orders given back after 150 s | Chases given up | Band time: fought / nearer / waited / still |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | version 14 (`main`), 25 minutes | none won; two keeps each from the 15th minute on | 11 | 900 | (not measured) |
  | version 14, the integrator's two hours | by keeps after 47 minutes · only by renown at the hour | 39 · 54 | 1,502 · 1,589 | (not measured) |
  | this work's first fixes, an hour | Thule by keeps after 21 minutes; the next still open at its 38th minute (8 keeps taken in all) | 1 | 884 | 48 / 24 / 19 / 10% |
  | the build before the detours saw walls, 35 minutes | none won (two or three keeps each) | 7 | 785 | 39 / 26 / 20 / 16% |
  | the final build but the new campaign's garrisons, an hour | Ériu by hallows after 45 minutes (19 keeps taken in it); the next open at its 13th minute, Thule having held four keeps for 3 of them | 0 | 1,042 | 47 / 34 / 14 / 5% |
  | version 15 merged (fitting it together), two hours | Logres by renown at the hour (43 keeps taken; no realm held four for more than 70 s) · Logres by keeps after 30 minutes, the next open at its 28th | 0 · 5 | 829 · 733 | 48 / 32 / 17 / 4% · 45 / 30 / 18 / 7% |
  | the narrow way and the stormers fixed, two hours | Logres by renown at the hour · Ériu by renown at the hour | 3 · 1 | 884 · 779 | 46 / 31 / 15 / 7% · 47 / 34 / 15 / 4% |
  | version 15 as published (a body out of a bank too), two hours | Ériu by renown at the hour · Thule by keeps after 57 minutes | 0 · 1 | 620 · 1,183 | 50 / 34 / 13 / 3% · 49 / 32 / 15 / 4% |

  In that last hour no band stood still for 30 s more than five times (twice at a gate, a raid on the march, a raid at a gate, a band chasing a hallow: 36 to 76 s each) and no order was given back; by order and phase the bands stood still most while storming a fallen tower (21% of `attack/breach`: waiting their turn on the field before the rubble, or for enough to go in, counts as standing) and chasing a loose hallow (18% of `retrieve/travel`: one band held 76 s by a foe nobody fought). `war.py`'s own campaign on this work's build was won by Ériu with both enemy hallows after 34 minutes. The war's pace: 19 keeps taken in the first campaign's 45 minutes (one every two and a half minutes; each keep changed hands about every 14), against version 14's 13 to 17 in an hour. The second campaign began with Ériu's garrisons thinned by the first and engines of the others still in the field: it lost both keeps in three minutes; a new campaign now begins with every keep's garrison whole (`restoreKeep`, 08g_campaign.js; not yet measured over a campaign's start: the engines left in the field and the walls and towers still breached are as they were). Cost (`perf.py low views=battle js`, one run of each build on a machine shared with three other builders, load 12-13): a step 4.4 ms before and 5.2 ms after (`thinkSoldier` 100 against 158 ms over the measure, `updateNPCs` 228 against 292); the battle is wherever the biggest fight is, so each run measures a different one, and a soldier's new looks are a few dozen distance tests (the towers' rooms and footprints, the walls round it): read it again on a quiet machine before trusting the difference.

  [v15] Fitting version 15 together, the merged build's soaks found two places a band stood for good, each fixed at its cause (the War AI's [v15] bullet): the strip between Hrafnsgarth's hall and its back palisade (two Thule bands' leaders stood in it 150 s at a time, order after order: 30% of the band time in `attack/assault` in one hour) and the inside of Ráth Brannagh's bank (an Ériu leader stood there through five orders, 500 s). Of the six hours above on version 15's builds, two campaigns were won by keeps (after 30 and 57 minutes), none by hallows, and four went to renown at the hour: keeps changed hands 36 to 44 times an hour and no realm held four for more than two minutes at a time (one more hour on the build before the bank's fix was won by Ériu with the hallows after 49 minutes). Still standing in the published build's hours: a band 96 m from Kestrel Hold by its idle trebuchet stood 106 s and on through two more orders (its leader's way ran 100 m on with no way round on the grid); a band in a court whose way lies out by its own gate waits while foes stand within 19 m of it (38 to 66 s at Ráth Brannagh).

- [v15] (fitting version 15 together) A band storming a fallen tower keeps storming after its leader is through: once the leader has walked the breach's way and stands in the court, the [war2] rule makes the band's phase 'assault' (the court and the lord its goal), and `siegeStormStep` lets each member still on its way in walk on through the store room and its door (`goingIn`: the band's breach is the storm's, the phase 'breach', or 'assault' and the member not yet through); one through drops its storm and goes with the band. Before, the flip dropped every storm at once and the members still outside fell back to their places in the ranks behind the leader: one pressed against the drum's broken wall (its trail along the leader's footsteps led through the masonry) or stood at a place in the ranks outside the walls for a minute and more. A leader inside a keep's or a temple's walls (within the walls' inner faces, 14.4 m from a square keep's middle or 15.6 m from a round one's, on the court's floor) that gets no nearer its point for 8 s and has no way round on the 1 m grid takes one on a grid of quarter metres to the court (`bandLeaderDetour`: `localPath(..., 0.36, 48, true, 0.25)`, its points dense in the narrow part and followed to within 1 m, `w2detour.narrow`); from there the band's own way. The 1 m grid padded by 0.6 m closes any way under about 2.2 m wide (the strip between a burh's hall and its back palisade is 1.2 m: two Thule bands' leaders stood in it, retaking Hrafnsgarth and then marching off, for some fifteen minutes of an hour's soak). It is never tried from outside the walls (through a breach the court is no way on for a band whose way lies out by the gate) nor up on a walk (the grid knows nothing of heights). A house's fire crew who gets no nearer its place by the fire for 2.5 s takes `localPath` round what stands between, once, before it tries the next place round (`houseBeatStep`, `WAR2.stats.crewWays`). A body standing inside a raised floor's solid walks out of it (Floors).

**Townsfolk's ways** (version 10)
- Out of doors a townsman or farmer who stops getting nearer the work asks `localPath(ax, az, bx, bz)` (08f_townlife.js): A* on a 1 m grid over the colliders within the box round both ends (doors open for townsfolk, so they are no wall), smoothed to a few waypoints; a sidestep is the fallback when there is no way. Indoors `houseRoute` does the same round the furniture. [v15] `localPath(ax, az, bx, bz, pad, maxSpan, gates, cs)`: `cs` is the cells' size in metres (1 by default); on a finer grid (a narrow way: 0.25) a sight line in the smoothing keeps a cell clear on either side, so the way stays as dense as the cells in a narrow part and does not cut a corner (a body cut one straight into the wall beyond it and was held there); the span is in metres either way, so a fine grid wants a short one (48 m: 37k cells).
- Companions (08h_animals.js): a straight way counts as clear only over ground the body can walk (or swim) as well as past colliders and paddocks (`anSegClear`), else the trail, then `localPath`, then a sidestep. One that makes no headway for 3.5 s out of sight (8 s in sight), or is held fast 5 s, comes back in a few metres behind you on your trail, or at its waiting spot by you when the trail is far behind (`anCatchUpSpot`), never at a stale trail point.
- A town's fisher (07e_boats.js) is a villager within 110 m of the jetty's foot; it leaves its house by the door first and walks with the time the walk needs (`boatWalkPlan`; a `go` step's `T` is its time in seconds, else 16, or 28 after a sidestep).
- A fire with `wall` set (the towers' torches) lights a brand but cooks nothing (`fireNear` skips it). Collider circles with `jetty` set are the jetties' posts (07e_boats.js), which the land's checks leave out.

**Farming**
- A bed's slots are 0.6 m apart; `slotPos(P, s)` is where a plant stands; `plotAt(x, z)`, `plotLocal`/`plotWorld` convert to the bed's furrows (u across, v along).
- Beds and strips go into the cover map (`farmCoverAll` at the end of `buildCoverMask`), so no grass grows through the soil; removing a bed rebuilds the cover map.
- Growth is stepped every half second in `updateFarming` (`FARM.speed` speeds it for tests); drawing refills the instances twice a second, when the camera moves 3 m or when `FARM.drawDirty` is set.
- The town fields are `W.fieldRects` (01_world.js): Brackenford's, then the gardens of Ériu and Thule (`realm`, `garden`, narrower `stripW`/`gap`; placed after the towns are built, clear of the houses with a clear walk from the plaza, and kept free of camps), so the saved strips keep their order. Apples hang on the trees only from late summer through autumn (`forageShown` asks the season), the trees blossom in spring. A garden strip has its town in `P.stand.town` and its rotation in `P.rotList` (`FARM_GARDEN_ROT`). No tree grows in a field: a tree that would is placed off its edge, keeping its index.

**Saves**
- [guide] `char.lore.pv`: the path's version the save was made with (1 when missing). `loreOnStart` takes the early steps added since quietly for a character past them; a step added later needs `pv` set to the new `PATH_VERSION`.
- Old saves must always keep loading.
- When you add fields, default them in `startGame` / `validSave` and include them in `coreKey`.
- [war2] World part `gates`: the keeps' and temples' broken gates (rammed, battered or burnt down: a burnt one lies charred), with the seconds since they broke; on loading they lie broken and are rebuilt by the war's own rule (a broken gate came back whole on loading).
- [water2] World parts `dive` (the finds taken, with the seconds until they come back) and `boats` (each boat away from its berth: where, its state, its char). [polish] World part `tracks` (the prints still showing near the player, capped at 320; see 07f_tracks.js).
- [siege3] World part `blaze` (the burhs' walks' and the timber hallow's ring's cells that are not whole, and burning gates). A gate burnt down comes back whole on loading, as a broken one does (the doors are not saved).
- New systems keep their state in save parts (`registerSavePart`, 06d_goods.js): `s.char.x[key]` with the character, `s.world.x[key]` with the world; `load` gets `undefined` for an older save and must sanitise whatever it reads; `key` says what counts as a change worth writing.
- `coreKey` counts the land by what changed in it (`landKey`: felled trees, logs, craters, tiles, piles, house and wall states), never by its clocks, so regrowth alone does not cause writes. `char.gift` holds the realm ultimate's cooldown and is no reason to write either.
- A busy land (four trees down, a grass fire, five craters, three houses and three walls changed) adds about 10 KB; an untouched world adds 7 bytes.

**Phones and performance** (version 9)
- Budgets: low about 700k triangles and 160 draw calls at most in every view of `perf.py`, medium about 1.6 million; high may spend more but nothing wasteful. Measure with `perf.py` before and after.
- A mesh taken out at runtime uses `removeFromParent()`: after its first frame it lives in the outdoor group (`regroupOutdoors`), so `scene.remove` misses it (grass rebuilt on a quality change was drawn twice over until version 9).
- Anything instanced gets a draw radius per quality and, if it is many, a far model; low has no shadow map at all.
- Far work: bodies more than 40 m from the camera are weathered every third step (`updateWear`); townsfolk and the town's and the wild's beasts more than 90 m from the player think every other step with two steps' time (`updateNPCs`). New per-body systems follow the same pattern (`let dt = dt0` per body).
- Where things stood and stand (`perf.py`, 640x360, SwiftShader; triangles / draw calls per frame, main and shadow pass; "before" is version 8, whose grass rebuilt on a quality change stayed drawn: the stale share is in brackets):

  | View | Low before | Low after | Medium before | Medium after |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | Logres town, fields and common | 1.91 M (0.90 M) / 108 | 696 k / 107 | 2.81 M (1.19 M) / 126 | 1.51 M / 162 |
  | Keep courtyard | 2.06 M (0.90 M) / 157 | 679 k / 129 | 2.71 M (1.19 M) / 147 | 1.19 M / 154 |
  | Inside the keep's hall | 1.99 M (0.90 M) / 145 | 669 k / 122 | 2.98 M (1.19 M) / 196 | 1.17 M / 150 |
  | River | 2.14 M (0.90 M) / 149 | 657 k / 130 | 3.33 M (1.19 M) / 223 | 1.23 M / 174 |
  | Frontier battle | 1.83 M (0.90 M) / 106 | 590 k / 111 | 2.86 M (1.19 M) / 178 | 1.13 M / 143 |
  | Forest | 2.31 M (0.90 M) / 207 | 774 k / 174 | 3.43 M (1.19 M) / 275 | 1.49 M / 220 |
  | The Barrow | 128 k / 30 | 120 k / 31 | 128 k / 32 | 123 k / 33 |

  (The "before" call counts are low for trees: that run did not bring the trees' visibility up to each view. The forest at low was measured before the last tree change: only the chunk under the camera now keeps near trees in full.) A simulation step in the town costs about 8 ms of JS here (15 ms before: far bodies weathered every third step, townsfolk and beasts far off at half rate). Under SwiftShader a frame's JS time is mostly first-use shader compiles (`getProgramInfoLog` in a profile), not steady work. Boot: about 23 s to ready here (the shader compile in "Lighting the sky" is the biggest step, then "Raising the keeps"); the JS heap after boot is about 180-190 MB.
- Version 10 ([motion]; `perf.py ... settle=16`: 16 more frames so the levels of detail are in place): low: town 705 k / 102 (its fields were fuller than in the version 9 run: 54 k triangles of crops against 12 k), keep 605 k / 116, hall 607 k / 113, river 658 k / 124, battle 582 k / 109, forest 667 k / 159 (it was 794 k / 172 at the start of version 10), Barrow 120 k / 31; medium: town 1.48 M / 143, keep 1.19 M / 143, forest 1.46 M / 199 (1.45 M / 216 in version 9), river 1.30 M / 163, battle 1.24 M / 144, hall 1.17 M / 143 (the Barrow at medium was not measured again: the machine was too loaded; it has no trees). What changed: the split chunks' trees go into per-kind bands culled tree by tree against the view (`treeBandsFill`; the near band's shadow copy only within the shadow box), the lobes beyond 64 m on low (`TREE_FAR2_D`), trees drawn to 200 m on low (`treeDist`; the farther ones were lost in the fog), the near-tree set's trees left out of the bands, and low no longer splitting sixteen chunks tree by tree (its 0 for `TREE_LOD_D` was read as missing).
- Version 11 ([keeps]; `perf.py low medium high views=keep render settle=16`, the keep courtyard with every tower hollow, against the version 10 build on the same machine the same hour): low 683k / 124 calls (654k / 120), medium 1.31M / 155 (1.27M / 150), high 2.19M / 197 (2.10M / 192). The keeps' meshes (`buildCastles`) 51k triangles in view (27k), the near keep's rooms (`buildTowers`, drawn within 70 m) 24k in one call (18k), the doors and lids of the keeps in view (`towerParts`) 11k in four calls.
- Version 12 ([look]; `perf.py low medium high views=town,keep,forest render settle=16`, against the version 11 build the same hour; triangles / calls): low town 718k / 106 (722k / 106), keep 690k / 124 (704k / 124), forest 737k / 167 (735k / 167); medium town 1.50M / 145 (1.51M / 145), keep 1.32M / 155 (1.34M / 155), forest 1.54M / 207 (1.54M / 207); high town 2.66M / 181 (2.67M / 181), keep 2.17M / 196 (2.18M / 200), forest 2.64M / 244 (2.64M / 244). No draw added; the houses' meshes (`buildHouses`) 1 to 2k triangles more in view (the walls on a grid close their reveals with their own faces: a Logres front with a door and two windows is 164 triangles against nine boxes' 108). The towns' green, paths and the bushes' turf are the cover map and the road channel (no geometry); the extra cover map drawn once the herbs stand costs one rebuild at boot.
- Version 13 ([look2]; `perf.py low medium high views=town,keep,forest render settle=16`, against version 12's numbers above; triangles / calls): low town 726k / 106, keep 701k / 125, forest 737k / 167; medium town 1.51M / 145, keep 1.32M / 155, forest 1.52M / 205; high town 2.68M / 181 (the run's 40 minutes ran out before the high keep and forest on a machine shared by four builders). No geometry or draw added: every change is in a shader or a map (the contact-shade map is RG now, 1 MB more; one more program, the forage plants' shadow pass, 87). The new shader work runs only where it is needed: the trees' hand-over within its 3.5 m band (outside it the tests return at once; at low there is no band), the leaves within 30 m of the camera on the chunk trees (at high and medium only where those stand past the near trees' reach, so a few metres), the paths' 1 m read wherever the map is not empty, a beast's room test as a person's (the 8 roofs nearest the camera), the climber's sling only on bodies at actions 14 and 18.
- Version 15 ([look4]; `perf.py low medium views=town,keep render settle=16`, against version 14's main measured the same morning on the same machine, loaded by three other builders' runs; triangles / calls): low town 722k / 106 (680k / 104), keep 685k / 124 (703k / 125); medium town 1.50M / 145 (1.44M / 143), keep 1.32M / 154 (1.34M / 156); programs 84, 85, 87 and 87 (84, 85, 87 and 88). The houses' merged meshes are lighter (`buildHouses` 34k against 38k in the town at low, 86k against 96k from the keep: version 6's scaffold of poles and planks, built into every house's mesh and hidden, is gone); the town's extra triangles are its fields' crops that morning (`farmSpecies` 67k against 24k at low, 91k against 28k at medium). Nothing new is drawn while no house lies fallen or is being built back (the rubble's mesh, a house's scaffold: one mesh and its shadow each, only then), a castle's scaffold draws only the lifts that are up, and the new shader work runs only for a fallen house's walls (`uHD.w`), burning or charred timber (`tChar`, `tGlow`, `uChar`) and holy ground's ring (up to 33 glows a ground within 60 m, and only while 80 of the 160 are free).
- The HUD: a new panel joins `HUD_ORDER` and `HUD_PREF` in `10p_hud.js` (and `HUD_COMPACT` / `HUD_HIDE` with a `.hl-compact` rule if it can shrink or wait). Touch controls are 40 px at least. Safe areas: `--safe-t`, `--safe-b`, `--safe-l`, `--safe-r` (the HUD sits inside the left and right ones).
- On a desk the layout pass stacks the bottom: the hotbar, the class panel over it, the builder's bar (while open) over both, and the prompt and cast bar lifted over the builder's bar; the log keeps its corner, narrows beside them, or rises (112 px tall) over them, and waits (`.hl-off`) when there is no room; panels in `HUD_HIDE` wait on a desk too when nothing is free. The zone's name keeps its place while it fades.

**What players are told** ([guide], version 13; 10c_lore.js and 10e_guide.js)
- A new system's first-time word is a field note: `Object.assign(FIELD_NOTES, { id: { t, x: (R) => text, fight?, ok? } })` and a watch `GUIDE_WATCH.id = (P, ctx) => bool` (`ctx.prompt` is what the E prompt says now); `loreNote(id)` queues one from anywhere. A note shows once per character, one at a time, never over a window, never in a fight unless `fight: true`, never before the first-steps note, and only while `ok()` holds; text short enough for a phone (about 40 words), the device's own words (`IS_TOUCH ? 'Use' : 'E'`). Prefer a note to a `uiToast(..., 'hint')`: a toast is for what is happening now (move to stop, the grass under you burns).
- Its help: a function returning HTML pushed to `GUIDE_HELP` (10e_guide.js), in the order a player meets it. Its Chronicle page: `chronAdd(key, label, group, fn, after)` (groups `land`, `you`, `war`, `life`); a section of an existing page: push a function to `CHRON_EXTRA.sieges`, `.gear` or `.goals` (siege engines, palisades and burning towers belong on Sieges). Its tips: `LOADING_TIPS.push(...)`. A deed worth a reward: a step in `PATH_STEPS` with `lv`, `pv` (the new `PATH_VERSION`), `old` if an old character past it should have it quietly, a hint, a reward, its event in `loreWatch`, and its place in `guideMarkOf`.
- Field notes and toasts live in `#banners`. While a field note shows, the HUD's layout places it on an upright phone right after the target frame and the class panel, and the path tracker right after it (`HUD_ORDER_NOTE`; on a phone on its side the note right after the target frame, `HUD_ORDER_NOTE_WIDE`): a note lasts seconds, the panels that stay (the companion bar, the war panel; sideways the class panel too) make room, shrink or wait for it; a toast alone keeps its old place after them (`HUD_ORDER`), so the panels you reach for do not jump at every toast; and a new banner is laid out in the frame it appears (`HUDL.k = 0`), so a note is always on the screen and never lies over a panel (placed after the class panel and the companion bar, most notes shrank to their titles on an upright phone): when the lane will not fit, the older banners wait (`hl-old`) and the newest shows; when even that will not fit (a 360x640 phone, a 667x375 one on its side) it shrinks to its title (`#banners` is in `HUD_COMPACT`: "Field note · tap" over its title), and a tap on it opens your path in the Chronicle, where every note seen is kept, the newest first. On a phone on its side a note is 300 px wide, beside the frames. The menu's Resume and New character stay at the foot of the sheet (sticky), and How to play folds each system's block under its heading (`guideFold`).
- [war2] (version 15) New things a player meets that nothing tells them of yet (for the guide: a field note, a line on the Chronicle's Fire and land and Sieges pages, a loading tip): townsfolk run to a burning house and beat it out, and **Use beside a burning house's walls beats the flames down** ('Beat out the flames', the same prompt as on a burh's walls; desk and touch); a house saved stands black under its eaves for a few minutes, and the log says "The people of <town> have beaten out the fire."; a fire that has taken the whole roof is past saving. The war's news has a new reason for a march ("marches on <keep> through the breach in its walls": a second band sent to a keep with a breach open) and a new way in ("<band> goes in by the gap burnt in the log wall of the Hall of the Ring!", the phase word 'going in by the gap'): a gap burnt in the Thule hallow's log ring is a way in once Thule has lost a keep, and the hallow may be taken while it is open. A band waits by a fallen tower until it is strong enough for the garrison it will meet (half again its number), or a second band comes, or a minute has passed. The long war's news says a hold on the March counts for more the longer the war lasts (a claim grows up to 1.6 times as fast from 15 to 40 minutes: the claim's countdown on the war panel follows it). A broken gate is kept with the world (a player who saves at a burnt-down gate finds it so). A new campaign begins with every keep's garrison whole. Soldiers no longer stand about on the walls, in towers' rooms or in a fallen tower's rubble after a fight, nor walk into walls: a band always gets on with its order or fights (the war panel's orders move).
- [guide2] (version 15, 10f_guide2.js) A Chronicle page is written so it folds on a phone (`chronFold`): a lead paragraph first, then `<h3>` sections (each folds under its heading), cards (`.card`, folded under their head and first paragraph when tall) or tiers (a `<div>` whose first child is an `<h4>`); a figure stays open (it is a summary); what the page says of now and its links go at its end with the classes `now`, `acts`, `tide`, `live`, `fineprint` or `skills` (they stay open); `data-cf-open` on an `<h3>` or a tier opens it by default; a long run before the first section takes its title from `G2_INTRO[page]`. Text in a closed fold is still in the page (`textContent`), so tests that read a page keep working; a test that taps inside a page scrolls the control into view first.
- [guide2] A note id is one module's: `G2_NOTES` adds its notes only where the id is free (an `Object.assign` with an id already taken replaced the portal stones' note in this work's first build). A deed done at no place (a step whose place `guideMarkOf` cannot give) gets a pointer: `g2Pointer(step)` names the button that does it (it glows, `.g2-point`) and what to say in the tracker. The war panel ends with your aim (`guideWarAimHTML`). Prefer a field note to a hint toast for anything a player meets once (the hall's greeting and the farmers' tips were toasts).
- [guide2] No panel moves under a finger: `hudLayout` waits while `g2TapHeld()` (a finger down on a panel the layout moves or the prompt, until that press's own click lands; `G2.tap.pending` is set by any press on the HUD and cleared by its click, and only that click lets the layout go on; a press never shortens a longer hold already asked for). A test that measures a HUD control and then taps it first waits for the last tap's click (`!G2.tap.pending`), lays out, holds the layout (`G2.tap.until = performance.now() + 30000`: a hold of 4 s ran out on a loaded machine between the measure and the finger), measures, taps, waits for its own click before it reads the world, and lets the hold go (touch_flows.py's `tap()`); a test that lays the HUD out by hand clears the hold (`G2.tap.until = 0`: guide.py's and guide2.py's `lay()`).

### Gotchas

- Grass and flowers are rebuilt when quality changes. Anything rebuilt at runtime must go back into the right group; see `regroupOutdoors`.
- Do not shadow common globals inside functions; a stray `P` in `gatherLights` once broke the build.
- Under SwiftShader, screenshots need a long timeout (300 s), and in-page timers lag behind wall time.
- The building shader in `04_props.js` is patched by other modules at anchor strings. Keep these lines intact, or update the patches with them:
  - the houses patch, which throws at boot if an anchor is missing;
  - the castles patch at `void main(){` and `vec3 col = lightSurface(alb, n, vAO);`, which silently loses its cracks instead, and ([keeps]) at `float wSnow = snowAmount(vW, n, uSnowCover * snowHere(vW), gPL) * (m == 14 ? 0.0 : 1.0);`, which silently lets snow and the wet film onto the floors inside the towers;
  - `alb *= 1.0 - 0.22 * uWet;`.
  - the building pieces' shader (`06k_build.js`, throws at boot if an anchor is missing): `attribute vec3 aCol;` and `vec4 w = modelMatrix * vec4(position, 1.0);` in the vertex shader, `uniform float uFlash, uWet, uSnowCover;`, `void main(){` and `vec3 col = lightSurface(alb, n, vAO);` in the fragment shader.
- [walls] The castle shader's cut (`CASTLE_CUT_GLSL`) includes `TBREACH_GLSL` from 04g_breach.js (it loads first), and both of its discards (`CASTLE_FS` and `CASTLE_DEPTH_FS`) ask `towerBreachCut` as well as `castleCut` and `towerCut`; the castle material and `castleCutUniforms` carry `uKA`, `uKB`, `uKC`. The tower fire's char (`tChar`) is charcoal with checks and ash, its embers (`tGlow`) glow in the checks (a burnt-out tower's fade over a couple of minutes: `T.fallen.ember`). A tower's hatch lids and its gallery's boards are drawn in the plaster branch tinted as wood (the wood branch's planks run along world x on a level face), and the gallery's boards butt up against each other (gaps between boards out in the sun caught light the roof's shadow could not reach).
- [siege3] The castle shader's cut also includes `BLAZE_GLSL` (04g_blaze.js, loads before 04g_castles.js): both discards (`CASTLE_FS`, `CASTLE_DEPTH_FS`) ask `deckCut(vW, vMat)` too, and the castle material and `castleCutUniforms` carry `uDS`, `uDY`, `uDA`, `uDB` (a burh's walk in zones of cells, a timber hallow's ring in sectors). `deckCut` writes the same `tChar`, `tGlow` and `tFresh` the towers and palisades do, and touches only the wood (1) and log (13; a hallow's ring also 5) ids. The timber hallow's two meshes are drawn with the castle material now (its own `cutU`). The siege engines' charring shader (`siegeMat`) also draws a burh's or the timber hallow's gate leaves while they burn or stand charred, and the walks' wrecks.
- `09u_depth.js` adds pieces to `BP`, `B_MODEL`, `B_ICON` and `BP_ORDER` at load (hip roof, lean-to, well) and replaces `B_MODEL.window` with leaded glazing; it also adds recipes to `RECIPES`. The forage shader (`FG_VS`/`FG_FS` in `09s_survival.js`) is patched at `attribute vec4 iPos;`, `float c = cos(iPos.w), s = sin(iPos.w);`, `vO = position;`, `varying vec3 vW;` and `vec3 col = lightSurface(alb, n, ao);` (an error is reported if one is missing).
- Keep halls are houses (`H.keepHall`): code that walks `HOUSES` meets them too (fire, lightning, the cover map, the save). Tests that count town houses filter them out (`houses.py`).
- A material id (`aMat`) is interpolated across a triangle whose corners carry different ids (a sheep's face meeting its fleece); the farm beasts' shader sends a blended id to the nearer of the two (`farmBeastFS`, version 9), so whole ids such as horn and udder are only ever read exactly. Keep separate materials on separate pieces of geometry where you can.
- [water2] `GLSL_ATMOS` declares `uUnder` and `uUnderCol` (02_render.js's `U`): a material made without `mat()` that includes it gets 0 (no underwater fog) unless it adds them. The composite's fragment shader is patched by 07d_dive.js at `uniform sampler2D tScene, tBloom, tRays;` and `vec3 col = clamp(finite3(texture2D(tScene, vUv).rgb), 0.0, 8.0);` (an error is reported if one is missing). The wildlife's leaping fish are `FISH`/`buildFish` (08b_wildlife.js); the diving fish are `DIVE.schools`/`buildDvFish`.
- The human crowd uses 15 of the 16 guaranteed vertex attributes. Pack new per-instance data into spare channels rather than adding attributes.
- [siege2] `addFire` adds no timed fire while 60 are in the list (40 until version 13's fitting together, when seven burning stretches of palisade filled it; a stretch keeps three flames at most, `PAL_FLAMES`), and only `drawFires` (each frame) lets the burnt-out ones go: a long `__hm.simulate` piles them up, so a burning thing that adds its flames during one gets none after the first few seconds. Code that keeps its own fires lets the burnt-out ones go first (`fireLetGo`, 04g_burn.js) and pushes `FIRES[FIRES.length - 1]` only when the list grew (a capped call left another fire's entry there, which it later put out as its own): `palisadeBurnStep`, the tower fire.
- [walls] Particles in tests: `__hm.simulate` runs the particles' clock (`U.uTime`) on ahead while the frames' clock (`renderTime`) stands still, and the next `tick` sets `U.uTime` back to `renderTime`. Whatever was emitted while simulating is born in the frames' future and shows later, frozen where it was emitted: a column of smoke over a fire that burnt during the simulation, a scatter of pale dust along a wall that was struck, in a picture taken minutes later. A test that simulates and then takes a picture should let those go after its first frame (`walls.py`'s `shot()` drops every particle born after `U.uTime`). A particle keeps the colour it is given: dust is pale at night unless it is lit as the light is (`wallLit`, or the ambient-lit colours of `05h_wear.js`).

## Balance of the callings

Measured with `python3 tools/tests/classes.py balance` (see Testing): fighters with a player's own measure and fair gear for the level, played by the simple bot (presses 1-8 in turn) and the skilled bot (07c_balance.js). TTK: the time to kill a standard foe (a garrison warrior of the level). Pack: the time survived against an endless pack of three wolves and two outlaws that never loses its quarry, every newcomer 1% stronger for each second the fight has lasted (a survivor of the 150 s cap scores 1.5 times). Duels: wins against the other three callings, both sides on the same bot (draws count half). The aim: the skilled bots within about ±15% of each other on every measure at every level, the simple bots within about ±25%, and the skilled bot clearly beating its simple self (mastery duels).

### Version 15 ([balance7])

**What a player meets** (the Callings page, the tooltips and the loading tips say it; 07c_balance7.js, 10d_classes_ui.js):
- The archers' realm ways are edges on their own ground, at their old full measure there and nothing elsewhere: an archer of Logres reaches full draw a quarter sooner and strikes 20% harder with a drawn shot from a wall, a tower or any perch; one of Ériu hides in half the time, stays hidden while it walks and strikes 80% harder from hiding (instead of 50%) among trees; one of Thule shoots arrows the wind does not move and lays snares that freeze what they catch and hold it 4.5 s in the cold (snow lying under it, or the air at freezing: its own lands most of the year, the heights, the winter), and anywhere strikes chilled and frozen foes 10% harder.
- A warrior's Charge comes round every 12 s at every level, and its landing blow slows the foe by 40% for 2.5 s at every level (from Leap Strike's level it waited 16 s and did not slow); Charge and Leap Strike are still one rush, but either holds the other back 5 s (10 s).
- Plain arrows strike 5% harder for each rank past the first (5% at 30, 10% at 40, 15% at 50): a seasoned archer's own shafts.
- Mend on a scratch heals barely a sixth of its measure (a third): mend at need.
- For the Chronicle's Callings page (10d_classes_ui.js; for whoever shortens it for a phone): the archer's realm-way line (`CLASS_REALM.archer[r].text`, built in 07c_balance7.js from `ARCHER_GROUND`) now names its ground first; the Charge row says every 12 s and the slow at every level and takes the share from `B7T.rushShare`; the Mend row "barely a sixth of it on a scratch"; the Arrows row the plain shafts' rank (from `B7T.plainRank`); three loading tips (the realm ways' ground, Charge every 12 s, Mend a scratch). On an upright phone (390x780, touch) the page is 9,227 to 9,417 characters and 6,871 to 6,976 px tall by realm (version 14: 9,297 and 6,956), with no sideways overflow.
- The skilled bots (the Hollow King check, the measures) play better: a warrior goes through burning grass after its foe (it stepped out of every fire a staff or a fire arrow lit, and lost the chase), everyone but a warrior steps out of a foe's volley before its second wave (a drawn arrow loosed first), and a priest in a duel of rank I mends lower and smites instead (it mended at 55-62% of its health and starved itself of power and harm: 3-8 harm a second of smiting, where the simple priest smote 25-31).

**The measures: open arenas, whole ground for every duel.** Version 14's duels stood in eleven arenas 90 m apart, four of them with 8 to 34 trees within 20 m of the middle. Now they stand on open ground (no more than two trees within 20 m and six within 30 m), 80 m apart: nine arenas (`duels.py` and `classes.py balance` by default; `wooded` for version 14's). And the meadow round each arena grows back as each duel ends (`CB.wholeGrass`): a duel's fire arrows, firebolts, meteors and burning brands burnt its grass, and every later duel there was fought on ash, where no grass catches and no skilled bot steps out of the flames. On ash the skilled warrior won 22 of 48 duels against a mage at 50, on whole grass 9 (the same build, in one run). Every table of version 14 was measured on ash after the first few duels in each arena. The duel scope now tells each bot kind apart (`kind=both` mixed them) and how long each side ran short of power (`dry`).

Found and left in the measures: **where in a run the skilled warrior's duels fall decides its share at 50.** In the full table (`kind=both`: the simple duels of all six pairings first, then the skilled ones) it won 22% and 27% of its 144 duels in two runs of version 14; its three pairings run alone at the start of a run 54% and, run again at once, 48%; after 144 simple duels of its own pairings 50%; after 144 simple duels of the other three pairings 49% (`tools/out`'s experiment runner, one boot each). Nothing came from outside a duel (`FOREIGN` 0 in every run), the fighters were the same (health, gear), the arenas whole, no snares, runes, holy ground, craters or burning grass left in them; mobs wandered within 60 m of some arenas (a wolf, a bear, skeletons, draugr) but struck no one. The other callings' shares moved by the noise between these runs. Read the warrior's number at 50 as somewhere between a quarter and a half until this is found; the tables below are the full table's, before and after alike.

Found in the measures: **the side of a duel made first acts first in every step**, and wins a mirror of archers by it. The world's lists are walked in the order bodies were made, so of two archers alike in all else drawing together, the one made first looses first every time. A duel's A side was always made first: the first calling of a pair (the warrior in each of its pairings, the priest in none), the realm's way in `kind=flavour`, the skilled bot in `kind=mastery`. Two archers of no realm's way: the A side won 51 of 72 at 50 (71%); two priests 30 of 72 (42%). The skilled archer of 50 beat its simple self 23 of 24 made first, 14 made second. Each realm's way of the archer won 16 to 20.5 of 24 against the plain archer made first and 6 to 8 made second (version 14's 75-92% was mostly this, and "an edge that is always there wins a race" was the order of making). `duels.py alt` makes every other duel's B side first, `plain=a` puts the plain calling on the A side, `plain=ab` makes both plain. The before and after tables below are made first (A side) alike, so they compare; mastery and the realm's ways are also given with `alt`.

How it was measured: each level a run of the full table (48 duels a pair, both bots: a calling's share over 144 duels, about ±4 points; a pair's over 48, about ±7), then 24 mastery duels a calling and 24 duels a realm's way against the plain calling (`kind=flavour`, all four callings), one boot for all three levels (50, then 30, then 10), on a machine shared with three other builders (load 9 to 18 on 2 CPUs; a level's full table took 4 to 8 minutes). The commands:

```
python3 tools/tests/duels.py levels=50,30,10 reps=48 kind=both tap lastfall     # the full table (a level a run, or all three)
python3 tools/tests/duels.py levels=50,30,10 reps=24 kind=mastery tap lastfall  # each skilled bot against its simple self (made first)
python3 tools/tests/duels.py levels=50,30,10 reps=24 kind=mastery alt lastfall  # ... made first and second by turns: the fair measure
python3 tools/tests/duels.py levels=50,30,10 reps=24 kind=flavour alt           # each realm's way against the plain calling, by turns
python3 tools/tests/duels.py levels=50 reps=24 kind=flavour only=archer plain=ab # two plain archers: the lean of the side made first
HM_BAL=tools/out/bal.json python3 tools/tests/classes.py balance together noduel mreps=0 reps=3 levels=10,30,50   # time to kill, the pack
python3 tools/tests/classes.py b7                                               # the rules of version 15 by number (also in mech)
```
