The archer kills sooner at 30 and 50 (plain shafts by rank; the skilled one 3.5 and 2.6 s against 4.0 and 3.1) and falls sooner to the pack (33 and 34 s against 50 and 49, -31% and -37% of the mean): the pack stands on open ground, where no realm's way of the archer helps it now (a Logres archer's quicker draw, Ériu's harder shot from hiding and Thule's longer snares were always on; the pack's times move by a third between runs). The skilled warrior's time to kill at 30 and 50 is still the slowest (+24% and +18%), as in version 14; the skilled mage the quickest (-19% at 10 and 30).

What changed in version 15 ([balance7]: 07c_balance7.js; small marked edits to 07b_classes.js, 07c_balance.js, 07c_balance3.js, 07c_balance4.js, 07c_balance5.js, 07_entities.js and 10d_classes_ui.js; the measures in classes.py and duels.py), and why:

- **The measures** (above): open arenas, whole grass after every duel, each bot kind told apart, how long a side ran short of power, and the lean of the side made first (`alt`). On ash every skilled bot fought without its first answer to fire (it steps out of burning grass), and a warrior chasing a staff through the flames lost the chase.
- **The archers' realm ways on their own ground** (`ARCHER_GROUND`): every version from 12 to 14 made them smaller and the measure did not move (four duels in five against the plain archer), because the measure put the realm's way on the side made first, which wins 71% of an archers' mirror by itself. A way at its old full measure where it belongs (Logres's perch, Ériu's trees, Thule's cold) and nothing on open ground gives each realm's archer a place to seek and a place to avoid, and leaves the open-ground duels to the calling: the three ways come out even with the plain archer there (above), and the archers' shares at 30 and 50 within the band.
- **Charge every 12 s, its blow slowing, at every level** (`chargeCdLate`, `chargeSlowLate`): from Leap Strike's level Charge waited 16 s and its landing blow did not slow, and the skilled warrior of 50 won a quarter of its duels (22% and 27%): it reached a bow or a staff and the foe walked away from it.
- **One rush of 5 s** (`rushShare`; 10 s): with Charge every 12 s and its slow, the skilled warrior of 50 still won 29% (42 of 144); with Charge and Leap Strike holding each other back 5 s, 35% (50 of 144), and at 30 54% (43%). The simple warrior presses both in turn and gains more (53% to 70% at 50, 57% to 74% at 30): within its band, at its top.
- **Plain shafts by rank** (`plainRank` 0.05: 5% a rank past the first, 15% at 50): the simple archer shoots plain arrows only and won 19-20% of its duels at 50 before; 35% with plain shafts 10% harder from rank II, 44% now. The skilled archer looses broadheads and bodkins at most foes (54% at 50, 57% before).
- **Mend on a scratch** (`MEND_WOUND.floor` 0.15; 0.3): a Mend cast by habit on a priest at nine tenths of its health mended a third of its measure, so the simple priest's heals pressed in turn lost little. The skilled priest mends at need and does not feel it.
- **The skilled bots**: a warrior after its foe goes through burning grass (`botFireThrough`: it stepped out of every fire a staff or a fire arrow lit under it, and the foe walked on); everyone else steps out of a foe's volley before its second wave, a drawn arrow loosed first (`botVolleyAt`: a skilled archer in a mirror stood drawing under all three waves); and a priest in a duel of rank I mends 0.15 lower and smites instead (`priestRace`: it mended from 55-62% of its health and smote 3-8 harm a second where the simple priest smote 25-31; the skilled priest of 10 won 31% of its duels before, 35% now).

Tried and set aside (each measured in a full table or in the pairings it touches, one run each):
- NPC priests keeping the devotion they come to the field with (`npcDevotion`): the war's priests (and the bots, made as NPCs) faded their 20 to 50 devotion at the first look (`devAt` -99); kept, the priests won 72% of their duels at 50, skilled and simple. A rule for a pass that weighs the war's healers too.
- Plain shafts 8%, 10% and 12% harder from rank II alike: at 12% the simple archer of 30 won 47.5 of 48 against the simple priest; by rank, the archer of 30 gains a third of what the archer of 50 does.
- A priest's own healing at 70% at rank I with its race at 0.25 (76% and 0.15): the simple priest of 10 won 79% (81%) and the skilled one 30% (35%): it cost the careful priest more than the careless one.
- Sanctuary on yourself weighed by the wound as Mend is (`sanctWound`): the skilled priest uses it at 70% of its health and lost as much as the simple one at 30.
- A priest's bot renewing for steadiness only when held, not when slowed (`priestSnareRenew` false): the careful priest of 30 lost its steadiness against a bow's snares.
- A priest's bot smiting through a ready bash or after a cut spell (`priestBait`), a skilled archer volleying a foe that stands (`archerVolley`), Leap Strike's landing slowing (`leapSlow`), light feet at 25% (`lightFeet`: it stays 0.15), a seasoned mage's frost not holding Charge (`deepFrost` false): none moved its pairings beyond the noise, or each moved one calling out of the band as it moved another in.

Where it stands against the aim (skilled duels 35% to 65%, simple 25% to 75%, the skilled bot beating its simple self 18 of 24 or more):

- Of the 24 shares (four callings, three levels, two bots) 22 are within the band (19 before): the skilled priest of 10 (31% to 35%), the skilled archer of 30 (66% to 52%), the skilled warrior of 50 (22% and 27% to 35% and 43%), the simple archer of 50 (19% and 20% to 44% and 50%) came in, and the simple mage of 10 went out (28% to 24%); the simple priest of 10 stays out (81%). At the band's edges: the skilled warrior of 50 (35% in one run of two) and the skilled priest of 10 (35%), the simple warrior of 30 (74%) and of 50 (70% and 69%).
- Mastery, measured fairly (`alt`): the warrior 23 or 24 of 24 at every level, the archer and the mage 18 and 19 at 50; short of 18 the archer (16 and 14) and the mage (15.5 and 17) at 10 and 30, and the priest at every level (16, 14, 15). Made first, as the before was measured, the archer and the mage at 30 and 50 and the priest at 10 reached it (19 to 23; before: 20 to 22 at 30 and 50, the priest 13 at 50).
- The time to kill and the pack (skilled): the time to kill within about ±20% of the mean but the warrior at 30 (+24%), as in version 14; the pack: the archer falls first at 30 and 50 (-31%, -37%), the warrior holds out longest at 30 (+56%).

Still off (for the next pass):

- **The skilled warrior's share at 50 hangs on where its duels fall in a run** (above; version 14's table, its pairings alone about half). Find what a run's earlier duels leave behind (the world's lists, a body's state that is not reset, the time of day: the run's clock is 25 to 60 minutes on) before tuning the warrior at 50 again.
- **The simple priest at 10** (81%): it beats the simple archer 41.5 of 48 and the simple mage 40, healing 35-43 a second (Mend 19-26) against their 68-72 harm a second; the priest's own healing lower and its race longer cost the careful priest more (tried, above). The simple mage of 10 (24%) is its other side (8 of 48 against it).
- **The simple warrior's pairings** at 30 and 50: it beats the simple archer 48 of 48 and the simple mage 47 of 48 at both levels, and loses to the simple priest 36-42 of 48 (its share is in the band, its pairings are not).
- **Mastery in the mirrors, measured fairly** (`alt`, above): the skilled archer, mage and priest beat their simple selves only 14 to 17 times in 24 at 10 and 30, and the priest at 50 too. Between two archers or two casters alike in all else a duel at 10 is a race of 6 to 7 s (at 10 the skilled archer dealt 65 harm a second to its simple self's 64, the skilled mage 71 to 59); the careful priest's edge is its timing, which a mirror of two healers spends slowly (its simple self renews by rote and smites in turn). Rules that weigh on the careless hand in a mirror (a Renewal by rote, a spell cast into a ready interrupt, a draw loosed at a foe out of its line) are the next thing to try.
- The archer against the pack on open ground at 30 and 50 (33 and 34 s, the others 40 to 74): its realm's ways no longer help it there.
- The measures: the lean of the side made first (above) is in every table of versions 9 to 15, mastery and the realm's ways above all; measure the next with `alt` (a before for it: version 15 with `alt`).

### Version 14 ([balance6])

**What a player meets** (the Callings page, the tooltips and the loading tips say it; 10d_classes_ui.js, 07c_balance6.js): a priest that renews itself while its last Renewal still has more than 2 s to mend pays twice the power for it (renew at need: the steadfastness it gives is as before, 4 s in which nothing but a stun breaks your spells), and its own wounds mend at 76% of a heal's measure at rank I (70%); a mage's Arcane Ward keeps its spell: while it holds, a blow that would break the spell (a blunt arrow, a storm, a mace, a flare of holy fire) breaks on the ward instead, for a quarter of what the ward has left ("Warded" over the player's head); a seasoned mage's spells strike 12% harder from rank II and 2% more for each rank after (7% a rank); a warrior's Charge held on its way (a snare, a frost, a wall, the water) comes round again in half its wait, and until Leap Strike its blow slows by 40% for 2.5 s (45% for 3 s); the Well of Ériu's blessing heals 1.2% a second for 15 s beside the Renewal it comes with (its 2.5% merged into that Renewal and gave little); the archers' realm ways are smaller (Logres draws 8% sooner, Ériu's shot from hiding strikes 55% harder, Thule's arrows drift three quarters as much and its snares hold 3.75 s, and it strikes chilled foes 5% harder). The skilled bots (the Hollow King check, the measures) play better.

**The measures: arenas far apart, a queue in each.** Version 13's duels stood 52 m apart within 170 m of one centre (`CB.spots`, made for the pack, whose beasts and outlaws rest beyond 170 m of the player), and one arena reached the next: its meteors, stray arrows and burning grass (a grass fire spreads up to 37 m from where it is lit in still air, twice that downwind), and the skilled bots stepping out of their neighbours' fire (688 steps in one run of mastery duels at 30, none in a mirror run alone) decided the mirrors. A bot does not rest far from the player, so `duels.py` (and `classes.py balance`'s own duels) now fight in `CB.spotsWide`: open, dry ground anywhere on the homelands' ring, at least 90 m apart (`gap=`; 11 arenas), flat within 24 m and with no water within 36 m (a skilled archer kited by a priest into a lake at an arena's rim, where a swimmer cannot shoot, dealt nothing in 8 duels of 48 at 30: `CB.spots` checked only 19 m out). A firebreak puts out every burning cell more than 34 m from every arena's middle each simulated second (`balBreakFires`, 25 m round the pack's arenas). And each arena runs its own queue (`DS.queue`, `DS.step`): when a duel ends its two fighters go, the arena is made whole (`balCleanArena`: their snares, runes, holy ground, steam, arrows and bolts in flight, and within 36 m the ice, blizzards, craters and burning grass) and the next duel begins there at once, while the others fight on (a phase of 18 waited on its slowest duel, up to a minute, with most of its arenas empty): 576 duels at 10 take about 2 minutes, at 30 and 50 about 8 on a shared machine. Found on the way: a Thule mage's blizzard lays its rune 6.6 s after it, by then from a mage removed from the world (`getEnt` failed and the rune burst as no one's under the next duel's fighters, 'none:mage' in `FOREIGN`): a caster gone from the world lays no rune now (`layRune`). `tap` shows no harm from outside a duel but a duel's own grass fire.

How it was measured: 48 duels a pair a level (`reps=48`: a calling's rate over 144 duels, about ±4 points; a pair's over 48, about ±7), both bots in one run (`kind=both`), mastery 24 duels a calling (`kind=mastery reps=24`, read as wins of 24), the realm's ways 24 duels a realm (`kind=flavour only=archer,priest`), on a machine shared with three other builders (load 8 to 15 on 2 CPUs). Two runs of the same build at 30 moved the mage by 10 points (31% and 41%): read a number over two runs before tuning on it. The commands:

```
python3 tools/tests/duels.py levels=10 reps=48 kind=both tap        # skilled and simple duels (and levels=30, levels=50: a level a run)
python3 tools/tests/duels.py levels=10,30,50 reps=24 kind=mastery tap lastfall   # each skilled bot against its simple self (a mutual kill to the last to fall)
python3 tools/tests/duels.py levels=10,30,50 reps=24 kind=flavour only=archer,priest   # each realm's way against the plain calling
HM_BAL=tools/out/bal.json python3 tools/tests/classes.py balance together noduel mreps=0 reps=3 levels=10,30,50   # time to kill, the pack
python3 tools/tests/classes.py b6                                    # the rules of version 14 by number (also in mech)
```

**Before** (the merged version 13 in these arenas; at 50 and in the mastery duels a Thule blizzard's late rune still burst in about one duel in twenty). A calling's duels won of 144, and each pair (the first calling's wins of 48):

| Level, bot | Warrior | Archer | Mage | Priest | w–a | w–m | w–p | a–m | a–p | m–p |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 10 skilled | 93 (65%) | 73 (51%) | 71.5 (50%) | 50.5 (35%) | 36 | 22 | 35 | 28.5 | 32.5 | 26 |
| 10 simple | 90 (62%) | 50 (35%) | 39 (27%) | 109 (76%) | 39 | 35 | 16 | 34 | 7 | 12 |
| 30 skilled | 69.5 (48%) | 67 (47%) | 59 (41%) | 92.5 (64%) | 13 | 26 | 30.5 | 27 | 5 | 16 |
| 30 simple | 89 (62%) | 68 (47%) | 50 (35%) | 81 (56%) | 47 | 37 | 5 | 33 | 34 | 24 |
| 50 skilled | 54.5 (38%) | 58 (40%) | 87 (60%) | 88.5 (61%) | 13 | 14 | 27.5 | 20 | 3 | 25 |
| 50 simple | 86 (60%) | 34.5 (24%) | 76.5 (53%) | 91 (63%) | 48 | 36 | 2 | 16.5 | 18 | 33 |

Mastery (the skilled bot's wins of 24 against its simple self): at 10 warrior 23, archer 17, mage 12.5, priest 19; at 30 23, 24, 16, 14; at 50 24, 24, 18, 17.5.

What changed in version 14 ([balance6]: 07c_balance6.js, the bots in 07c_balance.js, small marked edits to 07_entities.js, 07b_classes.js, 07c_balance4.js, 07c_balance5.js, 10_ui.js and 10d_classes_ui.js; the measures in classes.py and duels.py), and why:

- **The measures** (above): arenas far apart, dry and with a firebreak; a queue in each; the pack on open ground within 140 m of the player (`CB.packOpen`: in a grove of thirty trees the beasts and outlaws stuck on the trunks while an archer shot them down, and the archer outlasted every pack at every level, skilled and simple: a pack's number in a wooded arena measures the trees); `lastfall` scoring; a removed caster lays no rune.
- **Renewing by rote costs power** (`renewRoteCost`): a Renewal on yourself while your last one still has more than 2 s to mend costs twice the power (it only takes the old one's place: a buff of the same kind and source is refreshed, the heal left in it lost). The simple priest (pressing 1-8, renewing on every turn) won 76% of its duels at 10, and in the mirror at 30 its steadfastness from renewing by rote turned two of the skilled priest's flares in three; the skilled priest renews for healing when the last has run out, and pays the price when it renews for steadiness.
- **A priest's own wounds at rank I mend at 76% of a heal's measure** (70%): the skilled priest of 10 won 35% of its duels (a warrior and a bow three in four), while the simple one, which the rote's price now holds back, won 76%.
- **The ward keeps the spell** (`wardTakesBreak`): a mage's answer to the breaking blows a priest has its Renewal for: a seasoned bow's blunt heads broke 84 of the mage's spells in 48 duels at 30.
- **The seasoned mage's step** (`MAGE_RANK`: 12% from rank II, then 2% a rank; 7% a rank before): the mage won 31-41% of its skilled duels at 30 and 60% at 50.
- **A held rush comes round sooner** (`rushHeld`): from Leap Strike's level a third of the skilled warrior's Charges were held on their way (85 of 268 at 50) and it won 38% of its duels at 50 (33% in after2), a quarter against a bow.
- **The early rush's slow, 40% for 2.5 s** (45% for 3 s): the skilled warrior won 65% of its duels at 10, three in four against a bow or a priest.
- **The Well of Ériu's own heal** (`WELL_SRC`): its heal over time was laid with the priest as its source, the Renewal's own, and a buff of the same kind and source is refreshed, not added: the Well's 2.5% a second merged into the Renewal it came with (the greater each second, for 15 s instead of 9). It runs beside the Renewal now, at 1.2% a second.
- **The archers' realm ways evened out again** (`ARCHER_REALM`): each still beat an archer of no realm's way in 71-81% of their duels at 10 and 30 (Thule 88% at 50); each keeps its character at about half its measure (what that did to a mirror: below).
- **The skilled bots**: the archer takes a priest for a blade at its throat only at its mace from Tumble's level (it kited from one keeping 8 m off for its flare and never drew: it won 5 and 3 of 48 against the priest at 30 and 50) and casts its long shots with a priest close by when the priest's flare is spent; the mage meets a staff at its elbow with its nova and then a blink away (the simple mage blinks at its foe and novas: the mirror at 10 was a coin toss, 12.5 of 24), and keeps its meteors and blizzards for a foe that cannot step out of them (its meteors did 14 harm a second against a bow at 30: a skilled bow tumbles from under them); the priest keeps a warrior dazzled from rank II (at 10 it smote instead of mending and lost more), keeps its Holy Fire for a flare at most 3 s, and the bots see a warded mage's spell as a steadfast one.
- **What a player reads**: the Renewal slot on the hotbar shows the rote's price (twice its own) while your last Renewal still has more than 2 s to mend and you are its target (`updateHUD`, 10_ui.js); the tooltips of Charge, Arcane Ward and Renewal, the realm's ways of the archer and the Well of Ériu (their texts from the numbers), a loading tip for each new rule, and the Chronicle's Callings page, whose Charge, Ward and Steadfast rows carry the new rules in fewer words and whose Shield, Arrows and Light feet rows are tightened (every rule and number kept): the page is a little shorter than version 13's on a phone (9,297 characters and 6,956 px tall at 390x780 with touch, against 9,453 and 7,112; no sideways overflow there or at 1280x720, no errors; `guide.py` reads it on a phone too).

Tried and set aside:
- A Renewal on yourself steadying only the next spell begun, in every way (after1): the skilled priest won 17% of its duels at 10, 4 of 48 against a warrior (blows set back every spell after the first), and the mirror at 30 went no better (14 of 24).
- The next spell begun proof against breaking, the 4 s against set-backs as before (after2): the skilled priest of 10 won 21% (a bow's blunt heads broke every spell after the first: 98 in 48 duels), the mirror at 30 10.5 of 24.
- Renewing by rote at three times the power, the skilled priest paying the rote's price for steadiness only when rooted or with power to spare (after4): at 10 the skilled priest fell from 50% of its duels to 38% (a warrior won 35 of 48) and the simple priest stayed at 77%; the run was stopped at 10.

Where it stands against the aim (skilled within about ±15%, for duels a calling's share between 35% and 65%; simple within about ±25%, 25% to 75%; mastery 18 of 24 or more):

- Skilled duels: every calling within the band at every level (10: 46-55%, 30: 41-65%, 50: 42-55%), from four outside or at its edges before (the warrior at 10, 65%, and 50, 38%; the priest at 10, 35%, and 30, 64%). The pairs: every one between 15 and 33.5 of 48 (before 3 to 37): the archer against the priest from 5 and 3 of 48 at 30 and 50 to 30.5 and 17, the warrior against the priest at 10 from 35 to 27.
- Simple duels: within the band at 30 (44-59%); at 10 the simple priest 80% (76%) and the simple mage 22% (27%) stay outside, and at 50 the simple archer 24% (24%).
- Mastery: the warrior 24 of 24 at every level, the archer 21 and 21.5 at 30 and 50, the mage 21 and 20, the priest 19 at 10; below 18: the archer and the mage at 10 (14.5 and 16) and the priest at 30 and 50 (15.5 and 16).
- The time to kill (skilled): within ±16% at 30 and 50 but the mage at 50 (-18%); at 10 the mage the quickest (-19%, as in every version since 10). The pack (skilled, on open ground): within ±20% at 10 and 50 but the archer at 10 (+28%); at 30 the warrior outlasts it longest (+36%) and the priest falls first (-19%).

Still off (for the next pass):

- The simple bots at 10: the simple priest wins 80% (the rote's price did not hold it back as hoped: its power runs short in a duel of 11 s either way) and the simple mage 22% (it blinks at its foe and casts into the simple priest's mace, which breaks a spell one blow in two). A rule that helps a staff against a mace in reach, or a mend that weighs more against pressing it by rote, is the next thing to try; and the simple archer at 50 (24%: the simple warrior's rush catches it every time, 48 of 48).
- The priest's mastery at 30 and 50 (15.5 and 16 of 24) and the archer's and the mage's at 10 (14.5, 16): a mirror between two bots alike in their tools is a race the simple rotation runs nearly as well; in the priests' mirror the simple priest smites more and the skilled one waits on its flare. Three forms of a rule against renewing by rote were tried (above); what moved the mage's mastery (12.5, 16 and 18 to 16, 21 and 20) was the skilled bot's play, not a rule.
- Lopsided pairs left (both outside 15-33 of 48): the simple warrior against the simple archer (40.5, 48 and 48 at 10, 30 and 50) and against the simple priest (16, 5 and 4); the simple priest against the simple archer and mage at 10 (41.5 and 42 of 48).
- The realm's ways of the archer still beat a plain archer four duels in five in a mirror (any edge that is always there decides a race between archers alike in all else): a way that is an edge only in its own ground (a wall, the rain, the snow) would read nearer even in this measure.
- The duels' arenas are those of the before (the same 11, so the two tables compare), four of them with 8 to 34 trees within 20 m of the middle: a rush stops at a trunk and a bow kites round them. Open arenas (`treesNear` under 3, as the pack's now are) would want a gap of about 70 m to find 11, and a new before table.
- The noise: a calling's share over 144 duels moves about ±4 points between runs, a pair's over 48 about ±7, a mastery of 24 about ±2.5 wins.

### Version 13 ([balance5])

**What a player meets** (for the Chronicle and the guides; the Callings page and the loading tips say it already, 10d_classes_ui.js): a priest's Holy Fire, cast at a foe within 10 m in the middle of a spell, flares the spell away ("Flared" over the foe; a player whose spell is flared reads "Holy fire flares in your face: your spell is lost"), unless the caster stands steadfast from its own Renewal; a priest's holy ground dazzles the foes standing on it (motes of light over their heads: their weapon blows go wide and a warrior cannot parry); Crippling Arrow leaves a wound for as long as it slows (healing on the foe takes 30% less), and a broadhead's wound is 30% at every rank; a warrior's Whirlwind heals it 2% of its health for each foe each of its two cuts strikes; a priest's own wounds mend at 70% of a heal's measure at rank I (75%); the realm's ways of the archer and the priest are a little smaller (their texts give the numbers). Nothing else a player presses changed; the skilled bots (the Hollow King check, the measures) play better.

**The measures' ground.** Every phase of a run fights in the same arenas, and the fighters leave the ground behind them: a meteor a bowl 2 m deep with a rim of thrown earth (`natureCrater`), a Leap Strike a dent, fire arrows and firebolts burning grass. A bow or a staff standing in a bowl, or shooting over a rim, struck the earth (a drawn arrow meets the ground, `wallsProj` and `shotHit` stop a bolt or a Deadeye at it); Holy Fire, which does not fly, did not care. The same build measured the skilled archer against the priest at 50 at 23 of 24 with the pair alone in fresh arenas and at 0 of 24 late in the full table (version 12: 24 of 24 alone). So every table of versions 9 to 12 at 30 and 50 was measured on pocked ground, where bows and staffs lost duels they would have won (version 11's note "a pair run alone and in the full table once differed by half its duels" was this). Now `CB.wipe` (`classes.py`, used by `duels.py`) undoes every crater and puts out every grass fire before each phase (`__hm.bal5.flattenCraters`). At level 10 nothing craters (meteors come at 15, Leap Strike at 30), so version 12's tables at 10 stand; at 30 and 50 version 12 was measured again on clean ground for the before below, and stood far from its own tables: the skilled archer won 68-72% of its duels (24 of 24 against the priest at both levels) and the skilled priest 31-35%.

How it was measured: each level its own run of `duels.py` (24 duels a pair, both bots in one run: `kind=both`, the simple duels first), mastery 12 duels a calling, on a machine shared with three other builders' runs (load 10 to 28 on 2 CPUs; a level's run took 4 to 7 minutes; two runs were lost to a page killed for memory, which now ends the run at once). The commands:

```
python3 tools/tests/duels.py levels=10 reps=24 kind=both tap                    # skilled and simple duels at 10 (and levels=30, levels=50)
python3 tools/tests/duels.py levels=10,30,50 reps=12 kind=mastery                # each skilled bot against its simple self
python3 tools/tests/duels.py levels=10,50 reps=12 kind=flavour only=archer,priest   # each realm's way against the plain calling
HM_BAL=tools/out/bal.json python3 tools/tests/classes.py balance together noduel mreps=0 reps=2 levels=10,30,50   # time to kill, the pack
python3 tools/tests/duels.py levels=50 reps=24 pairs=archer-priest,archer-mage tap   # a pair alone (fresh arenas)
python3 tools/tests/classes.py b5                                                # the rules of version 13 by number (also in mech)
```

**The mage's own meteor shields its foe** (a finding, not a change of this version): at 30 and 50 the mage takes 8 to 15 s to kill the standing foe where the others take 3 to 5, in version 12 as merged as in version 13 (version 12's own table, measured on its branch before the four branches were fitted together, gave it 3.4 s at 30). Its meteor leaves a bowl 2 m deep under the foe, and its bolts strike the bowl's rim: a bolt's step meets the ground (`shotHit`, 07g_walls.js, merged in version 12; the walls' `ground` count rose by 145 in one phase). With every crater undone before each simulated second the skilled mage kills in 3.2 and 3.6 s at 30, against 13 and 14.6 s as it is (351-391 harm a second against 87-98; the same phase run both ways, twice; the other callings within 0.6 s either way; `classes.py balance noduel mreps=0 levels=30 flat` runs it so). It weighs on the mage's duels at 30 and 50 wherever a foe stands in its bowl, and on a player's mage the same way. **Put right in fitting version 13 together** (row 46): a homing shot whose way to its mark runs into the ground goes over it, by the point 0.6 m above the highest ground in the way, if that is within 5 m of the line (`wallsGroundVia`, `p.via`, 07g_walls.js; `wallsShotClear` holds a shot only for a hill): six firebolts and six frost bolts from 22 m into a fresh 1.8 m bowl did no harm before and land now.

| 50 | 10 / 8 / 7.5 of 12 | 10 / 9 / 11 | 7 / 8 / 5 of 12 | 10 / 7 / 7 |

What changed in version 13 ([balance5]: 07c_balance5.js, the bots in 07c_balance.js, small marked edits to 07_entities.js, 07b_classes.js, 07c_balance3.js and 10d_classes_ui.js; the measures in classes.py and duels.py), and why:

- **The measures' ground** (`balFlattenCraters`, from `CB.wipe`): every crater undone and every grass fire put out before each phase (above). Not a rule of the game, but it moved the tables more than any rule did.
- **Holy Fire flares** (`holyFlare`, asked by `priestView`): cast at a foe within 10 m (`FLARE_REACH`) in the middle of a spell, the spell is lost (`breakCast`: a steadfast priest keeps its spell; a boss is not flared). The priest's interrupt, and where its craft shows: the skilled priest keeps it for a heal or a long spell (`flareWorth`: at a priest only Mend, the Pillar of Dawn or a raising; at anyone else a spell with 0.3 s or more to go), keeps Holy Fire's burn off a caster in its reach that is not steadfast (the flare is worth more), and before a long cast of its own renews itself when a foe's flare is ready (`flareThreat`); the simple priest casts Holy Fire when its turn comes. Over all of casting range (30 m, its first form) it broke a mage's spells as fast as they began and a priest of 10 won 16 of 24 against a mage; within 10 m a mage that keeps its distance is safe from it.
- **Holy ground dazzles** the foes standing on it (`hallowDazzle`, from `stepGrounds`: the `dazzle` of the priest's rank for 1.3 s at a time, renewed while they stand there, the motes too): a blade fighting a priest on its hallowed ground misses some of its blows and cannot parry. The priest's answer at 10, where it has no Divine Shield (14), to a warrior's rush, bash and parries, and a craft of the skilled priest's: the simple one, pressing 1-8 in turn, never hallows the ground. Warrior–priest at 10: 20 of 24 before; 15, 21 and 15 of 24 in three runs since.
- **A priest's own healing at rank I** 70% of a heal's measure (75%): the simple priest, which heals with every other press, won 88% of its duels at 10; 74%, 83% and 74% in three runs since.
- **A broadhead's wound** takes 30% off healing at every rank (45% from rank II was set on the pocked ground): on clean ground a skilled bow won 24 of 24 against a priest at 30 and 50.
- **Crippling Arrow wounds** for as long as it slows (`crippleWound`, from `updateProjectiles` after its snare: the `wound` buff at the archer's measure, 30%): the simple archer shoots plain arrows and had no answer to a healer (archer–priest 0, 8 and 5.5 of 24 at 10, 30 and 50 before; 1 and 1.5 at 10 and 50 in a run of these rules without the wound); 4.5, 17 and 12 of 24 now. A skilled archer's broadheads keep the wound open anyway.
- **Whirlwind heals in the press** (`whirlHeal`, from the spin's cuts in `executeAbility`): 2% of health for each foe each of its two cuts strikes, up to five foes (`WHIRL_HEAL`): a warrior thrives where the foes are thickest, its answer to the pack at 30 and 50 (a duel's single foe gives 4% a spin).
- **The archer's realm ways** (`ARCHER_REALM`): every way beat the plain archer in 75-96% of their duels in version 12 (a mirror is a race, and a quicker draw, an opening shot from hiding at 80% more and arrows the wind never moves each decided it): Logres draws 15% sooner (25%), Ériu's shot from hiding is 65% harder (80%), Thule's arrows drift half as much in the wind (not at all), its snares hold 4 s (4.5) and it strikes chilled foes 10% harder (15%). **The priests' ways** (`PRIEST_REALM`): the Shield of Logres 6% less harm from the blessing and 6% on holy ground (8% and 8%: it won 9, 6 and 10 of 12 against the plain priest), the Well of Ériu's blessing heals 2.5% a second (2%: 3, 5 and 7 of 12), the Hearth of Thule's blessing 17% harder blows (15%: 5, 6 and 7 of 12). Their texts in `CLASS_REALM` come from the numbers.
- **The skilled bots**:
  - The warrior walks round a snare on its rush's way behind its raised shield (it walked round it under every arrow): warrior–archer at 10, 6 of 24 before; 10, 13 and 13 of 24 in three runs since.
  - The priest presses a bow: to its mace before Tumble's level (as before), within its flare's 8 m from then. From casting range it lost every duel against a bow at 30 and 50 on clean ground, and pressed to its mace at every level it won 23 of 24 at 50: archer–priest 11 and 5 of 24 at 30 and 50 now.
  - The priest renews against a ready flare and flares only what is worth it (above).
  - A mage turns to a storm focus against another mage soaked by its steam (a storm blow breaks three spells in five), not against a priest: against a soaked priest too its bolts broke three of the priest's spells in five and the mage won 23 of 24 against it at 50 (build g). Kept to a mage, the pair still stood at 23.5 of 24 in the next run, while the priest also lost its heals to the first form of the dodge and to the fresh Renewal's rule; 10.5 and 14.5 of 24 once those were put right.
  - Every skilled bot steps out from under a falling meteor (a mage blinks and an archer tumbles, letting its own spell go; on foot only from near the edge, else it finishes its spell), out of a blizzard and out of burning grass, a spell finished first (`botDodge`, from `botStep` before `botMove`). In its first form the bots threw their spells away for a slow harm or a walk they could not finish in time, and the priests lost their heals to it.
- **The measures**: `classes.py b5` (in `mech`), the `rules` line and the crash guard in `duels.py`, a screenshot of holy ground (`classes.py shots only=hallow`).

Tried and set aside (each measured once):
- Divine Shield learned at 9 and Sanctuary at 14 (they changed places): a priest of 10 won 97% of its skilled duels and 99% of its simple ones (a shield, a ward against harm and against every stun, root and slow for 8 s covers most of a duel that lasts 10).
- A warrior rushing before it changes stance at the start of a duel: it beat the bow 18.5 of 24 at 10 (the bow had no moment to lay its snare).
- A fresh Renewal's rule (a Renewal on yourself steadies only when none of your own still mends you, so a priest renewing on every breath is not steadfast all the time): it cost the careful priest more than the careless one at 30 and 50, where a seasoned mage's storm broke 150 of its spells in 48 duels (37 without the rule).
- A mage's storm focus against a priest soaked by steam (above).
- A priest's own healing at 65% of rank I's from rank II (55% was set on pocked ground): once the priest no longer threw its spells away from under a meteor and pressed a bow to its mace, it won 80% of its duels at 50 (build m).
- A priest pressing a bow to its mace at every level (above).

Where it stands against the aim (skilled within about ±15%, for duels a calling's rate between 35% and 65%; simple within about ±25%, 25% to 75%; mastery 6 of 8, or 9 of 12, or more):

- Skilled duels, the two runs of the final rules: at 10 warrior 53% and 58%, archer 52% and 49%, mage 49% and 61%, priest 45% and 31%; at 30 65% and 74%, 51% and 53%, 38% and 32%, 46% and 42%; at 50 58% and 65%, 51% and 49%, 35% and 30%, 56% and 56%. The archer and the priest are within 35-65% at 30 and 50 in both runs (before, on the same ground: the archer 72% and 68%, the priest 31% and 35%); the warrior is over it at 30 (69.5% over the two runs; the warrior against the mage 20 and 22 of 24) and the mage under it at 50 (32.5%) and at its edge at 30 (35%), where its own meteor's bowl shields its foes (above); the priest at 10 fell to 31% in the second run (45% in the first).
- Simple duels, the two runs: at 10 64% and 65%, 35% and 35%, 27% and 20%, 74% and 79%; at 30 71% and 73%, 44% and 49%, 38% and 32%, 46% and 47%; at 50 64% and 68%, 28% and 19%, 50% and 60%, 58% and 53%. Within 25-75% at 30; out of it the simple mage (23.5%) and the simple priest (76.5%) at 10 and the simple archer at 50 (23.5%). Before: the simple priest 88% at 10, the simple archer 24% and 19% at 10 and 50.
- Of the twelve skilled means (a calling at a level) ten are within the band (nine before), of the twelve simple ones nine (nine before): what changed is which miss. Before, the bow ruled at 30 and 50 and the priest fell there; now the warrior's rush at 30 outruns the mage, the mage's own craters hold it down at 30 and 50, and at 10 the simple priest and mage stay at the band's ends.
- Mastery: the warrior 12, 12 and 11 of 12 at 10, 30 and 50, the archer 8, 12 and 11, the mage 8.5, 9 and 11, the priest 8, 8 and 7 (before: the mage 3.5 and 6.5 of 8 at 10 and 30 and 5 at 50, the priest 4, 3.5 and 6 of 8). In the priests' mirror two flares in three met a steadfast spell (31 of 99 broke: the simple priest renews itself on every turn of its rotation, so it stands steadfast most of the time); with the fresh Renewal's rule (set aside, above) the skilled priest won 11, 10 and 11 of 12 (build f, on pocked ground).
- The time to kill: the skilled warrior, archer and priest within 10% of each other at every level, the simple ones within about 25% (the simple archer the quickest at 30 and 50); the mage the quickest at 10 (5.4 s, -18% of the mean) and the slowest at 30 and 50 by far, for its own craters (above). The pack: the warrior outlasts it longer at 30 and 50 with the press's healing (64.5 and 62 s, against 44 and 38 s in one run of version 12); the archer falls first at 30 and 50 (36 and 30.5 s, -38% and -51% of the mean), the mage holds out longest (87 and 94 s) and swings between runs (225 s in version 12's).
- The realm's ways: the archer's still beat the plain archer (62-83%, from 75-96%), Logres's quicker draw most (10 of 12 at both levels); the Shield of Logres no longer leads the priests (6 and 7 of 12, from 9 and 10), and the Well of Ériu is the weakest at 10 (3 of 12, as before).

Still off (for the next pass):

- Pairs lopsided in both runs of the final rules: the warrior against the mage at 30 (20 and 22 of 24), the archer against the mage at 50 (20.5 and 18) and the priest against the archer at 50 (the archer 5 and 7); the simple warrior beats the simple archer 19-24 of 24 at every level, the simple archer and the simple mage win 2.5 to 5.5 of 24 against the simple priest at 10, and the simple priest beats the simple warrior at 30 and 50 (it wins 17-20 of 24). Pairs move 3 to 6 wins between runs (the warrior against the mage at 10: 10.5 and 6 of 24; the archer against the priest at 10: 12.5 and 16).
- The priest's mastery (8, 8 and 7 of 12): the simple priest is steadfast most of the time and its flares are as good as the skilled one's against a steadfast foe. A rule that weighs on renewing by rote without taking steadiness from a careful priest (the fresh Renewal's rule cost the careful priest its duels against a storm) is the next thing to try.
- The mage at 30 and 50 (38% and 32% at 30, 35% and 30% at 50) and the warrior at 30 (65% and 74%): the warrior's rush at 30 (20 and 22 of 24 against the mage) and the archer at 50 (20.5 and 18 of 24), and the mage's own meteor's bowl shields its foe from its bolts (above). That one is for the walls' swept test or the crater's depth (07g_walls.js, 05h_nature.js: a bolt could pass a rim it would clear on a lobbed path, or a meteor's bowl be shallower), and the mage's numbers at 30 and 50 should be read again once it is put right, before the mage or the warrior is tuned on them. (Put right in fitting version 13 together: the shots go over the rim.)
- At 10 the simple priest (74% and 79%) and the simple mage (27% and 20%) stay at the band's ends (the simple mage casts into the simple priest's heals and loses 2.5 to 5.5 of 24), and the skilled priest fell to 31% in the second run (45% in the first: the warrior against it 15 and 19 of 24, the archer 12.5 and 16). The simple archer at 50 (28% and 19%) is caught by the simple warrior's rush every time (24 of 24) and loses to the simple mage (8 and 4.5 of 24).
- The pack: the archer falls first at 30 and 50 (-38% and -51%), and the mage's time swings between runs; two runs are too few for a measure this bimodal.
- The archer's realm ways: a mirror is still a race that a quicker draw (Logres, 10 of 12) or an opening shot from hiding wins; the Well of Ériu's blessing at 10 (3 of 12) heals too slowly to matter in a priest's mirror of 12-15 s. Twelve duels a realm are few (a cell moved 4 wins between runs in version 12).
- Neighbouring arenas: a meteor from the next arena struck a duel in 5 to 8 pairings a run (`FOREIGN` in `tap`); the arenas would want to stand farther apart.
- The noise: two runs at each level of the final build moved a calling by up to 14 points (the skilled priest at 10: 45% and 31%; the skilled mage at 10: 49% and 61%) and a pair by up to 6 wins; the machine was shared all day (load 10 to 28 on 2 CPUs). The integrator's quiet-machine table: the commands above, a level at a time, twice or more.

**The merged build measured** (fitting version 13 together, with the shots over a crater's rim; a quiet machine, one run of each: `duels.py` 24 duels a pair a level, `kind=mastery` 8 a calling, `classes.py balance together noduel mreps=0 reps=3 levels=30,50`). A calling's share of its 72 duels, warrior / archer / mage / priest, and the skilled bot's mastery duels won of 8:

Pairs (the first calling's wins of 24), skilled: at 10 warrior–archer 17, warrior–mage 8, warrior–priest 21, archer–mage 15, archer–priest 15.5, mage–priest 9; at 30 15, 17, 16, 18.5, 8.5, 12; at 50 16, 18, 13, 20, 7, 19. Simple: at 10 21, 15, 12, 17, 3, 6.5; at 30 24, 20, 9, 16.5, 16.5, 11.5; at 50 24, 19, 5, 8, 14, 17.5. The time to kill and the pack, skilled (median of three): at 30 warrior 4.4 s and 65 s, archer 4.0 and 80, mage 3.5 and 70, priest 3.7 and 41; at 50 3.4 and 70, 2.6 and 33, 2.2 and 88, 2.9 and 70 (the mage's time to kill is the others' now: 13.8 and 7.7 s before its bolts went over its own meteor's rim). Simple: at 30 5.2 and 25, 4.0 and 17, 3.8 and 24, 4.8 and 30; at 50 4.0 and 26, 2.8 and 17, 2.6 and 25, 4.1 and 38.

Mirrors in a full run and alone: in the full mastery run at 30 the skilled priest lost all eight of its duels to its simple self (each about 23 s); run alone (`kind=mastery only=priest`) it won 7 of 8, and the balance5 branch's own build in a full run 5 of 8. The skilled mage at 10: 1.5 of 8 in the full run, 5 of 8 alone. The skilled bots stepped out of danger 688 times in the full run at 30 (none in the priests' mirror alone): what one arena's fire arrows, burning grass and meteors do to its neighbours, or a bot's own dodging, decides a mirror. Measure a mirror alone, or with the arenas farther apart, before tuning on it.

### Version 12 ([balance4])

**Charge never rushed.** Since version 5 Charge had a reach of 28 m (`range: 28`) while its dash, its blow and its slow sat in the branch of `executeAbility` for `range: 'melee'`: pressed, it spent its power and its cooldown, freed the warrior of slows (from version 9) and of roots for 2 s (version 10) and gave momentum, and it never moved the warrior, struck or slowed. Every table of versions 9 to 11 measured a warrior without a gap closer, and their answers to kiting stood in for it: Charge every 10 s, the moment free of roots and slows after it, pursuit (every arrow, bolt or spell a second off Charge), the shield wall at full pace. With the rush working and nothing else changed, the skilled warrior won 97%, 82% and 84% of its duels at 10, 30 and 50 and the simple warrior 83%, 71% and 60%, so version 12 tunes the callings again around a real rush (`classes.py b4` checks the rush itself).

How it was measured: each level a run of `duels.py` (24 duels a pair, 8 mastery duels a calling) and `classes.py balance together noduel mreps=0 reps=3` for the time to kill and the pack, on a machine shared with three other builders' runs (load 11 to 32 on 2 CPUs; with the page's frame loop stopped a skilled table took 5 to 25 minutes). The noise is version 11's: a calling's 72 duels move by about ±7 points from run to run, a pair's 24 duels by 3 or 4 wins (a pair once moved 9 wins between two runs of the same build). The commands:

```
python3 tools/tests/duels.py levels=10,30,50 reps=24 tap                     # skilled duels
python3 tools/tests/duels.py levels=10,30,50 reps=24 kind=simple tap         # simple duels
python3 tools/tests/duels.py levels=10,30,50 reps=8 kind=mastery tap         # skilled against simple, the same calling
python3 tools/tests/duels.py levels=10,30,50 reps=12 kind=flavour            # each realm's way against the plain calling
HM_BAL=tools/out/bal.json python3 tools/tests/classes.py balance together noduel mreps=0 reps=3 levels=10,30,50   # time to kill, the pack
python3 tools/tests/duels.py levels=10 reps=2 pairs=warrior-archer trace=2   # a timeline: every press, blow and rush
```

| Level | warrior: Logres / Ériu / Thule | archer: Logres / Ériu / Thule | mage: Logres / Ériu / Thule | priest: Logres / Ériu / Thule |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 10 | 7 / 7 / 11 of 12 | 11 / 10.5 / 10 of 12 | 6 / 6.5 / 6 of 12 | 8 / 7 / 6.5 of 12 |
| 30 | 7 / 8 / 7 of 12 | 11.5 / 10 / 11 of 12 | 8 / 6 / 7 of 12 | 10 / 6 / 7 of 12 |
| 50 | 10 / 6 / 7 of 12 | 10 / 9 / 11 of 12 | 8.5 / 6.5 / 6 of 12 | 9 / 5 / 5 of 12 |

After the priests' ways were evened out (the priests only, one run): the Shield of Logres won 9, 6 and 10 of 12 at 10, 30 and 50, the Well of Ériu 3, 5 and 7, the Hearth of Thule 5, 6 and 7.

What changed in version 12 ([balance4]: 07c_balance4.js, the bots in 07c_balance.js, small marked edits to 06_data.js, 07_entities.js, 07b_classes.js and 07c_balance3.js), and why:

- **Charge rushes** (`chargeRush`, `chargeRushStep`, `chargeLands`): along the ground at 18 m/s, shield forward, homing on the foe, for at most 1.9 s; a wall, a tree, deep water, a drop or a climb stops it, and so does a root or a stun on the way (a snare it runs over, a frost nova as it comes in). It arrives on the step that brings it within reach (it first checked before each step, so against a foe walking away it ran at its heels until its 1.9 s ran out, busy and shot at all the while: the trace of a duel showed it). Landing, it strikes (80%), and until Leap Strike is learned (30) its blow slows the foe by 45% for 3 s, as Charge always promised (30% for 2 s was tried first). No rush on a horse, swimming, in a boat, from a fixed perch, at a foe on another floor or, for a guard at its post, at a foe beyond its leash (`rushWhy`; a gate's guards rushed 28 m out at anyone in sight). NPC warriors rush too (the war's warriors press Charge as before).
- **The rush's cost** (with nothing else changed the skilled warrior won 97%, 82% and 84% of its duels: a rush, a bash and a slash in the stun's shadow, and no caster could answer): Charge every 16 s (12 s until Leap Strike, the warrior's only rush there); landing, the warrior's abilities wait 0.3 s (`RUSH_FOOTING`; 1 s and 0.6 s were tried and gave bows and staffs the warrior at 10) and Shield Bash 3 s (`RUSH_BASH`; after a Leap Strike, which shares the rush, 3.8 s). Charge's own slow of 45% for 3 s on landing stays off once Leap Strike is learned (`RUSH.slow`).
- **What stood in for the rush is set aside**: pursuit (`PURSUIT_CD` 0; arrows, bolts and spells brought Charge back a second sooner each) and the 2 s free of roots and slows after a Charge (`CHARGE_FREE` 0). The rush itself still tears the warrior free of every slow and root but a seasoned mage's frost.
- **The shield wall** keeps its full pace (three quarters was tried: the wall fell behind a bow that kites, and the warrior won 1 of 24 against it at 10), but a volley falls over a raised shield (`classHarm`), and the skilled archer looses one at a raised shield.
- **Priest**: Mend weighs the wound (`mendWound`: all of its measure from half health down, 30% of it on a scratch), so a heal cast by habit does little and one cast at need does its full work: the simple priest, which heals with every other press, won 93% of its duels at 10 (78-83% in three runs now); the skilled priest mends at need (from 52-60% of its health while it can cast in peace, 58-70% with power to spare: it mended from 60-82%). Mend at 28 power instead of 22 was tried and taken back (it starved a careful priest at 10 as much as a careless one). A priest in its own Pillar of Dawn mends as its own wounds do (`priestSelf`; it mended in full). The dazzle takes 15% of a foe's weapon blows at rank I to 24% at rank IV (5% to 35%) and a dazzled warrior cannot parry at any rank (from rank II): a rush reaches a priest at every level now, and at rank IV a third of the warrior's blows went wide. Divine Shield on oneself not growing with rank was tried and taken back (the priest fell to 30-38% of its duels at 50).
- **Warrior**: Rallying Cry's heal is its own measure at every rank (`/ rankMult`: a body's own healing does not grow with rank, as a priest's does not).
- **Mage**: a seasoned mage's spells grow 7% for each rank past the first (`mageRankK`): a mage has no armour, heal or rush, and past 30 the warrior's rush, Rallying Cry and Bulwark, the priest's Divine Shield and Mass Renewal and the archer's Deadeye left it about a third of its duels.
- **The priests' realm ways evened out** (`PRIEST_REALM`): the Shield of Logres 8% less harm from the blessing and 8% on holy ground (12% and 10%), the Well of Ériu's blessing heals 2% a second (1.5%), the Hearth of Thule's blessing 15% harder blows (12%). Against a priest of no realm's way Logres won 8, 10 and 9 of 12 at 10, 30 and 50, Ériu 7, 6 and 5, Thule 6.5, 7 and 5.
- **The skilled bots**: the warrior leaps first, then rushes on a way clear of snares (`snareOnLine`: the rush's real way, which stops at reach short of the foe, and a snare still arming counts if it will be armed by the time the rush passes it; the warrior used to strafe off the line of any snare near a bow and never rush), walks round a snare on its way, and chases in fury when slowed (fury shrugs off much of a slow; the wall stood still). The archer lays a snare in a coming rush's path (`rushComing`) and volleys at a raised shield. The mage chills a coming rush (so the nova that meets it freezes it), steps away from a held blade at every level, breaks a long spell that is not steadfast with Chain Lightning, knows Shield Bash waits after a rush (`castFits`), and trusts only a ward holding more than 15% of its health. The priest casts through a blade while the bash is spent, raises Divine Shield against a rush on its way, at rank I does not swing its mace into a warrior's guard unless the light has dazzled it (`holdMace`: a parried mace stunned the priest and broke its spell 17 times in 24 duels at 10, for a mace that struck 4 a second; from rank II the mace strikes hard and the priest dazzles in reach, and holding it cost a sixth of its harm at 30), and presses a bow that cannot tumble (from casting range at 10 the bow won 21.5 of 24).
- **The measures** (`duels.py`): `kind=flavour` (a realm's way against the plain calling, `e.plainCalling`), heals told by what gave them, the rush's ends in a trace (`RUSH.onEvent`), the page's frame loop stopped while measuring.

Where it stands against the aim (skilled within about ±15%, for duels a calling's rate between 35% and 65%; simple within about ±25%, 25% to 75%; mastery 6 of 8 or more):

- Skilled duels: in the last run every calling is between 35% and 65% at every level (10: warrior 57%, archer 53%, mage 54%, priest 35%; 30: 56%, 45%, 44%, 55%; 50: 47%, 53%, 56%, 43%). In the run before it three were outside: the archer at 10 (66%), the warrior at 30 (72%) and the mage at 50 (74%). Over the two runs every calling is within the band, at its edges for the priest at 10 (37%), the warrior at 30 (64%) and the mage at 50 (65%). Before the rush worked (version 11 as merged) the table was about as even, on a warrior without a gap closer; with the rush alone the warrior won 82-97%. Pairs still lopsided in both runs: warrior-priest at 10 (20 of 24), warrior-archer at 10 (4 and 7 of 24), mage-priest at 50 (17 and 19 of 24), archer-priest at 30 (6 and 6.5 of 24).
- Skilled time to kill: within ±20% (the warrior +19% at 30, the mage -16 to -19%).
- Skilled pack: still bimodal for the mage (19 s at 10, 121 s at 30) and the archer at 50 (115 s); the warrior's falls to -32% and -45% at 30 and 50 (no moment free of slows after a Charge, no pursuit, and Rallying Cry's heal at its rank I measure).
- Mastery: the warrior 8 of 8 at every level, the archer 5.5, 8 and 7.5, the mage 2.5, 5 and 5, the priest 3, 5.5 and 6 at 10, 30 and 50.
- Simple bots: the time to kill within ±27%; in duels the simple warrior wins 62-64% (the rush suits pressing in turn), the simple priest 63-81% (93% at 10 before Mend weighed the wound), the simple archer 17-35% (a simple warrior's rush catches it 19-24 times in 24), the simple mage 26-56%.
- NPC warriors in the war rush too (`war.py`, `soak.py`).

Still off (for the next pass):

- The warrior against a bow at 10 (4-7 of 24): the skilled archer lays a snare at its feet the moment a rush is ready, and the warrior walks round it under fire (a trace: it reached the bow 3.4 s into the duel with 70% of its health, and the rest of the fight was even). An answer before Leap Strike that does not make Leap Strike stronger later: a rush told of a snare that bounds over it, or Shield Bash ready sooner after a rush below 30. The priest at 10 (35-39%) loses to the warrior (20 of 24): no Divine Shield until 14, and the rush, the bash and parries cut half its spells.
- The skilled warrior's pack survival at 30 and 50, and its duels at 50 (40-47%): Rallying Cry's heal at its rank I measure was set while the rush alone won 84% at 50; with the rush tuned it may be too steep.
- Mastery of the mage at 10 and 30 and the priest at 10: a mirror of casters or healers is a short race that pressing everything in turn can win.
- The simple priest at 10 (78-83%) and the simple archer at 50 (17%).
- Realm flavours: every realm's way of the archer beats the plain archer by far (75-96% at every level: a quicker draw, hidden shots, wind-free arrows and freezing snares each decide a mirror), Thule's Berserk at 10 and Logres's discipline at 50 once each (11 and 10 of 12); after the evening out the Shield of Logres still leads the priests (9, 6 and 10 of 12, against 3-7). Twelve duels a realm are few: a realm's cell moved 4 wins between runs.
- Noise: the same rules moved a calling by up to 18 points between runs (the mage at 50: 74% and 56%) and a pair by up to 7 wins; harm from a neighbouring arena (`FOREIGN`) reached 2-9 pairings a run, and a browser killed for memory (the machine's 8 GB, no swap, shared by four builders' runs) cost a run. The integrator's quiet-machine table: the commands above, with `levels=` one level at a time.

### Version 11 ([balance3])

How it is measured now: the duels with `tools/tests/duels.py` (24 duels a pair at each level, realms rotated and swapped, as many arenas at once as the land offers, up to 18; a calling's rate is over its 72 duels), mastery with `duels.py kind=mastery` (8 a calling), the time to kill and the pack with `classes.py balance together noduel mreps=0` (three repetitions, both bots in one phase). Every measure now runs under clear skies (rain damps fire and soaks foes for storms, and the weather of a run moved its duels) in arenas wiped of what the last fighters left (armed snares, runes, holy ground, steam and ice struck the next fighters there). The commands (each level can run alone with `levels=`):

```
python3 tools/tests/duels.py levels=10,30,50 reps=24 tap                     # skilled duels, with what happened in each pairing
python3 tools/tests/duels.py levels=10,30,50 reps=24 kind=simple             # simple duels
python3 tools/tests/duels.py levels=10,30,50 reps=8 kind=mastery             # skilled against simple, the same calling
HM_BAL=tools/out/bal.json python3 tools/tests/classes.py balance together noduel mreps=0 reps=3 levels=10,30,50   # time to kill, the pack
python3 tools/tests/classes.py balance levels=30,50 reps=5 dreps=12 mreps=8  # the whole table the old way (slow: an hour and more a level)
```

A word on the noise: a pair's 24 duels move by 3 or 4 wins from one run to the next (12 duels by 2 or 3), and by more when other work loads the machine; which arenas a pair is given and what else fights in the same phase move it too (a pair run alone and in the full table once differed by half its duels). A calling's 72 duels move by about ±7 points. Read a number over two runs before tuning on it.

What changed in version 11 ([balance3], 07c_balance3.js and the bots in 07c_balance.js), and why:

- Warrior, the answer to kiting: every arrow, bolt or spell that strikes a warrior takes a second off Charge (pursuit; Charge stays at 10 s), and in the guarded stance a raised shield costs no pace (a shield wall: the skilled bot and NPC warriors hold it against a bow out of reach while no rush is ready). At 10 the skilled warrior won one duel in twelve against the archer, in reach a third of the fight; a bow that keeps away now brings the warrior's rush back sooner, and its arrows (most of them turned) no longer win the race while the warrior closes. Leap Strike is untouched by it. The skilled warrior now charges from 5 m (Charge's own reach): an archer kiting at 5.5 to 6.5 m was never caught.
- Warrior, past 30: Charge and Leap Strike are one rush (using either holds the other back 10 s). Two rushes back to back undid every escape a mage had (the skilled warrior won 11 and 12 of 12 against the mage at 30 and 50).
- Mage, frost holds: no Leap Strike out of a frost root (a frost nova's or a frost rune's), and from rank II (level 30) no Charge either; no Charge or Leap Strike out of a blizzard. A Charge tore free of the nova's root at once and a Leap Strike followed; at 10 a Charge still breaks a frost root (the warrior's only rush there). The skilled mage starts a long spell only when it can finish before a warrior who has learned Leap Strike can stun it (Shield Bash once in reach, the leap's landing; a ward that holds takes one stun), steps out of a whirlwind's reach and off a held blade about to break free, and wards in frost against a blade (every blow on the ward chills the striker): it used to cast a Meteor into a rooted warrior about to break free and fall to the bash and the finisher.
- Archer: bodkins and Deadeye go through half the armour, not all of it. In full gear at 50 armour stops two fifths of a blow (an epic set's 39% and the calling's own), so a needle point that ignored it all struck 1.6 to 1.9 times as hard as anything else, and the skilled archer killed a mage in four to eight seconds. A broadhead's wound takes 30% off healing (45% from an archer of rank II; it was 40%): the skilled archer now keeps it open on a priest (it used to loose blunt heads at every spell, even a steadfast priest's that a blunt head cannot break, and lost 9 to 10 of 12 to the priest), and at rank I the 40% decided every duel. The skilled archer looses a blunt head only at a spell it can still break, broadheads at a priest and bodkins at the armoured.
- Priest: holy light dazzles. Smite and the Pillar of Dawn leave the foe missing some of its weapon blows for 3 s (5% at rank I, 17% at II, 26% at III, 35% at IV), and a warrior dazzled by a priest of rank II or more cannot parry. The priest's own healing falls with rank while a blade's harm grows with it, and the skilled warrior won 9 to 11 of 12 against the priest at 30 and 50. A priest's own healing is three quarters of a heal's measure at rank I and a steady 55% of rank I's from rank II (it was 75% over the square of the rank multiplier: 60% at 30, 41% at 50): at 30 the priest outlasted every bow and staff (9 and 10 of 12) and at 50 it did not; now the step comes with rank II and holds. The skilled priest presses an archer who has not learned Tumble, and casts through any blow but a warrior's (it waited on every blow of a mace, and its simple self, casting through them, won half their mirror duels).
- The measures: clear skies, wiped arenas, `duels.py`, `together`.

Where it stands against the aim (skilled within about ±15%, simple within about ±25%; for duels, a calling's rate between 35% and 65%):

- Skilled duels: every calling between 35% and 65% at 30 (46-61%) and at 50 but the archer (67%; 50% in the run before the last change to the priest's bot, which does not touch it: read it as about 58%), and at 10 but the warrior (33%; 35% and 38-41% in the runs before). The two pairs named for this work are even at 30 and 50 (warrior-priest 15 and 13 of 24, archer-priest 11.5 and 15 of 24, from 9-11 and 3-7 of 12), and the warrior meets a bow evenly at 10 and 30 (9 and 13 of 24, from 1 and 4 of 12).
- Skilled time to kill: within ±15% everywhere but the mage at 10 (-19%, as in version 10; the warrior at 10 +15%). The warrior's late time to kill is in (+4% at 30, +10% at 50, from +15% and +20%): bodkins and Deadeye through half the armour slowed the archer's kill of a mailed foe, and the mage's caution against a warrior's stun no longer counts a training dummy.
- Skilled pack survival: within ±15% at 30 but the mage (+20%); at 10 and 50 the mage swings (-25% and +38% here, +46% and +21% in the run before: a mage either falls at once or kites the pack for a minute and more) and the archer at 50 falls early (-23%). Three runs are too few for a measure this bimodal.
- Mastery: the skilled warrior, archer and mage beat their simple selves (6.5 to 8 of 8) at 30 and 50, and the warrior and archer at 10; the mage at 10 and the priest at 10 and 30 only about half the time (a priest's mirror is a long race of two healers; renewing from the first blow and casting through a mace moved the priest to 5-7 of 8 in one run and 3-6 in others).
- Simple bots: the time to kill within ±25% but the simple warrior at 30 (+28%). Duels and the pack are not: the simple priest wins 69-93% of its duels (it heals with every other press; 64-86% before), the simple warrior 19-30% (it never raises its shield, so the dazzle and the parry it does not use leave it nothing against a priest), and the simple archer's pack time swings between runs (14-48 s in one, 78-138 s in the next).

Still off (for the next pass):

- The cycle at 30 and 50: the archer still beats the warrior at 50 (16.5 of 24) and the mage at 50 (17 of 24), the warrior beats the mage at 30 (16 of 24), the priest beats the mage at 30 (18 of 24). Each moves 3 or 4 wins between runs; re-measure before tuning.
- The warrior at 10 (33-41% over five runs): it has one rush and no sustain before Rallying Cry (11) and Bulwark (15); a mage kiting with frost lances and a nova holds it off (6 of 24).
- The mage at 10: the fastest killer (-19%) and its mastery about half: the skilled mage plays a mirror no better than one pressing everything in turn.
- The priest's mastery at 10 and 30, and the simple priest's dominance and the simple warrior's weakness in duels: a healer that presses everything in turn heals with every other press. A rule that weighs on careless healing more than on careful healing (say, Mend on yourself at full health wasted in full) would move the simple priest without touching the skilled one.
- The pack measure wants five runs or more and the mage's and the archer's swings looked into.
- Realm flavours were not measured apart (realms rotate in every table).
- `classes.py balance` with its own duels (`dreps=12`) was not run on the final code: its duels are the same fights as `duels.py`'s, 6 a pair a swap at a time. The integrator's quiet-machine table: `python3 tools/tests/duels.py levels=10,30,50 reps=24 tap`, `... kind=simple`, `... reps=8 kind=mastery`, and `classes.py balance together noduel mreps=0 reps=5 levels=10,30,50`.

With the last change, the mastery duels at 30 and 50 again (eight per calling): 30: warrior 8/8, archer 8/8, mage 4.5/8, priest 4/8; 50: warrior 8/8, archer 8/8, mage 8/8, priest 7/8.

What changed in version 10 ([balance2]), and why:

- Gear (06e_gearcurve.js): fair gear at the level (item level = level, to 50), each piece with the stats its slot, grade and level give on average, so every calling measured wears the same totals (version 9's fixed seeds gave the warrior and archer at 50 a third more damage from their rolls than the mage and priest). Foes past level 24 meet a body in gear with the toughness the gear of their level adds (`gearLateHarm`), so the late game is as hard as it was with gear capped at 24 (`loot.py`: within ±7% per blow).
- Power: past level 20 a body in gear draws its power from the power stat, not from its level (`gearPowerBase`): at 50 a priest's power never fell below nine tenths in a pack, and power meant nothing to anyone; now a long fight drains it at 50 as it does at 20.
- Everyone: a melee blow landing in the middle of a spell sets it back 0.35 s, twice at most for one cast (`castPushback`): a caster pressed by blades or beasts casts slower.
- Warrior: Slash 175% (160%), Cleave 135% (120%); Charge every 10 s (12), and after it no root or slow takes hold for 2 s (`chargeFree`, shown as immune on the class panel); Leap Strike stuns 0.6 s (1 s) every 20 s (18): a leap and a bash held a priest or a mage for most of a duel; Bulwark every 40 s (45), 1.5 s longer for each rank past the first (its share of harm does not grow with rank as heals and wards do). The skilled bot raises the shield against an arrow or a bolt about to land (70% of them, one roll a shot) and lowers it as soon as it has landed (`botIncoming`). A raised shield no longer turns holy light (Smite). Thule's Berserk heals by finishers in fury only, as its text says (a Thule warrior in a guarded stance outlasted every pack).
- Archer: Volley pins what it falls on at 50% for 4 s (40% for 3 s); a sprung snare's cord whips every foe within 5 m for 50% for 3.5 s (4 m, 40%, 2.5 s); Tumble every 9 s (11), and the foes it leaps from are slowed by half for 3 s (`tumbleSlow`); a broadhead's wound mends slowly: healing on it takes 40% less for 6 s (`healWound`), the archer's answer to a healer (the skilled bot looses broadheads at priests).
- Mage: Firebolt 180% (200%); attunement strengthens an element's spells by up to 18% (22%; the Chronicle said 30%), the attuned firebolt's burn 1.25 times (1.5); steam 1.35 times on a chilled foe and 1.7 on a frozen one (1.45, 1.8); Arcane Ward 35% (40%), and a stun that strikes the mage while it holds breaks on it instead, at half the ward's strength (`wardTakesStun`; a freeze still holds): the mage's answer to a leap and a bash. The skilled bot blinks out of a chill about to freeze it, freezes a foe's spell or drawn bow when one more frost would, and keeps a frost focus on a wet foe instead of casting fire into it.
- Priest: Smite 190% (180%), and a raised shield does not turn it; Holy Fire 240% over 8 s (210%). Renewal on yourself loosens roots and makes you steadfast for 4 s: nothing but a stun breaks your spells and blows do not set them back (`priestSteadfast`, `breakCast`). A priest's own wounds mend at 75% divided by the square of the rank multiplier (`priestSelf`: 75% to 29, 60% at 30-39, 49% at 40-49, 41% at 50), so its own healing does not outgrow a pack. The skilled bot keeps a reserve of power with more than one foe on it, duels an archer at range and closes on a staff, renews after a spell is cut and (with power to spare) before it casts with a blade in reach, otherwise casts between the blows, meets a warrior with the Divine Shield before the leap and the bash, and heals sooner in a duel when its power allows (below 20 a priest's power runs short in a long fight: renewing for steadiness at every chance cost it its mirror duels at 10).
- The balance run: the pack's home is the arena (it gave up the chase 42 m from its spawn, so an archer who tumbled to the arena's far side "survived": version 9's archer pack numbers include some of that), every newcomer to the pack is 1% stronger for each second the fight has lasted (an endless pack of one strength left a fighter either about 30 s or the whole 150 s), and the pack never loses its quarry (an Ériu archer hidden out of its sight was forgotten and "survived").

Where it stands against the aim (skilled bots within about ±15%, simple within about ±25%):
  - Skilled time to kill: within ±15% everywhere but the warrior at 50 (+20%; +15% at 30), the slowest killer of a standing foe once the others have their late spells, and the mage at 10 (-18%).
  - Skilled pack survival: within ±20% everywhere but the mage at 50 (+23%); the pack measure is steady now (five runs of a calling usually within a fifth of each other), though a mage at 10 still either falls at once or kites the pack for a minute and more (the last run's +44%).
  - Skilled duels: 36-61% for every calling at every level but two: the warrior wins only a quarter at 10 and 69% at 30.
  - Mastery: the skilled warrior and archer win 6.5 to 8 of 8 against their simple selves at every level. With the last change the mage wins 7, 4.5 and 8 of 8 at 10, 30 and 50, and the priest 6, 4 and 7 (before it: 1, 6.5 and 3): both beat their simple selves, but not yet clearly at 30, where a mirror duel between two healers or two casters is a short race that pressing everything in turn can win.
  - Simple bots: time to kill within ±25%; pack survival and duels are not (the simple priest heals with every other press: it wins 61-88% of simple duels and outlasts the pack by 37-47% at 10 and 50; a simple archer kites a pack for a long time at 10 and 30, +44% to +64%; the simple mage falls first at 10, -29% to -47%; the simple warrior wins a quarter to a third of its duels at 10 and 50).

Still off after version 10 (version 11 took these up, above):
  - Level 10 duels: the skilled warrior wins about a quarter (one or two of twelve against an archer or a mage). Below Leap Strike (30) a warrior has only Charge (every 10 s now, and 2 s free of roots and slows after it) to reach a foe who kites, freezes and snares. Next: an answer to kiting below 30 that does not also make Leap Strike stronger later (a second Charge below 30, or a raised shield that turns arrows while it charges).
  - Hard counters at 30 and 50: the skilled warrior beats the priest nearly every time (10-12 of 12: parries on the mace, stuns through the casts) and the priest beats the archer most of the time (9-10 of 12: its own healing and Divine Shield outlast the arrows, even with broadheads' wounds and at range). The overall rates sit inside the band, but these two pairs are lopsided.
  - The warrior's time to kill at 30 and 50 (+15-20%): its late abilities (Whirlwind, Leap Strike) are for crowds and gaps, not a standing foe.
  - The table at 30 and 50 should be measured again with the last change (only its mastery duels were; the machine's memory cut the full runs short): `classes.py balance levels=30,50 reps=5 dreps=12 mreps=8`. The mage's and the priest's mastery at 30 (about half) wants a closer look at their mirror duels.
  - Realm flavours in the measures: a Thule warrior's Berserk heals by finishers only in fury now (in a guarded stance it outlasted every pack); an Ériu archer who hides loses a pack in play (the run's pack never loses its quarry, so the measure does not show it).
  - Runs of this size still move duel rates by about ±8 points and mastery by one or two of eight; tune on a number only after `dreps=12`.

What changed in version 9, and why:

- Warrior: health 1.4 to 1.3 (parries and the shield now carry a skilled warrior); a lowered shield turns about one frontal blow in eight (for 55% less), so a warrior played simply is not helpless; Shield Bash stuns 1.6 s (2.5), Leap Strike 1 s (1.5); a parry's stun on an ordinary foe 0.9 s (1.1); Charge every 12 s (16) and it breaks free of slows and snares.
- Archer: health 1.25 to 1.12 (an archer lives by keeping away); 5% quicker on foot (not while drawing); Volley 95% a wave (80%) and pins what it falls on (40% slow, 3 s); snares hold 3.5 s (3; Thule 4.5), re-arm in 9 s (12), and the cord whips any foe beside the prey (40% slow, 2.5 s); Crippling Arrow every 9 s (12); Tumble every 11 s (15).
- Mage: health 0.86 to 1.1; Arcane Ward 40% (30%) every 24 s (30); Blink every 11 s (14); Nova every 12 s (16), and a frost nova roots as long as a fire nova; a firebolt's burn 25% (32%).
- Priest: health 1.05 to 1.1, damage 1.2 to 1.3; Smite 180% (160%), Holy Fire 210% (180%); a priest's own wounds mend at 75% of what others get from Mend, Renewal, Sanctuary and Mass Renewal (`PRIEST_SELF`; Mend on oneself was 85%, the rest 100%), and healing costs more (Mend 22, Renewal 16, Sanctuary 32, Mass Renewal 36), so a long fight drains a healer; Divine Shield also lifts and wards off stuns, roots and slows while it lasts.
- Everyone: diminishing returns on stuns (a freeze counts), roots and slows for players and champions; a parry's stun under the same rule for anyone.

Still off after version 10: a galloping horse slides about 8 cm a stride, ridden or not; a body shoved sideways (sliding along a wall or a rock) skates its feet sideways, since the gait runs along its facing, not along the shove (the player's runs are measured over their even stretch: `steady`); a bear's trot at 3 m/s now and then 28 cm (mean 6); a sharp turn at a sprint still slides the planted foot. Over all 62 rows of the run the worst stances add up to 257 cm before and 214 after, the mean stances to 144 and 100. The lowest hoof at a gallop: 5th percentile -2.4 cm and lowest -7.9 before, +0.3 and +0.3 after (the vault: the feet reach the ground for the whole stance; a gallop's stance share 0.09 before, 0.17 after). Up a keep's stair (6.2 m of rise; `motion_tread.js` works the treads out from the flights, so both builds are measured alike), where a foot stands still, its height over the tread under it: version 10 median -13.2 cm, 10th percentile -24.4, 94% of them more than 3 cm off (the feet followed the flight's ramp, which joins the treads' back edges, and sank into the stone); version 11 median +0.1 cm, 10th percentile -1.6, 15% more than 3 cm off (the 90th percentile of the distance 6.8 cm: heels out over an edge).

Still off: at a walk or trot a few beasts' worst stance is worse than before (a deer at 1.5 m/s, a sheep at 2.6, cattle at 2.2, a wolf at 3.2: 4 to 7 cm against 1 to 4; their mean stances 0.7 to 3 cm), a single lift-off or touchdown frame in which a foot within 4 cm of the ground still moves a few centimetres: a trot's lift is low and its swing short, and the retraction holds the foot still only at the very ends of the swing (version 10's feet came off the ground at the ends of a stride: shorter stances, 0.25 to 0.46 of the time against 0.41 to 0.58 now). A cut at a run changes from run to run with where the stride is as the body swings round (version 10: 0.2 and 5.6 cm in two runs; version 11: 2.8, 4.4 and 16.6 in three): a planted foot that could only stay put by crossing under the body (the legs cross by 12 cm at most) is let go. A horse shoved sideways at a trot still slides 15 cm (its feet do not step across under it). The worst stance of a run moves by 2 to 4 cm between runs (which stance lands where): compare means and several speeds, not one number.
