| sheep | 0.4 / 1.1 / 2.6 / 5.0 | 0.9 / 1.9 / 4.6 / 4.6 | 0.0 / 0.0 / 0.1, 0.2 / 0.2 |
| cattle | 0.4 / 1.0 / 2.2 / 4.5 | 1.4 / 4.6 / 6.4 / 5.0 | 0.8, 0.9 / 0.1 / 0.0 / 0.1, 0.3 |
| pig | 0.4 / 1.0 / 2.4 / 4.5 | 0.9 / 2.5 / 4.6 / 9.2 | 0.0 / 0.0 / 0.1 / 0.2, 0.1 |
| hound | 0.6 / 1.5 / 3.6 / 7.5 | 1.4 / 3.3 / 1.4 / 0.5 | 0.0 / 0.0 / 0.1, 0.2 / 0.1, 0.2 |
| hen (unchanged) | 0.15 / 0.4 / 0.9 / 2.0 | 0.2 / 1.0 / 1.2 / 1.7 | 0.3 / 0.9 / 1.8, 1.2 / 1.9 |
| pheasant (unchanged) | 0.3 / 0.9 / 2.2 | 0.6 / 0.8 / 3.0 | 0.6, 0.4 / 1.5, 3.0 / 3.2, 3.5 |
| mallard (unchanged) | 0.3 / 0.9 / 1.6 | 0.3 / 0.2 / 1.5 | 0.3 / 0.3 / 1.5 |

Over all the 65 rows of a run the worst stances add up to 205 cm before and 21.9 and 22.7 after (the birds' 10 rows, whose legs are version 11's, are 12.3 and 13.3 of that, 10.5 in version 11's run), the mean stances to 102 and 9.3 and 9.2. Every beast's row is 0.3 cm or less but the cattle's crawl (0.9) and the shoved horse (1.6), where version 11's were 1 to 9 cm at a walk or a trot (a foot that left the ground already moving forward, or landed still moving, skated 2 to 5 cm over it near the ground); every person's 0.3 or less but the slowest walk (0.8, from 1.9). The birds' rows move from run to run by up to 1.5 cm on the same code (the pheasant at 0.9 m/s 1.5 and 3.0): measure them several times before reading anything into one. `gaits.py` runs every shove and cut three times (two runs of it on the final build): the walker shoved 0.2 to 0.7 cm, the standing one 0.1 to 0.2, the horse 1.4 to 1.9, the wolf 0.4 to 0.6, the cuts 0.0 to 0.3 but for two of the eighteen, 1.4 (46 degrees at a sprint) and 7.4 (92 degrees at a sprint). Up a keep's stair at a run the lowest foot of the whole climb, swinging or standing, is 0.0 and 0.1 cm over the tread under it (version 11: 5th percentile -19.6 cm, lowest -42.5: a foot set on the tread below, inside the riser); at a walk `gaits.py` finds the lowest -1.5 and 0.1 cm in its two runs, 1% -0.2 and 0.1. The `treads` line's share of standing feet more than 3 cm off their tread (0.32 and 0.40, from 0.37 in version 11 and 0.05 earlier in version 12) now counts the feet rising and coming down in place over the tread (they stand still over the ground while they rise): its lowest values (10th percentile 0.1 cm) are what show no foot is in a tread. A horse's footfalls (`gaits.py`): the walk hind left, fore left, hind right, fore right a quarter of a cycle apart (it was the diagonal sequence), the trot's diagonal pairs together (now and then a frame apart as a trot begins), the gallop hind right, hind left, fore right, fore left, the fore right down the frame the hind left lifts, and one leap a stride after the fore feet (version 11 had a second, with the legs flung fore and aft, between the hind feet and the fore). The lock for 400 soldiers near the camera: 337 or 338 rows a frame (version 11 stopped at 256) for 0.85 to 3.2 ms of JS a step here, as loaded as the machine was. `perf.py low views=town,battle js`, two runs of each build on a machine shared with three other builders: a step in the town 13.9 and 20.9 ms in version 11, 21.9 and 18.0 in version 12; in the battle 13.3 and 21.8, then 8.6 and 11.9 (no body farther than 86 m from the camera is followed now): within the machine's noise, the lock's own share too small to show.

Still off (version 12; [motion4] the cut, the paws and hooves at rest and the kneeling front foot taken up in version 14, below): one sharp cut at a sprint in seven to ten still slides a planted foot 2 to 11 cm (in runs of dozens; `gaits.py`'s last run 7.4 cm once in three 92-degree cuts), when a foot comes down or leaves the ground in the first frames of the turn: the player turns 0.2 rad a frame, faster than any leg, and the place it was coming down to swings far aside; hens, pheasants and mallards walk as in version 11 (their own legs, 05j_farmbeasts.js and 09v_birds.js: their shank's fold carries the foot back as it lifts, and the swing's own retraction made them worse), 1.5 to 3.5 cm at a run; a wolf's or a hound's paws stand 1.6 cm over the ground at rest and a deer's hooves 0.9, the cattle's 1.7 to 2 cm into it, the boar's and the pig's 0.7 to 1 (their feet's geometry against the rest point the legs reach for; unchanged); the gallop's leap is long, about half the stride at 11 m/s, since the legs reach only 0.46 of their length in the stance; a kneeling person's front foot stands 3.7 cm over the floor (as in version 11); a walker's toes point down less than they did as the foot leaves the ground (no faster than the foot rises), which reads a little flat-footed at a slow walk.

Version 14 ([motion4]: the sprint cut, a halt, and every action since version 10 measured as a body). Before: version 13 as merged (main), measured on this machine the same evening; after: this build. The cuts: the player sprinting (10 m/s) or running (6.9) and cutting by 46, 92 or 135 degrees, the cut started at every frame of a whole stride (26 runs a case: a stride is 26 frames at a sprint), the worst stance of each run (`gaits.py` cuts; main's sweep with the same measure, `motionMeasure` over the cut's second):

| The player cutting | Version 13: worst stance of 26 cuts (the cuts over 2 cm) | Version 14 |
|---|---|---|
| 46 degrees at a sprint | 14.8 cm (8.2, 10.2, 11.7, 14.8; mean 1.9) | 0.3 cm (mean 0.1; two runs) |
| 92 degrees at a sprint | 24.4 cm (5.0, 12.8, 24.4; mean 1.8) | 0.3 cm (mean 0.1; two runs) |
| 135 degrees at a sprint | (not measured) | 0.3 and 0.6 cm (mean 0.1) |
| 46 degrees at a run | 0.3 cm | 0.3 and 0.5 cm |
| 92 degrees at a run | (not measured) | 0.3 cm |

What slid: a foot that came down in the first frames of the turn landed where the turning body could no longer reach it (its landing place worked out afresh each step swung round with the body, 0.2 rad a frame, and the shaders drew it where the hip's swing ended), or a planted foot passed out of the leg's reach between two frames (the body 16 cm aside and turned 0.2 rad): the quick step that should lift it started a frame late, at 4 cm, from where the leg could still draw it, so the foot was carried over the ground first; and a foot that touched down as the body turned was turned about its ankle with it (1.3 cm).

Every action since version 10 (`actions.py`; the climb with the hands measured as well as the feet):

| Action | Version 13 | Version 14 |
|---|---|---|
| Climbing a corner tower's ladder (action 14) | no foot or hand ever held still: a sine of the body's height posed the limbs, the lower foot stood 0.3 m behind the rungs in the air | every foot stands on a rung while it holds (12 holds in 3.2 s, 10 to 14 frames each, the sole 2.9 cm over the rung's middle: on its top; the ball of the foot over it), every hand round one (12 holds, within 0.2 cm of the rung's middle) |
| Striking from the rungs (18) | as climbing | the feet on their rungs through every blow (12 holds), the left hand round its rung |
| The Charge's landing (a foot near the ground moving over it) | 4.2 cm | 1.7 cm (the stride's worst stance at 18 m/s 0.0) |
| Halting from a run / a sprint, six halts across the stride | 12.5 / 7.5 cm (one halt in six from a run, two in six from a sprint: the foot in the air was carried in over the ground as the stride shrank) | 1.5 to 1.8 / 0.0 to 1.8 cm over three runs |
| Shoving a boat off (19), a foot near the ground moving over it | 6.5 cm (the feet glided into the stance over the shingle, and the drive carried them with it) | 0.3 cm (the feet step in, lifted, and stand on the shingle's slope) |
| At the oars (16): the hand farther from its oar's grip | median 23 cm, the most 51 cm | median 1.7 to 2.3 cm, 90% 3.2 to 3.5, the most 3.7 (two runs) |
| Kneeling (12): the front foot over the floor | 3.8 cm | 0.1 cm |

The paws and hooves at rest (`motion.py`'s lowest foot, 5th percentile, at a walk): a wolf and a hound 1.6 cm over the ground to 0.3, a deer 0.9 to 0.0, the cattle 1.7 to 2 cm into it to 0.0 to 0.4, a boar's and a pig's 0.7 to 1 cm into it to 0.0 to 0.4, a bear's -0.4 to -0.8, a horse's 0.0. Every other row of `motion.py` is where version 12 left it (people 0.0 to 0.8 cm, every beast 0.3 cm or less but a horse shoved sideways at a trot 1.5, the player's cuts 0.0 to 0.3), and up a keep's stair at a run 0.8 cm (version 12: 1.3 and 1.7; 2.2 in this work's run before a quick step went over the floors on its way). `gaits.py`'s stair: the lowest foot over its tread, swinging or standing, 0.0 and -1.2 cm at a walk in two runs, 0.1 at a run.

Cost: nothing is drawn that was not (the climb, the shove, the oars and the blows are shader work on the people's own vertices; the shoulder's swing only where an action sets it); 87 programs, none bad (`shaders.py`). `perf.py low views=town,battle js`: a step 19.3 ms in the town and 9.8 in the battle, main 17.2 and 16.2 the same hour (the battle is wherever the biggest fight is; the machine at load 10 to 14 on 2 CPUs); the foot lock for 400 bodies near the camera (`gaits.py` crowd) 3.8 to 10.7 ms a step over six runs (median 4.5), main's 3.3 to 20.1 over three (median 5.3): within the machine's noise.

Still off: hens, pheasants and mallards walk as in version 11 (1.5 to 3.5 cm at a run, a mallard's webbed feet 2.5 cm into the ground); the gallop's leap is long; a walker's toes point down less as the foot leaves the ground; a stormer's feet on a fallen tower's rubble follow the rubble's floors (its ramps), not the tumbled stones drawn over them; a townsman at a scaffold swings his blow at the air 1.7 m from the frame (now with his feet planted: a blow shifts the weight over feet that stay); the diver's stroke was not measured; a boat shover's hands are held over her gunwale, not on it (`act_shove`).

Still off (version 16): a walker's heel rises about 2 cm before its toes leave the ground (more in life: the ball of the foot is the pivot, and the sole's middle, which motion.py measures, now rolls forward about 1 cm with it); a landing's crouch is the same whatever the height (a fixed 0.3 m sink, no give by the fall); a shover stands where it stepped in while she slides away (its hands come away after her first 0.3 m: nobody walks after her); the builders at a house hammer at one place a wall; a fallen house's heaps carry the feet as a tower's rubble does but were not measured; the gallop's stance is longer but a real horse's is longer still at a canter (the gallop's legs reach 0.58 of their length); hens, pheasants and mallards have no foot lock (the gait alone keeps them planted: a sharp turn would slide them).

## The world looked at (version 12)

[look] Every kind of place and thing seen at once, as a player sees it, with `tools/tests/look.py` (the views and the checks are listed under Testing): at high by day first, then at low, by night, at dusk, in rain and in snow where they change things. What was found, and what was done at its cause:

| Where | Found | Done |
|---|---|---|
| The towns | The houses stood in knee-high meadow right up to their doors with no way to them, and the meadow ended in a wall of tall blades round the plaza | The green between the plaza and the houses kept short (longer in patches), a worn toft round each house and a footpath of bare earth from each door to the plaza (13t_look.js, through the cover map and the road channel: nothing placed or saved moves) |
| The towns | The gardens fenced behind some houses: the meadow grew up through their beds of soil | The beds go into the cover map (`lookCoverAll`) |
| Every house | A thin light line up each side of every door and window to the eaves (a T-junction where a piece over the opening met the tall piece beside it; their vertex shades disagreed too) | Each wall is one slab on a grid cut at its openings, with no faces inside it (`holedWall`) |
| The round houses | The thatch's lining inside wove a plaid of crossing strands, and the cones a crosshatch | The strands laid across the axis the face looks along, the finest faded by the pixel's size (the building shader's thatch) |
| The camps | A bandit camp's fire pit burned against an oak's trunk and its tents stood round trunks; ten trees in all stood in camp furniture | A camp's layout moves up to 4 m off the trees, its tents turn about the fire until clear, a stone, boulder, log or rack in a trunk is left out (05d_fire.js); `look.py`'s check finds none now |
| The mountains | A smooth, sharp-faced pyramid stood on the range over the Logres muster: 149 single 2 m texels left standing above all eight round them by the shaping | Brought down to 0.6 m over their highest neighbour (`HTS.despiked`, 03d_heights.js) |
| The cliffs | Grass blades stood straight out of the rock faces as dark spikes | No blade where the rock mask passes 0.6 (03_terrain.js) |
| Trees near the camera | A crown over a low camera, or between the camera and the player, thinned through a 4 x 4 screen door of dots: dithered green blobs against the sky | Foliage parts by whole leaf cards and by clumps of the crown in the world (04c_trees.js, 04_props.js) |
| People | A priest's or a mage's striding or guarding leg came through the robe from the hip to the boot, and knees through tabards | Cloth over the legs is pushed out ahead of and behind each leg (`humanVS`) |
| After a jump | An orchard's apple trees and the bushes were missing for up to 0.4 s after a journey (the old place's drawn instead), and a town's glass and its fields' soil for 20 frames | Refreshed at once when the camera jumps 20 m (`drawForage`, `lodFar`) |
| After a jump | The grass's trail pressed a straight streak from where a body had been to where it was put (a journey, a release, a portal), glowing at night | A step of more than 4 m starts a fresh trail (13_main.js) |
| Inside a house at night | A body in a room was lit blue by the moon and the night sky (the moonlight through the roof at low, which has no shadow map, and the sky shining in steel at every quality) while the room round it was lit warm by its hearth and lamp | A body under a house's or a tower's roof (`underRoof`, GLSL_ROOFS) takes no sun or moon, less of the sky's ambient, none of the sky in its steel and a little of the room's warm light (`gRoom` in the people's shader, 05a_rig.js) |
| Rain on the courtyards, plazas and roads | The puddles mixed toward the sky at 0.8 whatever the angle: in a storm the ground lay under pale, matte blotches that read as snow | A puddle darkens the ground and mirrors the sky by its Fresnel term (0.22 looking down on it, 0.85 at a grazing angle: 03_terrain.js) |
| The water's edge | Meadow grew a metre and more out into the lakes, ponds and rivers, its tips through the surface: of 17,381 points a metre apart under 5 cm of water or more, 1,252 grew blades (272 under more than 30 cm), from the 2 m grass mask read bilinearly | Every 1 m texel whose middle is under the water is covered (`lookCoverAll`, once the herbs stand); `look.py`'s check finds none |
| The meadow | A bald, pale disc of turf round every bush, wider than the bush (its collider in the cover map) | Once the wild herbs are placed (their places came from the cover map), a bush only shortens the turf under it a little (`lookCoverAll`) |
| A Thule tower's gallery, an Ériu platform | The play camera rose out past the eaves and looked down on the roof: the player and the fight were hidden under it | The camera keeps under a roofed top's eaves while the player stands on it (`lookTowerCamCap`, `updateCamera`) |

Looked at and found right: each town's street by day, dusk and night, in rain (wet ground, the paths muddier), in mist, in autumn and in winter (Glenrua green by day under white peaks, Skarvik in snow), and at medium quality; the houses inside (the hearths' light, the furniture, the windows); each keep's gate, courtyard and wall walk, its corner towers' store rooms and rooms at the walks (ladders, doors, torches), Ulfsborg in snow; the temples and musters; the forest and its edge, a river, a lake from its shore, swimming, the view under a lake (the bed, the light, the surface from below); the mountains from the orchard and the range from a keep; the Barrow's arrival hall; Cinderwing in flight; each realm's four callings in a line, in rain too; the camps' beasts; people at 2 m; low quality (no shadows, the far models) in the town, a keep's courtyard, the forest and the orchard.

### Version 13 ([look2])

What version 12's look left, and a fresh look at what version 12 added and at the places a player meets most (`look2.py`, the `look2` group of `look_views.js`; before: the version 12 build, after: this one, the same views at 640x360):

| Where | Found | Done |
|---|---|---|
| A climber on a tower's ladder | He held the shield up on his raised left arm and the blade out to the side, off the rungs (body action 14 left the arms' weapon pose) | What the hands carried hangs on the back while they are on the rungs: the shield over the cloak, a blade, mace or axe across it from the right shoulder, a staff across it, a bow over the quiver; in a blow from the rungs (18) the weapon is in the right hand again (`humanVS`, 05a_rig.js). Read back from the shader: the blade's tip and the shield's middle behind the body on the ladder, in front of it and on the arm off it (`look2.py`) |
| A hound by the hearth at night | Lit blue by the moon and the night sky in a room the hearth lights warm (the room factor `gRoom` was set only by the people's shader) | Beasts are lit by the room as people are (05c_beasts.js: `underRoof`, no sun or moon through the roof, less of the sky, a warm fill): the hound's pixels from (11, 13, 20) to (24, 18, 17) |
| A crown in the band at the near trees' reach (42 m at high, 28 at medium) | Dotted edges: the near and far sets handed over through complementary 4 x 4 screen doors, a fine checker over the whole crown at medium | By whole cards, clumps and the trunk (`GLSL_TREE_HAND`, 04_props.js): the near tree's cards come in card by card ahead of its share, the far crown goes by clumps of one field in the world behind it (so the crown never thins in the band's middle), the trunk changes at the middle |
| Crowns near the camera at low (no near trees) | Big smooth lobes streaked diagonally by their clumps' noise (a 2D noise of `(x + y, z + y)`) with large holes | Leaf by leaf within 30 m (`treeLeafAt`): each leaf its own tone and tilt, dark between, the outline broken leaf by leaf; the clumps' noise on crossed planes |
| The towns from a rise | The footpaths from the doors to the plaza hardly showed (2 m texels of the road channel: a path 1.5 m wide was one soft texel) | The paths at 1 m (13u_look2.js, `groundPath`), read by the terrain as road with a ragged edge; the road channel keeps only a trodden verge round them |
| A plaza and the roads under snow | The dirt showed through in dark brown blotches (the snow took `A.r * 0.45` off its cover on a road) | Trodden ground under snow is packed grey slush with darker tracks, and the earth that shows wet and grey |
| The Logres orchard in winter | A bare apple tree cast its whole crown's shade (the shadow pass drew every leaf the colour pass drops) | The forage plants' shadow pass drops the fallen leaves as the colour pass does (`LOOK2_FORAGE_DEPTH_FS`): the ground behind the trees 2% brighter in the picture, its shade dappled |
| The roads and courtyards in a small window or on a phone (0.02 to 0.06 m a pixel) | A network of dark angular cracks over all bare earth: the pebbles' and grit's relief bumped through the screen's derivatives, shaded in 2 x 2 blocks, read as crazy paving | That relief fades before it is under about five pixels (`pebF`, `gritF` in the terrain shader); their colour, which takes no derivatives, keeps to a pixel as before |

Looked at and found right: arrows stuck in a Logres house's plaster and timbers and in the Thule keep's gate; the arrows that glanced off a Logres curtain lie in the grass at its foot (hidden by the blades, as arrows in long grass are); a breached Logres corner tower from the field (the rubble mound over the fallen face, the broken drum) and mended behind its scaffolding, the stones stacked at its foot; the burnt Thule log tower from the field (the char, its embers at dusk); a warrior's rush at a mage, shield forward; a horse at a gallop and a wolf at a trot near the camera from abreast; the climber from the room behind him; the roads at low and high and a Logres keep's courtyard at low.

A note on the pictures: walls.py's `walls_charred*` show a twisted column of pale slabs rising out of the burnt shaft into the sky. It is no part of the tower: the camera there stands in the court beside the palisade's top (6 m up, 1 to 3 m from it), and the column is the row of the palisade's sharpened tips seen from under them (a probe of the keep's mesh: the triangles drawn there are the palisade's wood at 6.8 to 7.4 m, the tips' undersides). The `charred` view looks from the field. And a still taken after `__hm.simulate` shows none of the dust of a rush or a stone's strike (born in the frames' future, see Gotchas): the look views drop those particles (`LK.unborn`); look at such dust in play.

### Version 14 ([look3])

What version 13's look left (the [look2] items of its Next row), and a first look at what version 13 added (`look3.py`, the `look3` group of `look_views.js`; before: the version 13 build's pictures, after: this one's, at 640x360):

| Where | Found | Done |
|---|---|---|
| A worn or breached stone tower, a worn curtain | The cracks were the iso-lines of a smooth noise (`c1`, `c2`): thick dark curves winding over the stones like vines, across joints and stones alike, and over a door's timber and a palisade's logs too | Cracks that climb the joints a course at a time (up a head joint, half a stone along the bed joint) or slant through a stone from joint to joint, break into runs and pinch out, with a lit lip on one side of the gap (`look3Crack`, 04d_look3.js), on the dressed stone's upright faces only. A first version that kept to the joints alone hid in the dark mortar lines: nothing showed |
| A house battered by stones | The same vines on its plaster, timber and stone (`houseBatter`); the plaster knocked off in big soft-edged blots that read as stains | Stone cracks as the castles' do; plaster, daub and timber in jagged, nearly straight runs that meet at angles and spread in patches as it is battered (`look3Craze`); the plaster knocked off in smaller patches with broken edges, a pale lip of broken plaster round them and the step down to the laths shaded |
| A tower, a curtain or a burnt palisade gap built back | Poles and two rings of ledgers, the boards on edge to the eye; every other stone of the stack at the foot (and every other log) half a metre up in the air | A putlog scaffold (`look3Scaffold`): standards on the ground, ledgers, putlogs into the wall, a floor of boards at every lift with a rail, braces zigzagging across the face, ladders standing in openings left in the boards at alternate ends; its boards are floors and its ladders climbed (by the player too: walking into one, E or the pad's Use) while it stands; a hoist, new-cut stone on the ground and on the top lift; the logs side by side on the ground |
| ... and nobody on it | The stone rose with no one there | Two masons come out of the keep's hall, walk out by the gate and round the walls; one dresses stone at the foot, the other climbs to the first lift and works at the wall; they go home a little before the work is done, or as soon as a foe comes or one is struck |
| A Logres keep's gate | The walk over the gate had no thickness, so `floorSolidAt` took it as masonry from the ground up: every step into the gateway was refused (found when the masons stood at it) | The walk is a bridge half a metre thick; a body of each keep's realm walks out through its open gate (`look3.py gates`) |
| Every scaffold of the March | Laid out at boot, all 44 (the curtains', the stone towers'), some 13 MB with the new ones that most never show | Laid out the first time each is put up (`look3Lay`), round what stands then. A curtain's scaffold stands on both its faces where there is room (behind a Thule palisade its walk's deck takes the inner face) |
| The rubble of a fallen tower | A heap of round boulders | Mostly the drum's own dressed stones, tumbled, with broken pieces between |
| A crown in the band where the near trees take over (high and medium) | Every crown in the 3.5 m band showed both looks at once, cards over lobes | Each tree hands over within its own fifth of the band (by its tint, the same on both sets), so at any moment most crowns show one look |
| Crowns near the camera at low | One grid of one leaf shape: a regular pattern of beads | Two grids a size apart and turned (the bigger leaves fewer), each leaf its own size, length and taper |
| A hound by the hearth at night | A dark cut-out against the fire | Its coat's outline glows where the fire stands behind it (`look3FurGlow`), and the room gives a little warm light back |
| The keeps' courtyards | No way worn to the halls' doors | A footpath from each hall's door to the inside of the gate (the path map, the cover map, the road channel's verge). The courtyards are trodden earth already: the path shows only where turf reaches it |
| The map on a desk | The guide's "Your path" label lay over the town's and the realm's names and read as neither | On a dark pill of its own (`guideMapMark`, 10e_guide.js) |

Cost (`perf.py low medium high views=keep,forest render settle=16`, against version 13's main measured the same evening on the same machine, loaded by three other builders' runs; triangles / draw calls): low keep 691k / 123 against 702k / 124, forest 737k / 167 against 737k / 167; medium keep 1.32M / 153 against 1.34M / 155, forest 1.54M / 208 against 1.54M / 207; high keep 2.21M / 196 against 2.17M / 195, forest 2.64M / 245 against 2.64M / 244 (the top meshes the same to the thousand: the rest is the war's bodies coming and going); programs 84, 86 and 88. No geometry is drawn while all is whole: a scaffold is laid out when first put up and drawn only while it stands (a tower's about 6,400 vertices); the cracks, the fur's glow, the trees' hand-over and the leaves are shader work where they show; the masons are two bodies a scaffold.

Two checks made steady where they leaned on chance: `walls.py`'s breach keeps its siege going while it reads the rubble (a keep left quiet 20 s by the war begins to mend a breach 2 s on, and the rubble's ramps stand down), and `siege2.py`'s palisade catch counts the stretch's flames over the whole burn (the fire spreads by chance: with eight or ten stretches and the grass round them alight, `addFire`'s cap of 60 timed fires left the stretch none at the moment it was read).

Looked at and found right (version 13's additions): a Thule palisade burning at night and burnt through into a gap (the flames in a still are round glows: the particles emitted while simulating are let go before a picture; in play they rise), built back behind its scaffold and whole again; an Ériu gate tower's thatched cone burning at dusk and fallen in; a town house holed by a stone (the hole, the rain under it) and one brought down to a ruin; a band climbing in over a fallen tower's rubble; holy ground's dazzle motes (small at 640x360) and the priest's light; the guide's gem on the minimap and the map, the field notes and the Aims page on a phone and a desk.

### Version 15 ([look4])

What version 14's look left (the [look3] and [siege3] items of its Next row) and a first look at what version 14 added, as a player meets them (`look4.py`, the `look4` group of `look_views.js`; before: the version 14 build's pictures, after: this one's, at 640x360):

| Where | Found | Done |
|---|---|---|
| A town house brought down by stones | A roofless shell with its walls whole to the eaves, its chimney standing, the burnt ruin's post stubs over the wall tops | Its walls broken off at a ragged line, sloping down from the corners at chest height to knee height where the stones broke them most, ragged at each half-metre step (`look4Stub`, the house shader and its shadow pass), the broken tops closed with the wall's core and a few rough lumps, the steps' ends filled, the masonry heaped in low dusty mounds and broken blocks along each wall (more where it stands lower), the roof's timbers, slates, thatch or shingles and dust over the floor (04j_rubble.js), the posts of a porch before its door down with the wall they stood by; its colliders and the shots' walls cut down with it. A first heap of round stones half a metre across read as boulders, lumps set evenly along the broken tops as battlements, and steps drawn anew every half metre between knee and chest height, every other one high, as battlements again from above |
| ... built back | The old walls with their cracks, the new roof frame on them from the start; poles, a plank a lift and a ladder no one could climb; the townsfolk hammering at the air 1.7 m off | The rubble cleared and the walls rising a course at a time, pale and sound, the roof frame on them once they stand (at once for a burnt house); a putlog scaffold round the house (standards, a lift of boards with its rail and braces, a ladder anyone climbs, by touch too), the doorway clear; the townsfolk at the wall inside it |
| A castle's and a palisade gap's scaffold | Whole from the first stone; the mason above kept to the first lift | Its lifts go up one by one as the work comes up to them (a tower's once its rubble is cleared), and the mason above climbs on to each. A first form, made again as each lift went up, showed its standards alone (the clear test, asked again, refused the places under its own boards): it stands where it was laid out (`LOOK4.prev`) |
| A climber on a scaffold's ladder | The ladder leaned and stood 0.42 m in front of its climber's line, so the hands and feet reached for rungs where a tower's are and held the air; it stopped at the boards, so at the top of the climb the hands held nothing | Upright, its rungs 0.33 m in front of the line and every 0.3 m from where the climb starts, up to a climber's reach (1.95 m) over the boards through an opening left in the next lift's (`look4.py`: every foot on a rung, 2.5-3.8 cm over its middle, every hand round one) |
| A burning plank gate up close, the wrecks | The char's checks and embers in long diagonal streaks (contour lines of a noise of (x + y, z + y)) | Charred timber checked into irregular blocks longer along the grain, each fissure its own depth, the embers in the fissures of the patches still alight (`look4Char`, `siegeMat`). A first try in rows of short blocks read as a brick wall with glowing mortar, a second with every fissure alike as a honeycomb |
| A burning log tower's roof from close above | A net of glowing veins over the whole roof, like lava | The same blocks, the embers only in the hot patches, the char keeping the shingles' and logs' own pattern (the castle shader) |
| A palisade, a tower or a walk built back | The new logs the old colour a fifth brighter: lost in shade | Peeled, new-cut timber: a pale yellow-tan whatever the old colour, streaked along the grain (the castle shader's `tFresh`) |
| A priest's holy ground | The frame blown out to white round the priest: a glow of 1.1 times the ground's width at full strength, and a disc whose middle was 3 to 4 times its light's colour | A soft light low over the ground and a gentler disc; its edge marked over the grass by a ring of slowly turning motes (in a meadow the disc on the ground was hidden, once the glow no longer lit everything) |
| A dazzled warrior | Three motes a few pixels wide at 640x360 | Motes at least 1/25 of the way to the camera, wider round the head, with a faint trail |
| The garrison beating out a fire | Empty fists | A fire beater, a wet leather flap on an ash pole, in the right hand while they beat (`WB.beater`) |
| The map on a desk and a phone | The realm's name over its town's, names over each other and under the guide's gem and its pill, a warband's name over the keep it stood at | Every name laid out clear of the others, the sites' marks, the gem and its pill, the warbands' marks, badges and names, and you (13w_look4.js; at 1280x720 and 390x780 none over another, every town, keep and realm named) |

Looked at and found right (`look4.py` and `look.py serve`, at 640x360 and 960x540): a town house of each realm brought down by stones, from its street at high, medium and low and from above (Logres's stone walls, Ériu's round wall with its thatch heaped inside, Skarvik's logs); Glenrua's roundhouse a little over half way through being built back behind its scaffold; a breached Logres corner tower's scaffold with two of its five lifts up, and a climber on its ladder going up through the opening in the boards, his hands on the rungs, his blade and shield on his back; a burh's plank gate burning at 3 m; a burning Thule tower's shingles from 4 m above; a Thule palisade built back, at dusk; a priest on holy ground in a meadow with a dazzled warrior beside her; a soldier with the fire beater; the map at 1280x720 and 390x780. Of version 14's additions and the places most met: a burh's walk burning at night at low (the flames along the walk lighting the palisade, a post's char checked and glowing); a worn Logres tower at night (the cracks faint, as they should be in the dark); the Thule hallow's ring burnt through (its charred logs fallen in the gap, the dais seen through it); Brackenford's plaza from the portal stones; Ráth Fiacla's courtyard at dusk; a bears' camp in a meadow.

Not looked at (the machine's time went to the fixes and the checks; the views are in the `look4` group): a burh's walk burnt through and built back (`deck_gap`, `deck_fresh`), a plank gate fallen and rebuilt (`gate_fallen`), a holed roof from inside and from above (`roof_in`, `roof_out`), the new cracks in rain and at dusk (`crack_tower`), the firefighters on a burning burh (`beaters`: its framing never found the beater against the flames; `beater_close` shows the beater), and the rest of a fresh pass (the other towns' streets and houses inside, the roads and passes, the people up close: `look.py views=towns,frontier,close`).

Cost: see Phones and performance (version 15): the houses' meshes lighter, nothing new drawn while nothing has fallen.

## History

- **Versions 1–4:**
  - The world and its three realms, towns, keeps and temples.
  - Classes and abilities; the war AI with warbands, sieges and hallows.
  - Horses, wildlife, Cinderwing, weather, and day and night.
  - The phone HUD.
- **Version 5:**
  - Loot in six grades that changes how you look, plus merchants and potions.
  - The Barrow: a dungeon with packs of the dead, a fire gallery, the warden's gate, and the Hollow King with his tomb-raising fight and hoard.
- **Version 6:**
  - Level 50: the frontier, the Barrow and the dragon scale with the player; borderland camps with named champions; eight new abilities with real motion and effects; ability ranks.
  - Lore and onboarding: the prologue, the Chronicle, the path with rewards, field notes and tips.
  - Working castles: hinged gates, stairs, wall walks, parapets, garrison routes, breaches and repairs, banners that stay clear of walls.
  - Working houses: doors, shutters, glazing, furnished rooms, townsfolk's days, houses that burn and are rebuilt.
  - The reactive land: trees that are felled and regrow, cut grass, grass and tree fires, craters.
  - Weather on everything: wet, snow, drying and melting, lightning on tall things. Gear wear: durability, blood, mud, repair and washing.
  - The war: announced orders with reasons, routes on the maps, player command of warbands, campaigns that are won and reset.
- **Version 7:**
  - A visual pass: meadow, canopy and ground-shade maps, height fog, graded composite, better water, sky, trees and beasts.
  - Realm gifts and ultimates; the land saved with the world; loot settling into craters; the woodcutter's axe.
  - Siege engines (rams, mangonels, trebuchets), breaches stormed, plugged and sallied against; campaign tuning.
  - A procedural score, the northern lights and shooting stars.
  - Foot-locked gaits for people and beasts; Cinderwing flies, glides and banks.
  - The 1 m cover map: no grass, flower or log through floors, walls or paving; the temple dais fixed.
  - Burning brands from any fire; fire spells aimed at the tree or grass in front.
- **Version 8:**
  - Real water: a river per realm down its valley, ponds in the hollows, see-through water with ripples; wading and swimming for people and beasts; drinking and waterskins.
  - The callings: warrior stances, momentum, finishers, shield and parry; the archer's draw, arrow goods, hiding and snares; the mage's fire, frost and storm with attunement and combos, ice over water, runes; the priest's devotion, holy ground, blessings and raising the fallen; realm flavours; NPCs use all of it.
  - Survival and crafting: gentle hunger and thirst, foraging, hunting, fishing, cooking, workstations in every town and keep, a crafting book of about 55 recipes, skills that grow.
  - Farming and herb lore: seasons, tilled beds and crops through their stages, weeds and soil, plant breeding with strains, town fields worked by the townsfolk, wild herbs and the herbal.
  - Animals: taming wolves, boars, deer and wild horses; companions with orders; livestock in commons and paddocks; breeding with inherited traits.
  - Keep halls with war tables, armourers and hearths; building from goods: walls, doors, roofs, stairs, fences, beds and chests that burn and are repaired.
- **Version 9:**
  - The seasons across the land: broadleaves turn and stand bare in winter while pines carry snow, blossom and apples in their season, the meadow and its flowers through the year, snow lying by local warmth, Thule's lakes and the high tarns frozen in deep winter (walk and fish on them), drifting leaves, birds, butterflies and fireflies by season, gardens in every town, farmland cleared of trees.
  - Balance and mastery of the callings: simple and skilled bots and the balance run with mastery duels, diminishing returns on stuns, roots and slows, Divine Shield against control, a priest's offer to raise the fallen player, the steam burst, rune glyphs and tracked quarry made to read, three rounds of tuning.
  - Depth: bushes and apple trees burn, fall and grow back; stumps are grubbed out; pheasants and ducks to hunt for meat and feathers; crafting to item level 50 with grave-iron, dragon scales, trophies and nine masterworks; hip roofs, lean-tos, leaded glazing and wells; built doors used by townsfolk and soldiers; hens that lay and hatch; a hound that herds; bands that guard their engines and storm the breach.
  - Phones and performance: levels of detail for terrain, trees and rocks, grass by quality (no longer drawn twice after a quality change), far work at a lower rate, phones starting at low; the HUD laid out on every screen with 40 px touch targets; names not shown through walls.
  - Fitting it together: roofs raised from the floor below, stone steps up to high doorways, walkers that detour round new obstacles, the desk HUD stacking the class panel and the builder's bar, arrows that fly in the Barrow, the beast you face coming first when you offer food.
- **Version 10:**
  - Loot that keeps up: drops, champions' spoils, the Barrow's and Cinderwing's hoards and the merchants follow the level to 50 on a gentler item curve; masterworks stay about 15% above a drop; foes past 24 as tough as the gear of their level; the callings tuned again in fair gear (a priest's own healing falling with rank, the mage's ward against stuns, broadhead wounds, the warrior's Charge free of roots, blows that set a spell back).
  - Motion: every kind of body measured from what its shaders draw and fixed (planted feet at every speed, short steps when slow, stepping round on the spot, leaning into turns and climbs, feet on stairs and slopes, kneeling on the ground); beasts on leg IK; pheasants and mallards on their own skeleton; Cinderwing rears, springs, climbs, slows and lands; the forest within its budget at low.
  - The mountains up close: unreachable faces reshaped, about a thousand crags, bedded rock with streaks and cracks, scree and heather, snow lines by altitude, realm and season. The stone keeps' corner towers are hollow, with doors, rooms, ladders and hatches, and the garrison climbs through them.
  - Water: diving in three dimensions with breath, the bed, finds and the view from under the water; rowing boats at every town's jetty with their own physics, fishing and the towns' fishers; tracks in snow, mud and wet sand, ruts, and drifts against the walls.
  - Fitting it together: townsfolk find their way round houses and fences on a small grid (`localPath`), a farmer's headway measured afresh at the work, companions routed round banks and walls and brought back in beside you (not at an old trail point), wall torches lit from but not cooked over, the land's checks telling the jetties' posts from the world's own; the mountains' rock made to read as bedded stone and the scree as loose stones (the first version looked like masonry and paving); tree chunks shown at once after the camera jumps; nameplates know their body (two may share a name).
- **Version 11:**
  - The mountains from everywhere: the whole mountain shaped once everything is placed (so saves keep every index), the walkable slopes and the skyline too, with outcrops and about 130 hardy pines and birches on the lower slopes.
  - Every tower of every keep you can go into, built from one kit: Logres's gate drums with a walk over the gate, Ériu's gate drums and lookouts under thatch, Thule's log towers with rooms and fighting galleries, which burn, fall in and are rebuilt; fighting on a ladder and at a hatch.
  - Feet that never slide: beasts vault over their planted legs, a foot lock holds planted feet through shoves, cuts and turns, feet stand on each stair tread; boats a body can push and shove off the shore; tracks kept with the world, footprints that read in the snow, no grass through drifts.
  - The callings nearer the aim: a warrior's pursuit and shield wall against kiting and one rush from 30, frost that holds, bodkins through half the armour and deeper wounds, holy light that dazzles, a priest's own healing falling with rank, better skilled bots, and `duels.py` to measure them.
- **Version 12:**
  - [look] The whole world looked at as a player sees it (`look.py`): the towns' trodden greens, tofts and footpaths and their gardens' beds, house walls without light lines at their doors and windows, thatch combed one way, camps laid out clear of the trees, a range without spikes, cliffs without blades, bushes without bald discs, foliage that parts by cards and clumps, robes and tabards that stay over striding legs, the camera under a tower top's roof, bodies lit by the room they stand in, and the world drawn at once after a journey.
  - The callings around a real rush: Charge rushes at last (it never had since version 5), and the callings were tuned again around it: its footing and Shield Bash held back after it, a snare or a frost nova that stops it, volleys over shields, Mend by the wound, a flatter dazzle, a seasoned mage's spells, the priests' realm ways evened out, skilled bots that know the rush, and the realm ways measured against the plain calling.
  - Every gait measured and held: feet rise and come down in place, the walk's four even beats, the trot's diagonal pairs, the gallop's gathered leap; shoves, cuts at a sprint and steep stairs held; the foot lock for every body near the camera.
  - What flies stops at what it hits: one swept test for every arrow, bolt, spell and siege stone against walls, towers, palisades, houses, built pieces, doors and the ground; arrows stick in timber and glance off stone, spells burst on walls, shots go out through crenels, slits, windows and open doors, archers pick foes they can strike; stone towers crack, fall into breaches that can be stormed, and are mended in peace.
  - Fitting it together: a war that never stands still (chases, breaches, engines and bands that get nowhere give up and take new orders), tumbles and blinks along the floor the body stands on (never over a parapet or off a tower), a town's fisher who leaves by the door and has the time the walk takes.
- **Version 13:**
  - The siege uses everything the walls can do: engines take a tower for their mark when it is the weaker way in, bands storm a fallen tower one or two at a time through its rubble, store room and door, the garrison plugs the court; a burh's palisade and Ériu's thatched cones catch, burn, fall into gaps that are stormed and are built again; siege stones and meteors crack houses, hole their roofs and bring them down to rubble the townsfolk rebuild; stuck arrows swing with a door; held archers look for another foe or a place with a line.
  - The callings on clean ground (every table at 30 and 50 since version 9 had been measured in cratered arenas): Holy Fire interrupts a spell within 10 m, holy ground dazzles the foes on it, Crippling Arrow wounds while it slows, Whirlwind heals in the press, the archers' and priests' realm ways evened out, smarter skilled bots.
  - The world looked at again: a climber's weapons slung on his back, beasts lit by the room, the near and far trees handed over by whole cards, leaves on the low-quality crowns, footpaths at 1 m, trodden snow as slush, bare apple trees' thin winter shade, no crazy paving in a small view.
  - Players know what to do: field notes that show on an upright phone, the first-steps note first and none over a fight, a gold gem for the next step on the minimap and the map, a path of 24 steps and the aims past it (the Aims page), field notes and loading tips for every system since version 6, How to play composed from every system with every key and the phone's controls.
  - Fitting it together: homing shots over a crater's rim or a low rise (a mage's meteor no longer shields its mark), a band given a new order leaves the gate it was striking, three flames a burning stretch and a cap of 60 timed fires, tests steadied where they leaned on chance.
- **Version 14:**
  - Fire and siege made whole: a burh's wall walks, its plank gates and the Thule hallow's log ring catch, burn, char, fall in and are built again in peace; garrisons run to a fire on their walls and beat it out with hides (body action 21), and the player can too; nobody stuck in the ruins (a band's places moved off rubble, members that take the leader's footsteps, a storm that gathers inside a tower's door), and the Logres gates passable at last (the walk over them stood solid to the ground since version 11); a stone's hole in a roof cut in the shadow too, with broken rafters; arrows that swing with a built door.
  - The callings measured in arenas far apart, every skilled bot within the band at every level: renewing yourself by rote costs twice, the mage's ward keeps a spell, a stopped Charge comes back sooner, the Well of Ériu heals beside the Renewal, a seasoned mage's spells, the archers' realm ways halved, better skilled bots.
  - The world looked at again: cracks that climb the joints and catch the light, working scaffolds with boards, braces and ladders that a player can climb and masons on them, a fallen tower's rubble of tumbled dressed stone, trees handed over tree by tree, leaves in two grids at low, a hound lit by the hearth, the halls' footpaths.
  - Bodies that move like living things in every action: no planted foot slides at a sharp cut or a halt, blows over still feet, a climber's hands and feet on the rungs, a rower's hands on the grips, the boat's shove, a kneeling foot on the floor, paws and hooves on the ground.
  - Fitting it together: beating out flames over feet that stand, guards left up on a walk going down by the castle's ways, firefighters back to their own floor.
- **Version 15:**
  - A war that keeps moving: every band either gets on with its order or fights (measured every 2 s over whole campaigns: fought, got nearer, waited on purpose or stood), soldiers come down off the walls, out of towers' rooms and out of the ruins, leaders leave a court by its gate and find a way round, engines that cannot work are settled or given up, a second band goes in by an open breach, raids go in by a gap burnt in the Thule hallow's ring; townsfolk run to their houses' fires and beat them out (and the player can help); broken gates kept with the world; campaigns won by keeps or hallows in about one hour in three at low (the rest by renown at the hour).
  - The callings measured both ways round (the side made first acts first: it decided archer mirrors) on open grass that grows back between duels: the archers' realm ways an edge on their own ground (Logres on a wall, Ériu among trees, Thule in the cold), Charge every 12 s and its slow at every level, plain arrows that grow with rank, Mend lighter on a scratch, skilled bots that chase through fire and step out of volleys.
  - The world looked at again: houses brought down by stones lie in rubble and rise again course by course behind real scaffolds that anyone can climb, castle scaffolds rise lift by lift, charred timber cracks into blocks with embers in the cracks, holy ground glows without blowing the frame out, firefighters swing a beater, the world map lays its names out clear of each other.
  - Players know what to do on a phone first: the Chronicle folds to its summaries (45,000 px to 17,000 on an upright phone), field notes have their own page, new notes for the hall, the farmers, scaffolds, timber that burns, stones on houses, the callings' rules and what happened to you in a fight, a marker for steps done anywhere, the war panel's line for your aim, Cinderwing's step by your share of its harm, and no tap lost to a panel moving under a finger.
  - Fitting it together: players told of townsfolk at their houses' fires and of the war's new ways in; stormers who keep going when their leader turns to the court, leaders out of the narrow strip behind a burh's hall and out of a rath's bank, fire crews who find their way round the houses, tests steadied where they leaned on chance.
